# OpenHands → Claude Shepherd — feature-mining report

> Source: `/Users/adam/Programming/check out/OpenHands` (this checkout = the management/server/skills/UI layer; the agent runtime is an external SDK, not vendored).  
> Target: **Claude Shepherd** — local single-user macOS console that WATCHES/STEERS/SPAWNS Claude Code GUI sessions (no executor, no sandbox, no server, no users, no secrets).  
> Method: 12 finders → dedup → adversarial FOR/AGAINST judge (double mismatch: in-process executor AND multi-user web server) → skeptic verify, cross-referencing queued L1–L4.  
> Counts: **163 raw → 91 candidates → 53 ADAPT, 38 DON'T-ADD, 0 pure-ADD** (all 53 ADAPT verified).

**Headline:** third independent source — **L1 (Agent Profiles) reinforced 32×**, L3 ×5, L4 ×5, L2 ×2. The genuinely-new value is a coherent **9-item 'richer watch-console' cluster** (surface plan/TODO, auto-title tiles, error-reason taxonomy, PR status, detail-panel tabs, session export, host stats, post-run summary, hooks inspector) → proposed **L5**, plus an **event-callback rule engine** (named when-event→action automations) that generalizes Shepherd's hard-coded auto-respawn/continue/escalation.

---

## ✅ ADAPT — 53 items, grouped by what they reinforce

### 🆕 GENUINELY NEW — richer watch-console / observability & automation (→ proposed L5)  (9)

#### Export conversation/session as a downloadable archive (events + meta.json)  `[H/S]`
- **For:** A "save/export this session" action genuinely fits a watch/steer console: a human triaging a fleet often wants to archive a finished session, hand it off, or attach it to an issue/PR. The need is real AND most of the work is already done. Claude Code writes the full event-by-event conversation to ~/.claude/projects/<ENCODED>/<session_id>.jsonl, and Shepherd already tracks every tile's transcript_path (cc-core.lua:54-59) and already reads those files for fleet search (claude-dashboard.lua:2033). The "meta.json" half is also free: Shepherd already computes the session metadata OpenHands would serialize — label, projectKey, provider/model, session lineage (lineageByProject), and gate-decision/timing counters (standupMarkdown / shift report). And the side-effect pattern is established: audit-export (claude-dashboard.lua:2120-2131) already writes a file under a known dir and alerts the path, and copy-text already pushes a report to the clipboard. So an "Export session" tile action is a thin assembly of parts Shepherd owns: copy the transcript JSONL (the events) + emit a small meta.json, write/zip to ~/.claude/cc-exports/, alert the path.
- **Against:** The OpenHands implementation's value is mostly the part Shepherd structurally cannot and need not host: paging every event out of a server-side event_service (because OpenHands' events live in a multi-tenant store) and streaming application/zip back to a remote browser client over HTTP. Shepherd has no server, no remote client, and no event store to page — the events are already a flat file on disk, so the entire serialization+streaming layer is the double-mismatch (b) server plumbing and is DONT_ADD. What remains is a small local convenience that overlaps with capabilities Shepherd already ships: the transcript is already grep-able via fleet search, and the ledger already has filter/redact/export/purge. A naive copy could also leak whatever the transcript contains, so it needs the same redaction discipline as the ledger — added cost for a feature that's borderline a "Reveal in Finder on the transcript_path" one-liner. It reinforces no queued L1-L4 item.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: the FOR side is a real management/lifecycle need (archive/handoff a conversation) that maps cleanly onto Shepherd primitives — a conversation ≈ a tile, and the "events" are literally the on-disk transcript JSONL Shepherd already locates and reads. The AGAINST side correctly identifies that the OpenHands code's substance (event_service paging + HTTP zip streaming to remote users) is server/multi-tenant plumbing that is DONT_ADD. The right call is ADAPT, not ADD (don't copy the router/streaming as-is) and not DONT_ADD (the underlying need is real and almost entirely buildable from parts Shepherd already has). Crucially it must be RESHAPED: drop the streaming/download entirely; reuse audit-export's write-file-and-alert side-effect path and the existing meta-data sources (lineage, shift-report counters, label/provider/model). It does not reinforce L1-L4 (those are spawn/policy/template/routing, not archival), so reinforces is empty. Must inherit the ledger's redaction discipline so an export can't leak prompt bodies the operator wouldn't otherwise share. Effort is small because the transcript already exists on disk, the metadata is already computed, and the file-write + zip + alert pattern is already in the codebase.
- **How (Shepherd):** New per-tile action "Export session" (and a fleet-level "export selected"): given a session's transcript_path (cc-core.lua:54-59/878-887), copy ~/.claude/projects/<ENC>/<sid>.jsonl as the events file and emit a meta.json built from data Shepherd already holds — label, projectKey, provider/model, lineageByProject entry, and the standupMarkdown/shift-report counters (toolRequests/approvals/escalations/durations). Reuse the audit-export side-effect path (claude-dashboard.lua:2120-2131): mkdir ~/.claude/cc-exports, write the bundle (optionally zip via hs.execute), then hs.alert the path (mirroring copy-text/audit-export UX), optionally Reveal in Finder. Pure assembly (filename + meta record shape) goes in cc-core.lua (unit-tested, zero hs.*); the file copy/zip/alert is a new FX entry. Gate it behind the same redaction posture as the ledger so prompt bodies aren't leaked unintentionally. Off by default in the sense of being an explicit operator action, no automation.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; the ADAPT ruling holds. I verified every Shepherd primitive the judge cited and none of the double-mismatch triggers fire for the RESHAPED feature. Double-mismatch tests: (a) executor/sandbox — NO. The "events" file is the raw Claude Code transcript that already exists on disk; cc-core.lua:54-60 (projectKey) derives it from data.transcript_path (…/projects/<ENC>/<sid>.jsonl), and Shepherd already locates/reads that file (search annotateSearchHits cc-core.lua:870-893; live-tail at ~1527). Building the archive is a cp + a JSON meta record — no agent loop, no Docker/remote runtime. (b) server/multi-user/secrets — NO once reshaped. The OpenHands substance (event_service paging + HTTP zip STREAMING to a remote browser) is genuinely server/multi-tenant and would be DONT_ADD, but the judge correctly drops the streaming/download and writes a LOCAL file instead. That is local, single-user, no secrets, no network. (c) redundant/queued — NO. Shepherd has ledger-export (its OWN cc-ledger JSONL), shift-report copy, and rg-search, but none produces a full per-session conversation bundle (raw transcript + meta); the routing-topology 'export' (todos.md:422) is explicitly NOT a file export. It does not map to L1-L4 (spawn/policy/template/routing), so reinforces is correctly empty (archival, not lifecycle). (d) plumbing-vs-console — the judge separates the PATTERN (archive/handoff a session) from the server plumbing and reshapes it as an explicit operator action. Mechanism is all present: audit-export's side-effect path (claude-dashboard.lua:2120-2131: hs.fs.mkdir an exports dir, FX.writeFile, hs.alert the path) is the reusable template; FX.writeFile / hs.fs.mkdir / FX.mkdirP / hs.execute (for an optional zip) all exist; metadata sources are already computed — lineageByProject (cc-core.lua:1061), standupMarkdown + shift-report counters incl. toolRequests/escalations/prompts (cc-core.lua:958-1238), plus label/projectKey/provider/model. The pure filename+meta-record assembly fits cc-core (unit-tested, zero hs.*); the copy/zip/alert is one new FX entry. This is correctly ADAPT, not ADD (must NOT copy the OpenHands router/streaming) and not DONT_ADD (no mismatch trigger fires for the local form; the need is real and almost entirely buildable from existing parts). One refinement to the mapping (does NOT flip the verdict): the judge's 'inherit the ledger's redaction discipline' is partly misapplied. Shepherd's redaction (FX.redactLedger, dashboard:586) only rewrites its OWN cc-ledger/*.jsonl; the proposed events file is the RAW Claude Code transcript, which Shepherd never writes and cannot redact via that machinery, so the bundle would contain verbatim prompt/response bodies. But this is an explicit operator action exporting the operator's own local data (no different from revealing the file in Finder) — a design caveat, not a scope breach, and needs no server/secrets/executor. So it stays a local console improvement: ADAPT (high) holds.

#### Read a workspace file from a session (e.g. PLAN.md) to surface the agent's plan  `[H/S]`
- **For:** Surfacing "what is this agent planning to do right now" on a tile is squarely a WATCH-console job and high value: a fleet operator triaging many sessions wants to see each agent's current plan/TODO at a glance before approving, nudging, or stopping. The need is real and human-in-the-loop, which is Shepherd's reason to exist. Shepherd can satisfy it cheaply with primitives it already has: it already reads transcript JSONL every refresh (transcriptSnippet at cc-core.lua:1544) and the transcript already carries the agent's plan as ExitPlanMode and TodoWrite content (verified: 4 ExitPlanMode entries in a live transcript). So "show the agent's plan" is a small extension of an existing, unit-tested pattern — a new pure parser (e.g. core.planFromTranscript) plus a detail-panel/tile section, with an optional direct read of <cwd>/PLAN.md for repos that use that convention. No new infrastructure required; the cwd is on the same local filesystem Shepherd already inspects.
- **Against:** The OpenHands feature's actual substance is the SANDBOX-download mechanism: resolve the running sandbox + agent-server, open AsyncRemoteWorkspace, stream a file out of an isolated container FS to a temp file, decode it. That entire value proposition is the agent-EXECUTOR/sandbox half of the double mismatch — Shepherd has no sandbox and no agent-server, so the load-bearing engineering transfers to zero. What's left ("read a file named PLAN.md") is nearly trivial on a local FS and is partly redundant: the agent's real plan lives in the transcript Shepherd already parses, and PLAN.md is just one project's convention, not a Claude Code primitive (none exists in this repo). One could argue DONT_ADD: the genuinely transferable part is so small it barely qualifies as a feature, and the rest is structurally inapplicable.
- **Ruling:** The NEED — surface the agent's current plan/TODO so the operator can steer with context — is real and fits the watch console. The MECHANISM — sandbox/agent-server file download via AsyncRemoteWorkspace — is the executor/sandbox half of the double mismatch and is DONT_ADD-able on its own. That split is the textbook ADAPT case: keep the need, drop the plumbing, reshape onto Shepherd's primitives. Shepherd should NOT model this as "fetch a remote file from a running session" (there is no remote, no running server to ask); it should read the plan from where it already lives locally — the transcript's ExitPlanMode/TodoWrite entries (the agent's genuine current plan), via the proven transcriptSnippet pattern — and optionally fall back to a direct <cwd>/PLAN.md read for projects that keep one. This is strictly better than copying OpenHands as-is, because the transcript reflects the live plan even when no PLAN.md file exists, and avoids any pretense of a server fetch Shepherd can't perform. It doesn't match L1-L4 (those are agent-profile/policy/template/routing systems), so reinforces is empty; it instead extends the existing status/snippet-surfacing layer.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a pure core.planFromTranscript(text) that scans the transcript tail (same ^%s*{ JSON guard as transcriptSnippet/transcriptError, cc-core.lua:1544) for the latest ExitPlanMode tool_use input and/or the latest TodoWrite todos array, returning a compact plan/TODO struct (empty when absent — mirroring OpenHands' empty-string-on-missing contract). Surface it in the detail panel (and optionally a tile "📋 plan" affordance) next to AskUserQuestion surfacing, loaded on selection/status-change like gateDecisionSummary, never on the 1s tick. Add an optional FX-layer direct read of <cwd>/PLAN.md (or a config-named file) as a fallback for repos using that convention — a plain local file read through the existing fx table, NOT any remote/sandbox path. No network, no server, no secrets touched.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt failed; the ADAPT ruling holds. Every claim verifies against the code. The OpenHands MECHANISM (fetch a named workspace file via AsyncRemoteWorkspace download from a running agent-server) is the executor/sandbox half of the double mismatch and is DONT_ADD-able on its own — Shepherd has no remote workspace, no running server to ask, no Docker runtime. But the judge already discarded that plumbing. What survives is the NEED — surface the agent's current plan/TODO so the operator can steer with context — which squarely fits the watch/steer console and reshapes cleanly onto Shepherd's proven local primitive. Verified facts: (1) transcriptSnippet (cc-core.lua:1544) already scans the transcript tail backwards using the exact `^%s*{` JSON guard the judge cites, decoding assistant message content; transcriptError (1588) and parseUsageLine (1647) reuse the same guard — so a pure core.planFromTranscript(text) that pulls the latest ExitPlanMode tool_use input / TodoWrite todos array is a direct extension of an existing, unit-tested pattern. (2) The transcript tail is ALREADY read every refresh via FX.readTail (claude-dashboard.lua:283), a plain local io.open — no network, no server, no sandbox, no secrets. (3) The surfacing path the judge proposes (load on selection/status-change, NEVER on the 1s tick) is exactly how gateDecisionSummary/decision-log already works (dashboard 2112, 4197 — comment literally says \"NEVER on the 1s refresh tick\"). (4) Not redundant: AskUserQuestion's pending data is captured by the HOOK at pretooluse time (cc-status.sh:123), but ExitPlanMode and TodoWrite are captured by NO hook and NO status field, so the transcript scan adds genuinely new information and reflects the live plan even when no PLAN.md exists. No plan/PLAN.md surfacing exists in todos.md/context.md/README, so it isn't already shipped or queued. Cannot knock it to DONT_ADD: the surviving value does not depend on the agent-execution loop, the sandbox, multi-user auth, or stored secrets — it is pure local-transcript reading Shepherd already performs. ADD is also wrong: copying OpenHands verbatim (\"fetch a remote file from a running session\") is physically impossible here (no remote, no server), so a reshape onto the local transcript/fx-read primitive is mandatory — the textbook definition of ADAPT. The optional fallback (a plain local <cwd>/PLAN.md read through the existing fx table) is also a local file read, not a remote/sandbox path. Doesn't match L1-L4 (agent-profile/policy/template/routing systems), so reinforces is empty; it extends the existing status/snippet-surfacing layer.

#### Per-session hooks inventory/inspector  `[H/S]`
- **For:** This is the rare OpenHands feature whose data model maps almost 1:1 onto something Shepherd ALREADY depends on, with zero double-mismatch baggage. The candidate reads a local, declarative JSON file of lifecycle hooks grouped by event type with per-matcher pattern/command/timeout — and Claude Code's own hook schema (in ~/.claude/settings.json, plus project-level .claude/settings.json / settings.local.json) has the SAME events (SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PreToolUse, PostToolUse, Stop, SessionEnd, plus Notification/PermissionRequest) and the SAME array-of-groups-with-matcher/command/timeout shape (verified in settings-hooks.json). Shepherd doesn't merely tolerate this file — it WRITES it: core.mergeHooks + install.sh jq-merge cc-status.sh/cc-approve.sh/cc-popup.sh into the user's settings, and OUR_HOOK_SCRIPTS already recognizes Shepherd's own hooks. So an inspector is a natural, high-utility diagnostic: today the install prints 'hooks already present (no change)' at install time and never again, yet Shepherd's entire status/approve/gate machinery silently breaks if those hooks get clobbered, mis-timed (the gate needs the 130s PreToolUse timeout), or shadowed by a foreign hook. A read-only 'configured lifecycle hooks, grouped by event, with Shepherd's own marked' view turns an invisible install-time merge into a live, verifiable surface — directly serving the watch/diagnose console. The 'read the file fresh each request' idiom also matches Shepherd's ~1s refresh-from-local-files model exactly, and it's pure-parse work over a file format the codebase already manipulates, so it lands as effort S.
- **Against:** It is adjacent to Shepherd's core mission rather than central. Shepherd watches/steers/spawns SESSIONS; hook configuration is fleet-global plumbing the user touches once (via make setup) and rarely again, so a dedicated inspector risks being a low-traffic panel. The candidate's per-CONVERSATION framing doesn't transfer cleanly: OpenHands hooks are workspace-scoped (.openhands/hooks.json), whereas Shepherd's load-bearing hooks live in the single user-global ~/.claude/settings.json — so 'per-session' is mostly the same data on every tile (only project .claude/settings.json adds per-cwd variation, which most of Adam's VS-Code-extension sessions won't have). There's also overlap with make doctor, which already reports tooling/wiring health; a hook inventory could fold into that diagnostic rather than warrant its own modal. And the expandable-modal/i18n-event-rows frontend is OpenHands web-UI scaffolding (EVENT_TYPE_I18N_KEYS, hook-matcher-content.tsx) that Shepherd's embedded single-user webview shouldn't copy verbatim. None of these kill it, but they argue for a small, embedded read-only view, not a faithful port of the OpenHands modal.
- **Ruling:** Passes the double-mismatch test cleanly: the value is a read-only inventory of a declarative config file on local disk, NOT proxying an agent-server, running an agent, sandboxing, auth, or secrets. The OpenHands impl proxies /api/hooks to an agent-server, but the PATTERN (parse a hooks file, group by event type, show matcher/command/timeout/async per row) is server-independent and exactly the 'management/UI over local files' transfer the brief sanctions. It does NOT match L1-L4: those are spawn-side construction (agent profiles, policy bundles, prompt templates, routing topology), whereas this is observability over an existing config — a diagnostic, which is why category is Settings & Profiles. The decisive point in favor: Shepherd is the thing that injects these hooks and breaks if they're wrong, so surfacing them is self-serving diagnostics, not generic feature creep. Reshape is required (fleet-global file, not per-conversation; reuse OUR_HOOK_SCRIPTS to mark Shepherd's own and flag the gate timeout; merge in any per-cwd .claude/settings*.json for the selected tile; embed in the Settings area or make-doctor rather than a faithful modal port), hence ADAPT over ADD. Drop async_flag (Claude Code hooks have no async flag — only type/command/timeout/matcher) and all i18n scaffolding. Effort S: pure cc-core parse of a file already read/written, one read-only webview view, recorder-tested.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a pure core.parseHookInventory(settingsJson [, projectSettingsJson]) in cc-core.lua that walks the same hooks table mergeHooks operates on, groups entries by event (SessionStart/UserPromptSubmit/PreToolUse/PostToolUse/PermissionRequest/Notification/Stop/SessionEnd), and per group returns {matcher, command, timeout, isOurs} where isOurs reuses M.OUR_HOOK_SCRIPTS (cc-status/cc-approve/cc-popup). Add a read-only 'Hooks' view: simplest is a section in the gear Settings panel and/or a line in make doctor / install.sh --tools-only output (which already knows about the merge), with a per-event count badge and expandable rows marking Shepherd's own hooks and warning if the PreToolUse cc-approve.sh entry lacks its 130s timeout (the install migrate_timeout already encodes this rule). For a per-session twist, when a tile is selected and its cwd has a .claude/settings.json or settings.local.json, read+merge it (new FX file reader) so the inspector shows that session's effective hooks. No new server/proxy; reads local files on the existing refresh idiom. Drop async_flag and EVENT_TYPE_I18N_KEYS scaffolding.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails on every common-error axis, so ADAPT holds. (a) No in-process executor/sandbox: the value is a read-only parse of a declarative config file on local disk (~/.claude/settings.json, optionally a tile's cwd .claude/settings.json), not proxying OpenHands' /api/hooks to an agent-server. Shepherd ALREADY walks this exact grouped-by-event hooks table — M.mergeHooks (cc-core.lua:2563) iterates existing.hooks[ev] groups and M.OUR_HOOK_SCRIPTS={cc-status.sh,cc-approve.sh,cc-popup.sh} (cc-core.lua:2562) marks Shepherd's own — so the parse is pure cc-core over data the app reads/merges today. (b) No server/multi-user/secrets: reads a local file on the existing ~1s refresh idiom; fits context.md's hard scope. (c) Not redundant and not a faithful L1-L4 duplicate: grep 'hook' in claude-dashboard.lua shows only operator-action writers and refresh-loop comments — there is NO inventory view shipped — and install.sh tooling_check/make doctor reports only jq/rg/fd, never the hooks merge state, so this surface does not yet exist. It also does not match L1-L4 (those are spawn-side construction: agent profiles, policy bundles, prompt templates, routing) — this is observability/diagnostics over an already-merged config. That is precisely why it must NOT be ADD: the parsing primitive and the 130s-timeout rule already exist in core and the installer (install.sh:91 'cc-approve.sh ... +timeout:130'), so the work is reuse/reshape, not new capability. (d) The OpenHands /api/hooks proxy and async_flag ARE server plumbing — Claude Code hooks have only type/command/timeout/matcher (confirmed against settings-hooks.json) — but the judge already drops async_flag, the proxy, the i18n scaffolding, and the per-request/per-conversation framing, reshaping to a fleet-global file + optional per-cwd merge embedded in the gear ⚙ Settings panel (settings-btn exists, claude-dashboard.lua:3002) and/or make doctor. The decisive point: Shepherd is the thing that injects these hooks and breaks if cc-approve lacks its 130s timeout, so a read-only 'are my hooks wired correctly, and which are mine' inventory is self-serving diagnostics for a local single-user console that watches/steers/spawns sessions — genuine value, no agent loop, no sandbox, no server, no secrets. Effort S: one pure core.parseHookInventory reusing OUR_HOOK_SCRIPTS + one read-only view + an optional FX file reader for the per-tile cwd merge.

#### Automatic conversation title generation (one-shot, self-disabling polling callback)  `[H/M]`
- **For:** The NEED is real and already half-built. Shepherd names every tile `basename "$CWD"` (cc-status.sh:60), which is uninformative when many sessions share one folder — a case Shepherd explicitly cares about (the same-directory collision warning). A short, content-derived title per tile would meaningfully improve a fleet console where you scan 10+ tiles to find "the one working on auth." Crucially, Shepherd ALREADY parses the local transcript for exactly this kind of summary: `transcriptSnippet` pulls the latest assistant text and the panel shows `it.last_prompt` ("Last: …", dashboard ~4355). So an auto-title is a near-free extension of existing machinery and fits the relabel/label system (applyLabelsByCwd / setLabel, keyed by the stable projectKey) — an auto-derived label that the manual relabel can override. The lifecycle shape (derive once, then stop; retry if not yet derivable) maps cleanly onto Shepherd's existing one-shot disciplines.
- **Against:** The literal mechanism is squarely on the DONT_ADD side of the double mismatch: OpenHands' `_poll_for_title` calls the sandboxed agent-server to get a MODEL-derived title (an LLM summarization call) and runs as an ADMIN webhook callback against a multi-tenant conversation store. Shepherd runs no agent loop, makes no API calls, and must never burn model tokens for chrome — so it cannot reproduce a model-generated title. Separately, the feature is pitched as an EXEMPLAR of the 'fires once then DISABLED' callback lifecycle, but Shepherd already has that pattern in spades: stepAutoRespawn and stepAutoContinue are bounded, per-folder, reset-on-clean-done loops, the watchdog has a re-alert guard, and the refresh loop's 'never fire from a missing prev' edge discipline IS one-shot-with-retry. So the lifecycle lesson is redundant, and the title mechanism specifically is unbuildable as-is.
- **Ruling:** The double mismatch kills the IMPLEMENTATION (poll the sandboxed agent-server for a model-derived title; ADMIN webhook on a multi-tenant store) but not the NEED. Reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives, the title comes from the LOCAL transcript Shepherd already reads — zero model tokens, zero server — not from an LLM call. Concretely: add a pure `core.deriveAutoTitle(transcriptTail)` that returns the first user prompt (or first assistant text) collapsed/truncated, reusing the line-scan already in transcriptSnippet/transcriptError. Store it as an auto-label distinct from manual relabels: a manual setLabel always wins; the auto-title only fills the slot when no manual label exists (mirrors applyLabelsByCwd's projectKey-vs-cwd precedence). The 'one-shot then disabled, retry until available' lifecycle becomes: compute once per session, cache by key, recompute only while still empty (first message may not have landed) — exactly the bounded, edge-gated discipline stepAutoContinue already follows, off the 1s tick (like gateDecisionSummary, compute on selection/status-change). This is an extension of the existing relabel/label system, not a parallel system, so it's ADAPT rather than ADD; it doesn't match an L1-L4 backlog item closely enough to reinforce one (L1 is the agent-profile registry — naming is adjacent but not the same need). Off by default to honor the 'everything that automates is off by default' invariant, with the manual-relabel-always-wins guarantee.
- **How (Shepherd):** New pure helper in cc-core.lua: `core.deriveAutoTitle(transcriptTail, maxLen)` — scan the tail (reuse transcriptSnippet's JSON-line loop) for the first user prompt or earliest assistant text, whitespace-collapse + UTF-8-safe truncate (utf8trunc already exists). Surface it as a separate display layer below the manual relabel: in tile-name resolution, prefer `it.label` (manual relabel via cc-labels.json) > auto-title > `it.name` (folder basename). Cache the auto-title by key/projectKey (new cc-autotitles.json or the in-memory prev row), recompute only while empty — the 'fires once, retry until set' lifecycle becomes 'compute until non-empty, then leave alone'. Compute off the 1s tick (on selection / status change, like core.gateDecisionSummary), never a model call. Pass it through esc() at the render sink (XSS invariant). Settings toggle, default OFF. The status hook (cc-status.sh) already captures last_prompt at userpromptsubmit and could write it as the seed so the title appears immediately.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; ADAPT holds. I verified the judge's mapping against the actual code and could not knock it down to DONT_ADD on any of the four grounds. (a) Executor/sandbox: The literal OpenHands implementation (poll the sandboxed agent-server up to 4x for a MODEL-derived title) does NOT transfer and is correctly discarded — Shepherd runs no agent loop, no server, makes no model calls. But the judge's reshape replaces the model call with a pure local-transcript scan, so the value no longer depends on the executor. Not a kill. (b) Auth/secrets/server: The OpenHands version is an ADMIN webhook on a multi-tenant store; that half is dead too. The reshape is a pure helper + a local JSON cache (cc-autotitles.json) keyed by projectKey, single-user, no secrets. Not a kill. (c) Redundant with existing/queued — the strongest refutation candidate, but it only downgrades ADD->ADAPT, not to DONT_ADD: Shepherd already (i) writes last_prompt into the status JSON (cc-status.sh:199) and surfaces it as "Last: ..." in the DETAIL panel (dashboard:4355), and (ii) shows transcriptSnippet activity ("Doing:"/"Why:") per tile. So a transcript-derived blurb already exists. HOWEVER the tile NAME itself is still just the folder basename (cc-status.sh:60 `basename "$CWD"`; cc-core `proj:match("([^/]+)/?$")`), rendered as `it.label || it.name` (dashboard:4969,4342). An auto-title that fills the name slot is visible across the WHOLE grid without selecting a tile — a distinct affordance from the selection-gated "Last:" line. No L1-L4 item is the same need (L1's `name` is the saved-profile name, not a per-session derived title). So it is an extension of the relabel/label layer, not a parallel system — exactly ADAPT. (d) Server-plumbing-mistaken-for-UI: The 'fires once then DISABLED, retry until available' lifecycle is genuine end-user-facing behavior, and it maps cleanly onto Shepherd's existing edge-gated discipline (compute-until-non-empty off selection/status-change, like gateDecisionSummary, never on the 1s tick). Not plumbing. All the cited primitives exist and check out: transcriptSnippet (cc-core.lua:1544, the JSON-line tail scan + whitespace-collapse), utf8trunc (1534), setLabel/applyLabelsByCwd precedence (293-314, projectKey-keyed). The mapping is faithful, default-OFF honors the automate-off invariant, and manual-relabel-always-wins is the correct precedence. ADD->ADAPT is the right call because it reinforces the label system rather than introducing a new one; it does not match an L1-L4 item tightly enough to be a pure reinforce. Ruling holds at ADAPT.

#### Conversation terminal-state detection + error-reason taxonomy classification  `[H/M]`
- **For:** Triage value is real and the pure-classifier half maps directly onto Shepherd's primitives. Shepherd already derives an "error" status (transcriptError() catches api_error freezes) and already stashes a free-text it.error_message, but it never buckets the cause. A best-effort keyword classifier (budget_exceeded / model_error / runtime_error / timeout / user_cancelled / unknown) is exactly the kind of zero-hs.*, deterministic, unit-testable function cc-core.lua is built for, and it would let the panel filter/group errored tiles and let the insights view + shift report break down "what killed sessions today" instead of one undifferentiated error count. The terminal-state half is also already half-built: SessionEnd, Stop->done, and the derived error state cover finished/errored/stuck; the missing buckets are signals Shepherd can read locally (usage-limit it already detects via autoDeny by='usage-limit'; ESC/cancel; watchdog stall=stuck).
- **Against:** The detection mechanism OpenHands describes is pure server/executor plumbing that doesn't exist here: ConversationStateUpdateEvent and execution_status come off OpenHands' in-process agent event stream (key=='stats'/'execution_status', is_terminal()) — Shepherd has no agent loop and no such event; it reconstructs state from hooks + transcript tails, so none of that code transfers. And the classifier is admittedly best-effort keyword matching on an English error string, which is brittle: Claude Code's api_error formats are undocumented and change, so the buckets will misfire and may give false confidence. Shepherd already surfaces the verbatim error_message to the human (its whole reason to exist is human-in-the-loop), so an auto-bucket adds modest value over just reading the message. Real risk of a taxonomy that mostly resolves to "unknown".
- **Ruling:** The double mismatch kills the IMPLEMENTATION (event-stream watcher off an agent executor) but not the NEED. The need — classify why a terminal session ended so the dashboard can filter/triage — fits Shepherd's watch role and rides primitives that already exist: the derived error status + it.error_message, the model_change/session_end ledger events, the autoDeny by='usage-limit' (budget) signal, and the watchdog 'hung'=stuck signal. The reshape is small: add a pure M.classifyError(message) -> bucket in cc-core.lua, stamp it onto the error tile (it.error_reason) and into the ledger, then add an error-type facet to fleetStats/shiftReport for "errors by cause." It does NOT match L1-L4 (agent profiles / guardrail bundles / templates / routing — orthogonal), so reinforces is empty; this is a small new extension of the Observability/ledger layer, not a parallel system. Honest caveat baked into scope: keep it best-effort and always show the raw message, so misclassification never hides the truth from the human.
- **How (Shepherd):** cc-core.lua: add pure M.classifyError(message) returning budget_exceeded|model_error|runtime_error|timeout|user_cancelled|unknown via lowercase keyword match (extend transcriptError()'s result with .reason). claude-dashboard.lua ~line 5426: set it.error_reason = core.classifyError(err.message) when it.status becomes "error"; render a small cause badge on the error tile + add it as a tile-search/filter facet. Ledger: include reason on the error/auto_respawn event so M.fleetStats (totals ~958) and the shift report can add an "errors by cause" breakdown, plus a bucketEvents metric. Prefer non-message signals where they exist: usage-limit autoDeny (by='usage-limit') -> budget_exceeded, watchdog hung -> stuck/timeout. All off the critical path; raw error_message always still shown.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted but the ADAPT verdict holds. The OpenHands double-mismatch correctly kills the IMPLEMENTATION (watching ConversationStateUpdateEvent/execution_status off an in-process agent executor's event stream, with executor-native terminal states finished/errored/budget-exceeded) — Shepherd has no agent loop, no event stream, no sandbox, no server. But the NEED — label why a session terminated so the dashboard can filter/triage — is pure observability and squarely Shepherd's watch role. I verified the real anchor exists: cc-core.lua:1588 M.transcriptError(text) already captures an aborted-turn's API-error message and returns { message }, and claude-dashboard.lua:5426-5427 stamps it.error_message and overrides it.status to "error". A pure M.classifyError(message)->bucket keyword matcher fits cc-core's deterministic/unit-tested style with zero executor/sandbox/server/secrets dependency, all off the critical path with the raw message always shown. It is orthogonal to L1-L4 (profiles/guardrails/templates/routing), so reinforces is correctly empty — a small extension of the existing Observability/ledger layer, not a parallel system. TWO FACTUAL ERRORS in the judge's mapping (they weaken the mapping, not the verdict): (1) The cited "autoDeny by='usage-limit' -> budget_exceeded" signal does NOT exist — grepping the whole repo finds no 'usage-limit' string; the gate's `by` values are only autoDeny/autoAllow/autopilot/approveRepeats/human/timeout-fallback, and autoDeny is a tool-gate denial, not a budget signal. (2) There is NO persisted `error` ledger event to "stamp .reason onto"; the error status is a transient in-memory it.status recomputed each refresh tick and never written to the ledger — so the fleetStats/shiftReport "errors by cause" facet requires first INVENTING a new persisted error event (a normal new ledger event type, like the existing model_change/session_end/auto_respawn, not new infra). Verified-true primitives the reshape genuinely rides: transcriptError() (extend result with .reason), it.error_message render path (4351/4954), model_change (2433) + session_end/session_start (cc-status.sh hooks) ledger events, fleetStats/shiftReport/bucketEvents aggregators, and the watchdog `hung` signal (-> stuck/timeout). I cannot honestly force DONT_ADD: the capture primitive demonstrably exists and the reshape is a real, local, single-user console improvement; the verdict stays ADAPT with the budget/ledger-event parts of the mapping corrected to non-existent-until-added.

#### Open / track Pull Requests & Merge Requests as agent MCP tools (create + status checks + PR metadata)  `[H/M]`
- **For:** The status/metadata half is a real management pattern Shepherd can host cheaply. OpenHands records the PR number on the conversation, surfaces a "continue refining the PR" backlink, and polls open/closed state (get_pr_details/is_pr_open). Mapped onto Shepherd: a "conversation" is a session/tile, so the equivalent is to detect the open PR for a session's project and badge the tile with its number+state, add an "open PR in browser" jump, and roll PR-opened/merged transitions into lineage + insights + the shift report. Shepherd already has every piece of LOCAL plumbing this needs and nothing more: M.repoFromRemote (git remote -> owner/repo), a cached FX.gitRoot used by core.collisions, hs.execute git access, /usr/bin/open for the link, and a locally-installed gh CLI (/opt/homebrew/bin/gh) that can read PR status read-only via the user's own auth (gh pr view --json state,url) with NO stored tokens. PRs are the natural "output" of a coding session; surfacing "this session shipped PR #123, now merged" turns the watch/steer console into a true work-tracking fleet view, which is squarely Shepherd's job.
- **Against:** The headline of the feature — create_pr/create_mr/create_bitbucket_pr/create_azure_devops_pr as MCP tools — is a clean triple scope violation and is also redundant. (1) It surfaces tools to a running agent loop via OpenHands' in-process FastMCP server mounted in app.py; Shepherd runs no agent loop and no server, so it has nothing to mount tools into. (2) Each tool reads provider tokens from request headers and calls GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket/Azure REST APIs; Shepherd stores no secrets and makes no outbound API calls — a hard charter line in context.md. (3) It is redundant on its own terms: the already-running Claude Code GUI session creates PRs itself (gh pr create or its own GitHub MCP), owning both the executor and the credentials — Shepherd would be wiring a PR-creation tool into an agent loop it does not drive. So the create_* layer is pure DONT_ADD, and even the status layer is arguably marginal: it is a small enrichment, not a primitive, and could be deferred indefinitely.
- **Ruling:** Two layers on opposite sides of the double mismatch. The create_pr/create_mr MCP-tool layer is DONT_ADD on all three counts (no executor to surface tools to, no server to mount FastMCP in, no stored provider tokens) AND redundant (the live Claude Code session already opens PRs via its own gh/MCP). That half is excluded — Shepherd must NOT reimplement PR creation. The save_pr_metadata + backlink + is_pr_open/get_pr_details layer is a legitimate management/observability pattern that reshapes onto Shepherd primitives using only local, read-only tooling Shepherd already ships (repoFromRemote, FX.gitRoot, hs.execute, /usr/bin/open, local gh). Hence ADAPT, not DONT_ADD and not ADD-as-is. It does not squarely match L1-L4 (those are the agent/policy/template/routing registries, not artifact tracking), so reinforces is left empty; it instead extends the shipped insights/lineage/collisions surface. Note the "default open when indeterminate" heuristic from OpenHands should be dropped — Shepherd's local gh gives a definite answer, and Shepherd's ethos is no-guessing/observation-not-transition, so an indeterminate PR state should simply show no badge.
- **How (Shepherd):** Build ONLY the status-surfacing half, none of the create_* tools. (1) Pure cc-core: core.parsePrStatus(jsonFromGh) -> {number, state(open|merged|closed), url, title}, plus core.prBadge(item) deciding the tile glyph/color; unit-tested with recorder doubles, zero hs.*. (2) Side effect in claude-dashboard.lua behind the fx table: best-effort, cached, opt-in poll using the LOCAL gh CLI for the session's project (gh -C <gitRoot> pr view --json number,state,url,title 2>/dev/null), reusing the cached FX.gitRoot already feeding core.collisions and M.repoFromRemote; no token storage, no API call by Shepherd. (3) Tile badge "PR #123 open/merged" + a Jump-style action that does /usr/bin/open <url> (mirrors the existing open/jump effects). (4) Feed PR-opened and PR-merged as ledger events so they appear in session lineage, fleet insights, and the shift report ("3 PRs opened, 1 merged this shift"). Off by default like every automation; self-gates when gh is absent or the folder has no remote. Explicitly do NOT add create_pr/create_mr/Bitbucket/Azure tools, do NOT store provider tokens, do NOT make Shepherd call any VCS API — the running session owns PR creation.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute and could not. Verified against the actual source: the create_pr/create_mr/create_bitbucket_pr/create_azure_devops_pr MCP-tool half is correctly DONT_ADD on the double mismatch — Shepherd runs no agent loop and mounts no FastMCP server to surface tools to, stores no provider tokens, and the live Claude Code session already opens PRs via its own gh/MCP. The judge already EXCLUDED that half; excluding-and-reshaping is exactly what ADAPT means, so this is not a reason to downgrade to DONT_ADD. The surviving status-surfacing half survives the mechanical checklist: (a) no in-process executor/sandbox needed — a read-only `gh -C <gitRoot> pr view --json number,state,url,title` poll is the same shape as the EXISTING collision git-root shellout (claude-dashboard.lua:954 FX.gitRoot, cached in gitRootByCwd) and FX.runImprove's `git -C ... remote get-url` call; (b) no server / no multi-user / no stored secrets — gh uses its own local auth, Shepherd calls no VCS API and stores no token; (c) NOT redundant — grep confirms zero existing PR/MR/gh-pr handling in cc-core.lua/claude-dashboard.lua, and PR tracking is NOT in todos.md/context.md; it doesn't match L1-L4 (agent/policy/template/routing registries), so 'reinforces' is correctly empty and it instead extends the shipped insights/lineage/collisions surface; (d) it's an end-user console feature (tile badge 'PR #123 open/merged' + a /usr/bin/open jump action mirroring claude-dashboard.lua:1423), not server plumbing. Confirmed primitives exist and are real: gh IS installed (/opt/homebrew/bin/gh, v2.79.0), FX.gitRoot + M.repoFromRemote (cc-core.lua:470) + M.collisions (cc-core.lua:2334) + the append-only JSONL ledger + M.fleetStandup shift report (cc-core.lua:1137) + M.lineageByProject (cc-core.lua:1061) all ship. Strongest refutation I found, and why it fails: the shift-report code documents an explicit design boundary — 'Reports OPERATIONS ONLY: there is NO what-shipped changelog, by design ... Shepherd has no git/CI/diff ground truth, so the honest signal ... is the continue/auto_continue count, not a claimed outcome' (cc-core.lua:1126-1129). A 'N PRs opened/merged this shift' line looks like it crosses that line. But the boundary's stated rationale is that a PROMPT is a claimed, unverifiable outcome and Shepherd lacks GIT GROUND TRUTH. A PR open/merged state read from local gh IS git ground truth — the exact thing the comment says is absent — so it is the one honest outcome Shepherd can observe, categorically different from a prompt-as-claimed-outcome. The judge also correctly flagged that OpenHands' 'default-open-when-indeterminate' heuristic must be DROPPED (no-guessing ethos: show no badge when gh can't answer). With that correction the tension dissolves. Net: it genuinely improves a LOCAL single-user console that WATCHES Claude Code sessions — a tile badge for a real artifact the watched session produced is observation, not transition, and self-gates when gh is absent or there's no remote. Holds as ADAPT (build only the read-only status-surfacing half; none of the create_* tools, no tokens, no VCS API calls by Shepherd).

#### Per-session detail with switchable/pinnable sub-panels persisted per-conversation (tabs: Changes/Code/Terminal/Browser/Planner/Task List)  `[H/M]`
- **For:** Shepherd's per-session detail panel (claude-dashboard.lua ~line 3048, the #detail div) is already a busy flat stack — head/dot/status, pending, ask, activity, prompt, meta, lineage, decisions, usage, action buttons, four live-control dropdowns, nudge/queue/templates — and the richest per-session views (Timeline, Shift report, fleet insights, fleet search) live in a SEPARATE audit overlay rather than beside the selected tile. There is genuine information-density pressure, and the OpenHands pattern is a clean answer: a small tab bar that switches one right-pane region between sub-views, with a pin/unpin context menu to hide tabs you never use, and the selected-tab + panel-shown state persisted per-conversation so reopening a session restores your working view. That maps cleanly onto Shepherd primitives: a conversation is a tile/projectKey, and the candidate views become tabs over data Shepherd ALREADY computes — Activity (live), Timeline (core.sessionTimeline), Decisions (the decision-log), Usage (token/context), Queue (the per-session editor), Lineage. localStorage is available in the embedded webview, and per-session state already persists by projectKey (labels, groups, gate.tools), so keying view-prefs the same way is idiomatic. Lazy-loading per-tab also fits: the Timeline/decision-log already load on-demand (per selection, never on the 1s tick — see requestDecisions / ccDecisions guards), so a tab system would formalize a discipline the code already follows. It would declutter the detail panel and let the user pin the two or three views they actually watch.
- **Against:** The specific tabs OpenHands ships — Changes (git diff), Code (embedded VS Code), Terminal, Browser, Planner, Task List — are almost entirely the WRONG half of the double mismatch. Code/Terminal/Browser are live views INTO the Docker/remote sandbox the executor manages; Shepherd has no sandbox and no in-process agent, so there is nothing to embed, and embedding a VS Code/terminal/browser surface contradicts Shepherd's whole model (its job is to JUMP to the user's OWN native VS Code/Cursor/Kitty/Terminal window via fx.focusWindow, not to host a copy). Browser specifically is the agent's browsing-tool observation stream — pure executor output. Planner and Task List are the agent loop's internal scratchpad, which only exists because OpenHands runs the loop; Claude Code owns its own todo/plan state and Shepherd never sees it. Changes (git diff) is the closest to plausible, but cc-core.lua line 1128 states the invariant outright: "Shepherd has no git/CI/diff ground truth," and surfacing live diffs would mean shelling git per selected tile against a possibly-drifted cwd — new data plumbing for marginal value over just jumping to the editor. So copying the feature as-is is DONT_ADD; only the container pattern (pin/persist/switchable sub-panels) transfers, and even that is a nicety, not a gap — the detail panel already works, has collapsible sections (detailExpanded), and the heavy views already live in an overlay, so the marginal UX win is real but modest and competes with higher-value backlog (L1 agent profiles).
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch to the CONTENTS vs the CONTAINER separately. CONTENTS: Changes/Code/Terminal/Browser/Planner/Task List are sandbox/executor surfaces (embedded workspace views plus the agent's internal plan state). Shepherd hosts no sandbox and runs no agent loop, so five of the six tabs have no data source and contradict the jump-to-native-window model; git Changes is explicitly disclaimed by the codebase (cc-core.lua:1128). Copying the feature literally is DONT_ADD. CONTAINER: the underlying NEED — organizing an already-overloaded per-session detail view into switchable, pin/unpin-able, per-session-persisted sub-panels with lazy loading — is real and maps onto primitives Shepherd already has (detail panel, Timeline, Decisions, Usage, Queue, Lineage), with localStorage available and per-projectKey persistence already idiomatic. That is textbook ADAPT: reshape the pattern, drop the sandbox tabs, populate tabs from data Shepherd already computes. It reinforces no queued L-item (those are agent/policy/template/routing, not view organization), so reinforces stays empty. The user's saved-agents/skills/MCP priority doesn't bear on this UI-shell decision, so no favorable weighting applies. Verdict ADAPT, high confidence: the divergence between literal feature (DONT_ADD) and underlying pattern (worth adapting) is exactly what ADAPT is for.
- **How (Shepherd):** Reshape into a tab bar over the existing #detail panel in claude-dashboard.lua, NOT the OpenHands tab set. Replace the flat stacked sections with a small tab strip whose tabs are views Shepherd already produces: Activity (the live d-activity/prompt/pending block, default), Timeline (core.sessionTimeline, currently a separate overlay — pull it inline), Decisions (the existing decision-log: requestDecisions/ccDecisions), Usage (token/context tracking), Queue (the per-session queue editor at ~line 2808), and optionally Lineage. Tabs pin/unpin via a context menu (Shepherd already does native right-click menus on tiles — see the popup-menu path near line 3515) with hidden tabs filtered out. Persist {selectedTab, detailShown, unpinnedTabs} to localStorage in the webview, keyed by projectKey (the same stable identity used for labels/groups/gate.tools), so reopening a session restores its view. Keep the existing lazy-load discipline (load Timeline/Decisions on selection + on status transitions, never on the 1s tick). Explicitly DROP the Changes/Code/Terminal/Browser tabs — no sandbox to render them; the Code/Terminal affordance stays the existing Jump button (fx.focusWindow to the user's native editor). No new git-diff plumbing. Pure-UI change: no new cc-core logic beyond reusing existing aggregators, so it is mostly panel JS plus the persistence keying.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute and could not honestly knock this below ADAPT. Running the four common-error tests: (a) Executor/sandbox — does NOT apply to the adapted version. The judge already amputated the five sandbox/executor tabs (Changes/Code/Terminal/Browser/Planner/Task List) as DONT_ADD. I confirmed Shepherd has no git/diff/CI ground truth: cc-core.lua:1128-1129 explicitly disclaims a "what shipped" changelog ("Shepherd has no git/CI/diff ground truth"). And there is no embedded Code/Terminal/Browser view anywhere — the README's editor affordance is "Jump to window" (fx.focusWindow to the native editor), confirmed in claude-dashboard.lua (b-jump act('focus'), native popup "Jump to window"). The CONTAINER being adapted — a tab strip over views Shepherd already renders — needs no agent loop or sandbox. (b) Multi-user/secrets/server — not involved at all. Persistence is local-only. (c) Redundant with existing/queued — this is the only real refutation angle, and it weakens the supporting argument but not the verdict. Shepherd's #detail (claude-dashboard.lua:3048-3117) is TODAY a flat stack of exactly the views the judge would tab: d-activity, d-pending, d-prompt, d-meta, d-lineage, d-decisions, d-usage, plus a separate Timeline overlay (openSessionTimeline / core.sessionTimeline) and the per-session Queue editor. So the DATA all exists, but there is NO tabbing/pin/persist affordance today — it is a genuine new (small) UI organization, not a duplicate system. It correctly reinforces no L-item (L1-L4 are agent/policy/template/routing, not view-shell), which is consistent with ADAPT, not with a queued duplicate. This makes it cosmetic-leaning but not redundant. (d) Not server/library plumbing — it is a real end-user console feature. Two mapping inaccuracies I found do NOT change the verdict but should be flagged: (1) the judge says "localStorage available... per-projectKey persistence already idiomatic" — but grep shows localStorage/sessionStorage is used NOWHERE in the webview; Shepherd's persistence is server-side Lua-to-disk keyed by projectKey (relabel/groups, claude-dashboard.lua:3544-3556). Persisting {selectedTab,detailShown,unpinnedTabs} would be a NEW channel (either webview localStorage or another disk file), not the existing idiom. (2) The pin/unpin "3-dot menu" maps onto native hs.menubar popups (claude-dashboard.lua:2254), not DOM menus, so a tab-strip menu is new webview JS. Net effect: the change is slightly larger than the "pure-UI, no new logic" framing — but still bounded, local, single-user, no executor/sandbox/server/secrets. Verdict: the literal OpenHands feature (sandbox tabs) is DONT_ADD, but the container pattern (reshape the overloaded flat detail stack into switchable, pin/unpin-able, per-session-persisted sub-panels populated from data Shepherd already computes) is a legitimate local-console improvement that survives reshaping onto existing primitives. That is the textbook ADAPT shape. I cannot push it to DONT_ADD (it is not redundant with any existing tabbing system and needs no sandbox), and it is not a clean ADD (the literal feature is mostly inapplicable sandbox tabs). ADAPT holds at high confidence.

#### Post-run summary callback + acknowledgement/status feedback (eyes reaction, tracking link, error guidance)  `[M/M]`
- **For:** The feature has two distinct halves and the SECOND half contains a genuinely transferable pattern. Strip away the GitHub bot and what remains is: "when a managed agent reaches a terminal finished state, fire a prepared instruction that asks the agent to self-review its work against a checklist (was the request fully met? are the changes concise?), and surface the result to the human." Shepherd already owns every primitive this needs. (1) The trigger exists: core.shouldFeed/shouldAutoContinue/remoteControlSweepTargets all fire on a FRESH transition into `done` through the dispatchSerialized keystroke chokepoint — an "on-done self-review prompt" is the same shape as auto-continue's "on-error type continue," just a different canned prompt into an idle/done session. (2) The prompt-template machinery exists: saved task TEMPLATES (cc-templates.json) and the L3-queued parameterized templates, plus the existing auditReviewPrompt/improvePrompt builders that inject a "review-first" instruction into a session. (3) The opt-in/off-by-default discipline is already the house style. So a "self-summary on completion" toggle — pure summaryPrompt(item) builder + a gated dispatch on the done-transition, the agent's reply landing in its own transcript/tile and optionally ledgered as a `summary` event — fits cleanly as a small Events & Callbacks extension. The acknowledgement half also has a faint local echo worth noting: Shepherd already does immediate visual feedback on state change (tile color flips, notification history) and even ledgers escalation at fire time, so "acknowledge that work started/finished" is conceptually native, just rendered as a tile/ledger entry rather than a posted comment.
- **Against:** Both halves are deep in OpenHands' double mismatch and the strongest reading is that almost nothing survives intact. The acknowledgement half — _add_reaction('eyes'), the "I'm on it! track at <CONVERSATION_URL>" reply, and the MissingSettings/LLMAuthentication/SessionExpired error branches — exists ONLY because OpenHands is a multi-user web server with a per-user secrets store and a remote issue/PR thread to post into and a hosted conversation URL to link to. Shepherd has no bot, no remote thread, no posting target, no conversation URL, and explicitly NO stored secrets/auth — so "re-login," "set your LLM key," and "session expired" map to literally nothing (Shepherd resolves $ENV via the spawned shell and never authenticates). The summary half leans on the sandboxed agent-execution loop: the EventCallbackProcessor watches execution_status=='finished' (a status Shepherd doesn't produce — it only ever sees done/idle heartbeat-less files) and calls the SANDBOX ask_agent to generate text — Shepherd cannot ask an agent anything; it can only TYPE into a GUI session that Claude Code itself drives, and it has no clean signal that a turn "finished successfully" vs merely went idle. And the "did this fully meet the request / are changes concise" claim is exactly the outcome-claim Shepherd's fleetStandup deliberately REFUSES to make ("a prompt is an instruction, not an outcome … Shepherd has no git/CI/diff ground truth") — adding a self-graded summary risks manufacturing the false-ground-truth the project intentionally rejected. So most of the feature is DONT_ADD; only the thin "inject a self-review prompt on idle" kernel is salvageable.
- **Ruling:** The candidate is a bundle: (a) immediate ack (eyes reaction + tracking-link comment), (b) failure-path error guidance (re-login/set-key/session-expired), (c) post-finish self-summary posted back to the origin thread. Halves (a) and (b) are pure double-mismatch — they require a bot, a remote issue/PR thread, a hosted conversation URL, multi-user auth, and a stored-secrets/settings store, none of which Shepherd has or will have; the error branches in particular are artifacts of OpenHands' secrets/auth model and have no local referent. So those are DONT_ADD. But the feature is not WHOLLY redundant or wholly impossible: the underlying NEED of half (c) — "close the loop on a finished session by getting a concise self-assessment in front of the human" — is real and reshapes cleanly onto Shepherd's primitives. Shepherd already fires canned instructions on a fresh `done` transition through one serialized chokepoint (auto-continue, queue auto-feed, RC sweep), already has review-prompt builders (auditReviewPrompt/improvePrompt) and saved templates, and already keeps an opt-in ledger that could carry a `summary` event. That is the textbook ADAPT shape: real need, must be reshaped onto spawn/templates/ledger/done-transition rather than copied as a GitHub callback. I withhold high confidence and keep effort modest because there are two real hazards: Shepherd has no trustworthy "finished successfully" signal (only heartbeat-less done/idle, which is also where a user may be mid-typing), and the project has an explicit, documented stance AGAINST self-claimed outcomes in its shift report — so any summary feature must be framed as a steer ("here's a self-review prompt I typed for you") landing in the session's own transcript, not as an authoritative outcome record. I do not set reinforces: it touches L3 (templates) tangentially but is fundamentally a new on-done event-callback, not an extension of any single queued Lx.
- **How (Shepherd):** Drop the GitHub bot, reaction, tracking-link, and all auth/secrets error branches (those parts are DONT_ADD — no bot, no remote thread, no stored secrets/auth). Keep only the self-summary kernel as an opt-in, off-by-default Events & Callbacks automation: add a pure core.summaryPrompt(item) builder alongside the existing auditReviewPrompt/improvePrompt (a short checklist prompt: "before you finish — did you fully address the request? are the changes minimal/concise? summarize what you changed"), plus a pure target filter modeled on remoteControlSweepTargets/shouldAutoContinue that selects sessions on a FRESH `done` transition (skip working/approval/error, skip mid-type idle, respect a per-session/global enable like autoContinue.enabled). The dashboard types it via the existing dispatchSerialized chokepoint so the agent's reply lands in its OWN transcript/tile; optionally emit a `summary` ledger event (reusing the e.summary field the narrative/decisionGroups code already reads). Frame it in UI strictly as "Shepherd typed a self-review prompt," never as an authoritative outcome — honoring the fleetStandup "no claimed outcomes" stance. Native Claude Code owns the actual text generation; Shepherd only emits the prompt and records that it did.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute and could not. Every primitive the ruling leans on is real in the code: improvePrompt (cc-core.lua:481) and auditReviewPrompt (cc-core.lua:715) are pure prompt builders, the exact siblings a core.summaryPrompt would join — improvePrompt even ends "Present it as a plan for me to approve," the same steer-not-outcome framing. The fresh-`done`-transition filters exist (shouldFeed:1970, shouldCloseOnDone:2088, sessionFree:2020) and already skip `idle` precisely because "an idle session may be one the operator is actively typing into" (cc-core.lua:2016) — so the "skip mid-type" requirement is already solved machinery. dispatchSerialized (claude-dashboard.lua:1221) + FX.typeIntoWindow (1259) type arbitrary text into a session's OWN window, so the reply lands in its own transcript/tile. autoContinue.enabled is off-by-default (3758), giving the opt-in gating model. The ledger already reads e.summary (678/689/772). The fleetStandup "no claimed outcome — a prompt is an instruction, not an outcome" stance is real (cc-core.lua:1126-1129), but that is the documented HAZARD the judge already routed around (frame as a typed self-review steer, not an authoritative record), not a refutation. Refutation fails on every common-error axis: (a) no in-process executor/sandbox — native Claude Code generates the text, Shepherd only types a prompt into an already-running GUI session, identical to the shipped auto-continue/queue-feed/RC keystroke paths; (b) no multi-user/secrets/server — the judge already stripped the bot, eyes reaction, tracking-link, and ALL the re-login/set-key/session-expired error branches to DONT_ADD (those ARE pure OpenHands auth/secrets-model artifacts with no local referent); (c) not redundant — the closest queued item, L4 "Per-task timing/outcome counters" (todos.md:427), is explicitly OPERATIONS metrics with "NO in-process output object," and never types a self-review; auto-continue fires only on `error`, queue-feed only on an armed non-empty queue, so neither covers a self-review on a clean done. The original GitHub callback IS server/library plumbing (error path (d)), but the judge correctly carved that away. What survives — on a clean `done`, optionally type a "did you fully address the request? are changes minimal? summarize what you changed" checklist into the session's own tile via the existing chokepoint, framed as a typed steer — is a legitimate improvement to a LOCAL single-user console built entirely from existing primitives. It is a genuinely new on-done event-callback (correctly NOT marked as reinforcing any single Lx), reshaped onto spawn/templates/ledger/done-transition rather than copied. ADAPT (medium) is the right verdict.

#### Server/host resource stats + idle-since-last-activity (CPU/mem/disk/IO/uptime)  `[M/M]`
- **For:** The "explain slowness / spawn-pressure" and "idle-since-last-activity" parts are a real, structurally-hostable need on a fleet console. Shepherd already tracks per-session staleness, runs a stuck-session watchdog (core.isHung / 600s frozen), and keeps a queue-starvation clock (starvedSince/starvedAlerted) that todos.md wants to grow into auto-spawn-on-starvation. A small host-health read — CPU%, free memory, free disk, Shepherd uptime, and a fleet-wide "idle since last event" — gives the operator the missing context for "why is the whole fleet sluggish / why won't spawns land." Crucially the data is available LOCALLY with zero new infra: hs.host.cpuUsage / hs.host.vmStat / a disk-free stat, no psutil, no server, no sandbox, no secrets. It fits the existing insights/fleet-footer overlay, the aggregation is a clean pure cc-core function, and it directly feeds a signal Shepherd already computes (starvation) — so it strengthens spawn/queue decisions rather than bolting on a new system.
- **Against:** The literal feature is /server_info: monitoring the OpenHands SERVER PROCESS itself (process CPU%, RSS/VMS, read/write bytes via /proc/<pid>/io, server uptime). That is the (b) multi-user-web-server half of the double mismatch — its whole value is "is my long-running FastAPI process healthy under multi-tenant load." Shepherd has NO server process to introspect: it is a Hammerspoon webview with a ~1s refresh loop. There is no persistent agent-execution process whose RSS/IO would explain anything, macOS has no /proc to parse, and per-process IO bytes track a target that doesn't exist in Shepherd's model. Most of the payload (process RSS/VMS, read/write bytes, the /proc parse) maps to nothing. Idle-since-last-execution is also largely redundant: per-session staleness, the watchdog, and the starvation clock already cover "nothing's happening." A host-stats strip is monitoring-for-its-own-sake on a single-user laptop and risks scope creep into a mini-Activity-Monitor.
- **Ruling:** Split the feature. The process-self-monitoring core (RSS/VMS, read/write bytes, /proc/<pid>/io, "server uptime") is DONT_ADD plumbing — it monitors a server process Shepherd doesn't have, on a /proc filesystem macOS lacks, for a multi-tenant-load concern that doesn't apply. But the surrounding NEED — host capacity + idle context to explain fleet slowness and inform spawn-pressure — is genuine and already half-present (starvation clock, watchdog, fleet footer). Because the value survives only after reshaping onto Shepherd's primitives (drop the psutil/proc process introspection, swap host telemetry to hs.host, fold idle into the existing staleness/starvation signals, surface in the insights overlay not a /server_info endpoint), this is ADAPT, not ADD and not a clean DONT_ADD. It doesn't match L1-L4 (those are agent profiles / policy bundles / templates / routing, not host telemetry), so reinforces stays empty; the closest neighbors (shipped fleet footer/insights, deferred auto-spawn-on-starvation) are not Lx items. Confidence is medium: the host strip is clearly buildable and useful, but it's marginal — a single-user laptop rarely needs CPU/mem readouts, so the strongest justification is narrowly the spawn-pressure/starvation tie-in rather than general monitoring.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a pure core.hostHealth aggregator + a core.fleetIdleSince(events/tiles, now) in cc-core.lua (deterministic, injected now). Feed host telemetry from hs.host.cpuUsage / hs.host.vmStat / a disk-free read in claude-dashboard.lua's refresh loop (NOT psutil; NO /proc). Surface as a compact strip in the existing 📊 insights overlay / fleet footer: CPU%, free mem, free disk, Shepherd uptime, and fleet "idle since last event." Wire the same host signal into the existing starvedSince/starvedAlerted logic so a starvation alert can note "box is also CPU/disk-pressured" — strengthening the deferred auto-spawn-on-starvation gating. Drop entirely: process RSS/VMS, read/write IO bytes, and the /proc/<pid>/io parse (no server process, no /proc on macOS). Off-by-default / read-only display, consistent with the automation-off invariant.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails — ADAPT holds. I tried every skeptic lever and none knocks the survivor below ADAPT: (a) Executor/sandbox: N/A to the adapted core. The judge already drops the process-self-monitoring (RSS/VMS, /proc/<pid>/io read/write bytes) — correctly: Shepherd runs no server process and macOS has no /proc. What's left (host CPU%, free mem, free disk, Shepherd uptime) is plain host telemetry via hs.* in the existing ~1s refresh loop; no agent loop, no Docker/remote runtime. Confirmed Shepherd already uses the same namespace (hs.caffeinate at claude-dashboard.lua:1068-1081), so hs.host.cpuUsage/vmStat is feasible; zero hs.host usage exists today. (b) Multi-user/secrets/server: N/A. A /server_info HTTP endpoint is pure server plumbing and is dropped; the survivor is a read-only strip in the EXISTING 📊 insights overlay/fleet footer — no endpoint, no auth, no secrets, consistent with the local/single-user/no-server scope. (c) Redundant / already-have-it (the strongest lever, and where this is genuinely marginal): the idle-since-last-activity half overlaps Shepherd's per-session staleness clocks, starvedSince (claude-dashboard.lua:165), and the ledger timeline — but there is NO single fleet-wide 'idle since last event' aggregate today, so core.fleetIdleSince is a thin but genuine new pure helper, not a re-add. The HOST half is NOT present at all: no CPU/mem/disk readout, no 'uptime' anywhere in cc-core.lua/claude-dashboard.lua. So not redundant. (d) Server plumbing mistaken for a console feature: the ORIGINAL feature mostly IS that (/server_info, RSS/VMS, /proc io) — but the judge already excised all of it; the remainder is an end-user read-only display. No L1-L4 match (those are agent profiles/policy bundles/templates/routing, not host telemetry), so reinforces stays empty; the only real neighbor is the DEFERRED (non-Lx) auto-spawn-on-starvation item. The honest weakness the judge conceded survives scrutiny: on a single-user laptop raw CPU/mem/disk readouts are near-Activity-Monitor vanity, so the value rests almost entirely on the spawn-pressure tie-in — wiring a host-pressure signal into the existing starvedSince/starvedAlerted/queueStarved logic (cc-core.lua:2075; claude-dashboard.lua:5663-5671) so a starvation alert can note 'box is also CPU/disk-pressured' and gate the deferred auto-spawn. That tie-in is real and lands exactly where an auto-spawn guard would need it. Marginal but not hollow — it survives only after reshaping onto Shepherd's primitives (drop psutil/proc; swap to hs.host; fold idle into staleness/starvation; surface in insights not an endpoint), which is the textbook ADAPT envelope: not a clean DONT_ADD, and not ADD. Verdict unchanged: ADAPT, medium confidence. #### Markdown+frontmatter skill/definition file format (microagent + SKILL.md)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is the user's #1 stated interest expressed as a concrete, parse-once contract: "pre-configured skills saved into a directory." The format is exactly what Shepherd's queued L1 skills enumerator must read — a self-describing Markdown file with a leading YAML block (name/type/description/triggers) plus a prompt body. OpenHands' _parse_skill_frontmatter + _load_skills_from_dir give Shepherd a ready blueprint for the pure parser behind FX.listSkills: rglob a root, strip the leading --- YAML --- fence, fall back to the file stem for name, skip README.md, and derive a /<name> command when no slash trigger is present. The two-shape handling (flat skills/*.md microagents AND directory-per-skill SKILL.md + references/ subfolder, flagged is_agentskills_format) is a genuine refinement over L1's current single-shape wording (~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md only), and it matches reality: native Claude Code on this Mac already ships both shapes (the system-reminder skill list proves SKILL.md is the live native format). Crucially the format is READ-ONLY data for Shepherd — no executor, no sandbox, no server, no secrets touched — so it sits cleanly inside scope as operator/filesystem data, the same posture as cc-presets.json. It powers the skills card and the by-name skills[] reference threaded into --append-system-prompt/--agent at spawn-from-agent.
- **Against:** Shepherd neither authors nor executes skills — native Claude Code wholly owns the ~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md format, its frontmatter semantics, the /<name> command derivation, and the actual loading/triggering at agent runtime (the harness already lists these skills to itself). So Shepherd copying OpenHands' loader logic risks reimplementing a parser whose schema is owned by another tool and could drift; the load-bearing facts note explicitly says Shepherd "does NOT reimplement MCP/skills/plugins" and stays a thin flag/file emitter. Most of OpenHands' implementation value is server-side plumbing Shepherd can't host: SkillInfo/SkillObject DTOs, the agent-server returning content+triggers over the wire, on-demand reference-subfile following during an agent loop, and the /v1/skills proxy — all of which belong to the executor/server half of the double mismatch and are DONT_ADD. The triggers field and on-demand reference following only matter to a runtime that actually fires skills; Shepherd never does. Net residue once you strip the server/executor parts is small: a frontmatter card-reader, which L1 already commits to.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: OpenHands' skills_router.py / skill_loader.py is server-side code that returns SkillInfo objects to an agent-server which then FIRES the skills inside its execution loop and FOLLOWS reference subfiles on demand. That firing/following half is pure executor+server territory — DONT_ADD. But the candidate as framed is the FILE FORMAT and its PARSE CONTRACT, not the firing engine, and that half maps perfectly onto a pure, testable cc-core enumerator: read a directory root, parse the leading YAML, derive name/description, flag the two shapes, surface a card. That is precisely L1's "pure enumerator over ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md parsing frontmatter -> card view via FX.listSkills" plus the by-name skills[] reference resolver. So the NEED is real and already queued; the verdict is ADAPT/reinforces L1, not a new system. Two concrete enrichments justify logging it against L1 rather than treating it as fully covered: (1) widen the enumerator to BOTH shapes — flat skills/*.md microagents and directory SKILL.md + references/ — instead of L1's single SKILL.md glob, skipping README.md and falling back to the file stem for name; (2) surface the is_agentskills_format distinction and the derived /<name> command on the skills card so the operator can see, at a glance, what a referenced skill will inject. Shepherd never parses triggers for firing and never follows reference subfiles itself (Claude Code does that at runtime) — it only reads name/description/path to display and reference. Confidence high because this is the most direct fit to the user's stated #1 priority and a feature already partly specced; the only judgment call is scoping out the server/firing half, which the double mismatch resolves cleanly.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 as the concrete parse contract for the skills card. Implement as a pure cc-core enumerator behind a new FX.listSkills filesystem read: rglob ~/.claude/skills for SKILL.md AND flat *.md, skip README.md, strip the leading --- YAML --- fence to derive {name (stem fallback), display_title, description, path, shape: flat|agentskills}, and compute a /<name> command when no slash trigger is present. cc-core stays hs.*-free and unit-tested; the file read is the only side effect, funneled through fx. The skills[] names on an L1 agent profile (cc-agents.json) dereference against this enumerator via L1's config-by-reference resolver, then spawnFlags emits --append-system-prompt/--agent. Apply the existing safe-path hardening ([A-Za-z0-9._-], reject names resolving outside the skills root) before any read. Surface shape + derived /<name> + description on the profile/skills card. The judge already excised the firing/reference-following half (DONT_ADD). What remains — read a dir, strip the leading `---` YAML fence, derive name/description/path, flag the shape, render a card — is a pure cc-core enumerator with one filesystem side effect through fx. No agent loop, no sandbox. Fails. (b) Multi-user/secrets/server: It reads only name/display_title/description/path locally from ~/.claude/skills — never values, no auth, no network. Fails.
