#### MCP-tool-injecting skills (mcp_tools frontmatter / default-tools repo agent)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The declarative binding is genuinely useful and lands squarely on the user's #1 stated interest ("pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs"). Letting a skill's own definition declare the MCP servers it needs means picking that skill in a profile automatically pulls in its tooling — no separate manual step of also remembering to attach the right cc-mcp.json server. Shepherd already has every primitive to express this WITHOUT running anything: it has a skills enumerator over ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md (L1), a cc-mcp.json stdio/sse/http server registry with command/args (L1), a config-by-reference resolver, and a spawnFlags builder that emits --mcp-config/--strict-mcp-config files. So adding ONE field — parse an mcp_tools (stdio command/args) block out of skill frontmatter and have the L1 resolver fold those servers into the spawned --mcp-config file — is a small, well-fitting extension. It also matches the local CLI's capabilities exactly (claude --mcp-config + --append-system-prompt), and native Claude Code does the actual launch/registration, so the safe-path/name-sanitization hardening already planned for L1 covers it.
- **Against:** The literal OpenHands mechanic is the executor half of the double mismatch: OpenHands PARSES the frontmatter and then LAUNCHES/REGISTERS the stdio MCP server in-process and AUTO-SURFACES its tool descriptions into the live LLM loop (empty body needed because descriptions inject themselves). Shepherd runs no agent loop, registers nothing into a live LLM, and launches no MCP servers — native Claude Code owns 100% of that. So the "auto-register on load / descriptions auto-surface" behavior cannot be copied; Shepherd can only emit a config FILE at spawn-time. The shipped default-tools.md "always-on repo skill that wires an MCP fetch server for every agent" is the server/multi-tenant half: a product shipping an org-wide default that every agent inherits. Shepherd is single-user with no server-pushed defaults and no notion of an org repo. And mcp-server-fetch is a network-fetch tool — wiring a default outbound-fetch server for every session cuts against Shepherd's no-network-server posture and its OFF-BY-DEFAULT-automation principle. Finally it is largely redundant: L1 already lets a profile attach mcpServers[] and skills[] independently, so the marginal value is only the auto-coupling, not new capability.
- **Ruling:** Strip the executor/server plumbing and what remains is a declarative idea: a skill definition can record the MCP servers it depends on, so selecting the skill carries its tooling along. That is purely a parse-and-emit transform — exactly the layer Shepherd lives in (emit flags/files; native Claude Code executes). It does not need the in-process register-and-surface loop (that is Claude Code's job once the --mcp-config file exists), nor a sandbox, server, users, or stored secrets (any auth token stays an authTokenEnv NAME resolved via the spawned shell, never a value — consistent with L1's cc-mcp.json record and requiredEnv handling). It is not a parallel system: it adds one optional frontmatter field to L1's existing skills enumerator and routes the result through L1's config-by-reference resolver into the existing mcp-config-file builder. The marginal capability over plain L1 is modest (auto-coupling skill→servers rather than attaching both by hand), which is why this is ADAPT-as-an-enrichment-of-L1 rather than its own backlog item. The always-on default-tools.md variant and the descriptions-auto-surface behavior are explicitly NOT adopted (server/executor halves); at most the "fleet-default scope" notion already noted in L1 for knowledge[] could let a profile mark a skill as a fleet default — but never an org-pushed always-on one. Effort is small because it reuses L1's enumerator, resolver, builder, and name-sanitization wholesale.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 as a sub-bullet on the skills enumerator. (1) Extend the existing pure skill-frontmatter parser (over ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md) to read an optional mcp_tools.stdio_servers list ({name, command, args}) into the skill's card data. (2) In L1's config-by-reference resolver, when a profile's skills[] resolves a skill that declares mcp_tools, contribute those servers (after the planned [A-Za-z0-9._-] name + command sanitization) into the same in-memory server set that cc-mcp.json attachments feed. (3) The existing spawnFlags / mcp-config-file builder writes them into the one --mcp-config file emitted at spawn (gated by spawn.live; dry-run by default) — native Claude Code launches and surfaces them, Shepherd never does. (4) Pre-flight validator flags any unknown mcp_tools field or unresolvable command, and surfaces a requiredEnv-style NAME-only warning for any authTokenEnv. Explicitly NOT adopted: in-process launch/registration, descriptions-auto-surfacing into a live LLM, and the always-on org-repo default-tools.md (no server-pushed fleet defaults; at most a per-profile/fleet-default opt-in marker reusing L1's existing fleet-default scope).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT(high)/reinforces-L1 ruling is sound. I verified against the actual repo: the skills enumerator over ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md, the cc-mcp.json MCP registry, the mcp-config-file builder, the config-by-reference resolver, name sanitization, and requiredEnv NAME-only handling are ALL part of the L1 plan in todos.md (lines 242-301) — today's shipped spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) only emits --permission-mode/--remote-control, and there is no listSkills/SKILL.md parser or MCP builder in the code yet. So this feature lands squarely on L1's planned primitives.\n\nRunning the common-error checklist: (a) No in-process executor/sandbox. Stripped to its core, the feature is a declarative idea — a skill records the MCP servers it depends on, so selecting it carries the tooling along. That is a parse-and-emit transform; native Claude Code owns the actual MCP launch/registration once the --mcp-config file exists (claude CLI confirmed present at ~/.local/bin/claude with --mcp-config, todos.md:224-225). The 'auto-registers tools' / 'descriptions auto-surface into a live LLM' half IS the executor behavior, and the judge explicitly does NOT adopt it. (b) No server/multi-user/secrets: any authTokenEnv stays a NAME resolved via the spawned login shell, never a stored value — consistent with scope limits and L1's requiredEnv design; the always-on org-pushed default-tools.md (the only org/fleet-push smell) is explicitly excluded. (c) Not pure-redundant and not a parallel system: the marginal delta over plain L1 is ONE optional frontmatter field (mcp_tools.stdio_servers {name,command,args}) that auto-couples skill->servers instead of hand-attaching both on a profile — a modest but real enrichment routed through L1's existing enumerator -> config-by-ref resolver -> mcp-config-file builder, with the planned [A-Za-z0-9._-] name sanitization. That is why ADD->ADAPT is correct rather than its own backlog item. (d) Not library plumbing mistaken for a console feature: the frontmatter parse+emit is genuinely console-side; the register/surface loop is the library half and is correctly excised.\n\nIt genuinely improves the LOCAL single-user console (a saved skill carries its MCP dependency so 'spawn from agent' emits the right --mcp-config automatically), and it directly serves the user's #1 stated priority (pre-configured agents + skills + MCPs saved to a directory). No common-error category survives. Ruling holds: ADAPT(high), reinforces L1, with the executor/server/always-on halves dropped.

#### Skills inventory / search endpoint (list with name+type+source+triggers, paginated; toggle list)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is a near-exact match for the user's #1 stated interest ("skills saved into a directory") and is one of the few OpenHands features that sits squarely in the local model with NO double-mismatch contamination. The implementation is pure disk-reading: rglob two markdown dirs, parse YAML frontmatter, return {name, type, source, triggers}. It touches no executor, no sandbox, no server-as-value, no users, no stored secrets — the comment "lives under the user router but reads disk, not the DB" is the tell that the VALUE is the disk enumeration, not the web plumbing. Shepherd already reads `~/.claude/*` off the local filesystem as its core idiom, and Claude Code's real skills live at exactly `~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` with frontmatter. The "render a toggle list" intent maps cleanly onto a panel skills card / per-profile skill picker. Notably this candidate adds two fields beyond what L1's one-line "Skills (read-mostly)" card spells out — a `type` taxonomy (knowledge/repo/task/agentskills) and a `triggers` list — which is genuinely useful prior art: triggers tell the human WHEN a skill auto-activates (matters for steering), and type lets the card group/filter. Cheap, safe, directly serves spawn-from-agent skill attachment.
- **Against:** Most of the candidate's literal surface area is structurally inapplicable to Shepherd and must be discarded: the FastAPI `/skills/search` endpoint, the `SkillPage` HTTP DTO, and especially the cursor pagination (`next_page_id`) exist only because OpenHands is a multi-tenant web server streaming over the network — Shepherd has no server, renders in an embedded webview reading Lua tables, and a single user's local skills directory is tens of entries, not a paginated corpus. OpenHands' two-dir split (global + user) and its global-first sort are an artifact of its packaged-skill vs user-skill distinction; Claude Code's local model is a single `~/.claude/skills` tree, so the source/sort dimension is partly vestigial here. And the feature in isolation is inert: a read-only inventory list has no value unless it feeds an action — which is precisely why it can't stand alone as ADD and must fold into L1's spawn-from-agent skill attachment.
- **Ruling:** The OpenHands double mismatch does NOT bite the core here: the feature's value is disk enumeration + frontmatter parsing, which Shepherd structurally CAN host (it already reads ~/.claude/* off local disk). The mismatch only bites the delivery shell — FastAPI route, HTTP page DTO, cursor pagination — all of which is server plumbing to strip. What survives is exactly L1's planned "Skills (read-mostly): pure enumerator over ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md parsing frontmatter -> card view via a new FX.listSkills." So this is not a new system; it corroborates and slightly enriches an already-queued item. Verdict is ADAPT (not ADD) because (1) it must shed the server/pagination shell and land as pure cc-core + one FX file-read, and (2) a bare inventory is only valuable when wired to L1's skill-attachment/spawn-from-agent path. It is not DONT_ADD: no executor/sandbox/auth/secret is required to enumerate local skill markdown. The candidate's `type` and `triggers` fields are a concrete improvement to fold into L1's skills card (L1 currently only names "frontmatter -> card"). Confidence high: this is the cleanest local-model fit among OpenHands features and matches the user's explicit top priority.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's "Skills (read-mostly)" card. Add a pure cc-core enumerator `M.skillList(raw)` (sibling to `presetList`:3658 / `providerById`:2632, zero hs.*) that takes file contents and returns sorted records `{name, type, source, triggers}`, plus a pure `M.parseSkillFrontmatter(text)` (a minimal YAML-frontmatter reader for name/description/triggers — no external yaml dep). The side effect is one new `FX.listSkills` that rglobs `~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md` (and optionally a user/project skills dir) and feeds raw text to the pure fn — mirroring how FX.loadLabels/listDirs feed pure core. Surface as an off-by-default skills card in the panel (the webview "toggle list" intent), and as the multi-select skill picker in the New Session / spawn-from-agent modal whose checked names populate profile.skills[] -> resolved by L1's config-by-ref resolver -> emitted as --append-system-prompt/skill flags by spawnFlags:3001 (gated by spawn.live). DROP: the FastAPI route, SkillPage HTTP DTO, and cursor pagination (next_page_id) — local list is small; render in full. Adopt the `triggers` field (show on card so the human knows when a skill auto-activates) and the `type` taxonomy (group/filter the card). Reuse the [A-Za-z0-9._-] name sanitization from L1's safe-path hardening when a skill name becomes a spawn flag.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted on all four common-error axes; none lands, so the ADAPT ruling holds. (a) Executor/sandbox? No — the feature's surviving value is pure local-disk enumeration + YAML frontmatter parsing. Verified the existing FX file-read pattern (claude-dashboard.lua:696 FX.listDirs, :498 FX.loadLabels) is plain hs.fs reads feeding pure cc-core; no agent loop, no Docker. Skill EXECUTION is owned by native Claude Code via --append-system-prompt/--agent flags (spawnFlags at cc-core.lua:3001), never by Shepherd. (b) Auth/secrets/server? The candidate's FastAPI route + SkillPage HTTP DTO + cursor pagination ARE server plumbing — but the ADAPT mapping correctly DROPS all three; the surviving disk-scan + parse needs no auth, no secret, no network. (c) Redundant? This was the strongest refutation lever and it backfires for DONT_ADD: grep confirms NO skillList/listSkills/parseSkillFrontmatter exists in cc-core.lua today, so it is NOT already shipped — but it IS explicitly QUEUED at todos.md:261-262 ('Skills (read-mostly): pure enumerator over ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md parsing frontmatter -> card view via a new FX.listSkills'). Per the task's own rule, matching a queued Lx -> ADAPT (reinforces L1), not a parallel new system, and not DONT_ADD. (d) Plumbing-mistaken-for-feature? Only the endpoint framing is plumbing; the underlying toggle-list card feeding profile.skills[] is a genuine local-console feature and matches the user's stated #1 priority (saved agents+skills+MCPs). Cannot push to ADD because it duplicates queued L1; cannot push to DONT_ADD because no executor/sandbox/auth/secret is required and it is unbuilt. #### Slash-command autocomplete menu in chat input (typeahead picker over skills/commands)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** Shepherd already injects keystrokes into sessions (nudge text, /model, /effort, /clear, /compact, queued tasks, templates via "Tpl ▾"). A typeahead over saved/available commands and skills would speed up that injection: type "/" in the nudge box and pick from skills found in ~/.claude/skills (or a saved-agent's skills[]) plus Shepherd's own built-in injectables (/model, /effort, mode switch, clear, compact). The single most reusable sub-piece — getSkillDescription parsing frontmatter `description:` or the first stripped body line from a skills/*.md library — is precisely the "skills card from ~/.claude/skills" already queued in L1 and the user's stated priority ("skills saved into a directory"). Building that description-extraction reader once feeds both the L1 skills card and any picker. The UI shell (filtered dropdown, name+description rows, keyboard nav) is a near-clone of patterns Shepherd already ships, so the increment is small.
- **Against:** The candidate's actual value is autocomplete for a CHAT INPUT that drives an agent — and Shepherd has no such input. The slash commands it fronts (built-in chat commands + skill triggers + AgentSkill /<name>) are interpreted INSIDE the running Claude Code session, which Claude Code itself owns and already provides a far better native "/" autocomplete for (live, context-aware, in the real prompt). Shepherd's nudge box is a fire-and-inject field, not the agent's prompt; duplicating Claude Code's own slash picker there is redundant and would drift out of sync with what the session actually supports. Worse, the BUILT_IN_COMMANDS list and AgentSkills enumeration in OpenHands reflect ITS executor/skill runtime; Shepherd can only know what `~/.claude/skills` and the `claude` CLI flags expose, not what a given session will accept. As pure UI sugar over an input Shepherd doesn't conceptually own, the standalone feature adds maintenance for little watch/steer/spawn value.
- **Ruling:** No double-mismatch blocker: it needs no executor, sandbox, server, users, or stored secrets. But the feature as-specified is autocomplete for an agent chat prompt, and Shepherd is not a chat client — Claude Code owns the live session prompt and already offers native, context-aware "/" autocomplete there. So building the picker as-is (ADD) would duplicate native behavior on an input (the nudge/inject box) Shepherd doesn't conceptually own, with no way to know what a session will actually accept. That kills ADD. It is not redundant enough to be DONT_ADD, though: the genuinely transferable core — a reader that lists ~/.claude/skills/*.md and extracts each one's description from frontmatter `description:` or the first meaningful, markdown-stripped body line — is real, fits the local filesystem model, matches the user's explicit "saved skills in a directory" priority, and is already the "skills card from ~/.claude/skills" line item under L1 (Agent Profiles registry). Hence ADAPT/reinforces L1: keep the description-extraction logic, drop the chat-input autocomplete framing. The UI scaffolding it would need already exists twice (the #tpl-menu name+text picker and the #n-suggest fuzzy folder picker with ArrowDown/ArrowUp/Enter keyboard nav over core.fuzzyFilter), so no new menu/keyboard machinery is warranted — reuse those.
- **How (Shepherd):** Take only the skill-description extractor and fold it into L1's "skills card from ~/.claude/skills." Add a pure cc-core function (sibling to presetList/templateList/fuzzyFilter) — e.g. core.parseSkillCard(text) -> {name, description} that reads YAML frontmatter `description:` if present, else the first non-blank, non-heading body line, with a small core.stripMarkdown (drop `#`, `*`, `_`, backticks, link syntax). An FX reader enumerates ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md (and ~/.claude/skills/*.md) once per modal open, like the folderIndex cache; results render in the L1 agent-profile editor's skills card (and, if wanted later, as filterable rows in the existing #tpl-menu-style dropdown using core.fuzzyFilter). If a lightweight "/" picker is ever desired in the nudge box, reuse the #n-suggest keyboard-nav handler (onPathKey: ArrowDown/ArrowUp/Enter/Escape at claude-dashboard.lua:4083) and the #tpl-menu render pattern (claude-dashboard.lua:3462) verbatim — no new menu component. Inject only Shepherd-known commands (/model, /effort, mode, clear, compact) plus skill names from the directory; never invent an executor-side BUILT_IN_COMMANDS list.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt failed; ADAPT(reinforces L1) holds. (1) No double mismatch: the feature needs no in-process executor, no sandbox/runtime, no server, no multi-user auth, no stored secrets — it's pure local-filesystem reads of ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md plus string parsing, which fits Shepherd's model. So neither DONT_ADD-by-double-mismatch nor a server/library-plumbing dismissal applies. (2) It is NOT fully redundant, so DONT_ADD overall is wrong: the genuinely transferable half — extract each skill's description from frontmatter `description:` or the first markdown-stripped body line — is a real, queued line item. todos.md:261-262 already specifies L1's "Skills (read-mostly): pure enumerator over ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md parsing frontmatter → card view via a new FX.listSkills." The feature's parser IS that parser. (3) ADD is correctly killed (not upgradable): the chat-input autocomplete framing duplicates native behavior on an input Shepherd doesn't own. Verified at claude-dashboard.lua:1290-1292 — when Shepherd types a "/"-prefixed string into the nudge box (#nudge, line 3104), the Claude Code EXTENSION's own slash autocomplete popup opens in the live session (Shepherd even sends a 2nd Return just to dismiss it). Claude Code owns the live prompt's context-aware "/" menu; a Shepherd-side picker over "built-in commands" would shadow it and would have to GUESS the runtime's command set, with no executor-side knowledge of what the session accepts. That command-autocomplete half is DONT_ADD, exactly as the judge ruled. Net: keep the description-extraction core, fold it into L1's skills card, drop the chat-input autocomplete — which is precisely the ADAPT verdict. Mapping is also accurate: the proposed cc-core siblings (parseSkillCard/stripMarkdown next to presetList:3658/templateList:3590/fuzzyFilter:3502) are sound, and the reusable UI scaffolding the judge cites exists verbatim — the #tpl-menu name+text render pattern (claude-dashboard.lua:3462) and the #n-suggest fuzzy picker with ArrowDown/ArrowUp/Enter/Escape keyboard nav (onPathKey, claude-dashboard.lua:4083) — so no new menu/keyboard machinery is warranted. I could find no path to downgrade to DONT_ADD without discarding a feature half that is already on the L1 roadmap and matches the user's #1 stated priority ("agents/skills/MCPs saved into a directory").

#### Per-user/per-profile skill enable/disable opt-out (disabled_skills) + skills settings toggle list  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is the user's stated #1 priority territory: "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs." OpenHands' skills library + a per-profile opt-out is direct prior art for the management/UI layer Shepherd wants. L1 already commits to a skills card enumerating ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md (frontmatter name/display_title/description) and a per-profile skills[] selection. The OpenHands feature contributes two concrete, transferable UX patterns on top of that: (1) the settings/profile screen renders per-skill rows with a toggle plus trigger + source/type subtext — exactly the card-row layout L1 needs and a small enrichment to "show role/goal on the card for scannability"; (2) the notion that a profile carries a curated skill set without deleting the underlying skill folder, which maps cleanly onto L1's skills[] being a per-agent-profile selection. It is pure-data + read-only enumeration + JSON list mutation — no server, no sandbox, no secrets — so it sits squarely in the transferable MANAGEMENT/SKILLS layer the brief flags as plausible.
- **Against:** The feature's actual MECHANISM does not exist in Shepherd and cannot. In OpenHands the value is: the conversation service LOADS ALL skills then FILTERS OUT disabled-named ones before ATTACHING them to the in-process agent. Shepherd runs no agent loop and attaches no skills — native Claude Code itself auto-loads every ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md for a session, and Shepherd has no hook to intercept or filter that load. So a literal disabled_skills filter has nothing to filter: there is no Shepherd-owned "all skills loaded" step to subtract from. Worse, disabled_skills is persisted on a multi-user Settings object and toggled via useSaveSettings — the per-user web-settings plumbing is exactly the multi-tenant half of the double mismatch (DONT_ADD territory). The only place a skill selection has teeth in Shepherd is at SPAWN, where flags are emitted — and there the natural primitive is a POSITIVE per-profile skills[] allow-list (already L1), not a negative global disabled_skills list. A standalone global "turn this skill off everywhere" toggle would be redundant with, and weaker than, per-profile selection, and it cannot affect already-running sessions (which is most of what Shepherd watches).
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: the candidate is half transferable (a skills library + a settings toggle list = the MANAGEMENT/UI layer) and half structurally impossible (filter-before-attach to an in-process agent + per-user persisted Settings). The transferable half is already absorbed by L1's planned skills card and per-profile skills[] selection. The right move is therefore ADAPT/reinforce L1, not ADD a parallel disabled_skills system. Two reshapings make it concrete: (a) the per-skill toggle-row UI with trigger + source/type subtext becomes the row design for L1's skills card and the per-profile skill picker in the New-Session/agent modal; (b) the opt-out semantic flips from a negative global list to a positive per-profile skills[] (Shepherd's primitive), since Shepherd emits spawn flags rather than attaching to a loop and cannot retro-filter the native auto-loaded skill set. Note also Shepherd cannot honestly replicate "skill is off but not deleted everywhere" because native Claude Code auto-loads all skill folders regardless — so a global disable would be misleading. Keep it scoped to spawn-time profile selection where Shepherd actually has leverage. Do NOT port the Settings-object persistence/useSaveSettings path; per L1's facts, registries like cc-agents.json live OUTSIDE the Settings round-trip as operator JSON. Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L1; this is an enrichment, not new scope.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 (Agent Profiles registry). Concretely: (1) The skills card (pure enumerator over ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md via FX.listSkills) renders each skill as a toggle-row showing name/display_title + the trigger/description and source/type as subtext — adopt OpenHands' row layout. (2) Each agent profile's skills[] is the per-profile selection; the toggle on the card, when used inside the profile editor, adds/removes a skill from that profile's skills[] (positive selection), which spawnFlags threads as --append-system-prompt skill refs at spawn (gated by spawn.live). (3) Reuse L1's config-by-reference resolver + safe-path hardening ([A-Za-z0-9._-], reject names resolving outside ~/.claude/skills) to validate the selected skill names before emitting flags. (4) If a fleet-default "don't surface this skill in pickers" convenience is wanted, store it as a hidden/deprioritized flag on operator JSON (cc-agents.json sidecar), NOT on the Settings round-trip, and label it honestly as picker-scope only (it cannot disable native auto-load on a live session). Do NOT build a global disabled_skills Settings field or a useSaveSettings-style persistence path.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute the ADAPT (reinforces L1) and could not. The feature is the OpenHands double-mismatch in miniature: half (a) is structurally impossible and half (b) is a genuine local UI enrichment of an already-queued Shepherd item. REFUTATION ATTEMPT 1 — push to DONT_ADD via "secretly needs an in-process executor": The literal mechanism is "load all skills into the running agent's context, then filter out names in disabled_skills BEFORE attaching to the agent loop." Shepherd runs no agent loop and attaches to nothing — it emits spawn flags. I VERIFIED the only native disable knob: /Users/adam/.local/bin/claude --help shows just `--disable-slash-commands` ("Disable all skills") — all-or-nothing, NO per-skill selective filter; skills auto-resolve from ~/.claude/skills (confirmed: deno-fresh2, rune present, frontmatter name/description as documented). So a global disabled_skills list would be a DISHONEST UI in Shepherd: the spawned session still auto-loads the "disabled" folder. That kills half (a) — and the judge ALREADY drops it ("Do NOT build a global disabled_skills Settings field; Shepherd cannot honestly replicate skill-off-but-not-deleted"). But it does not kill the whole feature. REFUTATION ATTEMPT 2 — push to DONT_ADD via "needs Settings-object persistence / multi-user": disabled_skills lives in the per-user Settings round-trip (useSaveSettings). Shepherd's registries (cc-agents.json) live OUTSIDE the Settings round-trip as operator JSON (todos.md:237). The judge explicitly forbids porting that path. With it dropped, the residue needs no server, no users, no secrets. Failure mode disarmed, not fallen into. REFUTATION ATTEMPT 3 — push to ADD or "redundant→DONT_ADD" via L1 overlap: grep confirms ZERO skill handling in cc-core.lua/claude-dashboard.lua today, but L1 ALREADY plans (todos.md:261-262, 290-293, 297-298) the skills card (FX.listSkills enumerator over ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md), the positive per-profile skills[] selection, the config-by-reference resolver, and [A-Za-z0-9._-] safe-path hardening. So the feature introduces NO new scope — it reinforces L1, which is the definition of ADAPT-not-ADD. The transferable residue (the toggle-ROW layout: name/display_title + trigger + source/type subtext, and toggle = add/remove from a profile's skills[]) is a concrete UI enrichment of L1's already-queued card, with the semantic correctly flipped from a negative global list to Shepherd's positive per-profile primitive emitted as --append-system-prompt refs at spawn (gated by spawn.live). All three errors checked: (a) in-process executor — kills the literal filter, judge dropped it; (b) Settings/multi-user persistence — judge dropped it; (c) redundant/queued — it folds into L1, which is exactly why it's ADAPT(reinforces L1) and not ADD. Cannot reach DONT_ADD (an honest local skills-card UI survives) and cannot reach ADD (no new scope). Ruling holds at ADAPT, reinforces L1. #### Glossary / documentation repo-microagent (shared domain vocabulary + doc-citation rules)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** A shared domain vocabulary genuinely improves a fleet of sessions: if every spawned Claude Code session in a project speaks the same terms and follows the same doc-citation rules, output is more consistent and reviewable — and that benefit is real even though Shepherd doesn't run the loop, because Claude Code (which Shepherd spawns) DOES inject `--add-dir` / `--append-system-prompt` content into its own context. This is also a clean instance of the user's #1 stated interest: declarative, file-on-disk knowledge attached to saved agents. The local 'claude' CLI explicitly supports `--add-dir` and `--append-system-prompt`, so wiring a glossary.md/documentation.md as an attachable knowledge file at spawn is mechanically feasible today and needs no server, sandbox, or secrets.
- **Against:** There is nothing here that L1 doesn't already cover, and the autonomous part — auto-injecting the glossary into the agent's running context on every relevant turn, OpenHands' actual mechanism — depends on the in-process agent loop Shepherd structurally lacks. The documentation.md "cite-every-fact" half is a trigger-keyword knowledge microagent that fires mid-loop on words like "document"; Shepherd has no loop to fire in. And curating project terminology is squarely what a repo's own CLAUDE.md is for — the pad-mirror batch already DECLINED "durable conventions + notes" as "domain creep or duplicates of CLAUDE.md + the gate" (todos.md:150-151). Building a glossary-specific feature would re-add exactly that declined surface. Shepherd should attach files, not own a vocabulary.
- **Ruling:** Double mismatch: OpenHands' value here is auto-injecting the glossary/doc-rules into the in-process agent's context (executor side) and serving it across a repo for contributors (server/multi-user side). Shepherd has neither. BUT the underlying need — "ship a curated knowledge file into every session of a project so they share terminology" — maps cleanly onto a primitive Shepherd has ALREADY specced: L1's `knowledge[]` (todos.md:279-281, "validated local paths → `--add-dir` at spawn (+ small inline refs via `--append-system-prompt`); fleet-default vs per-profile scope. Claude Code does the retrieval — NO embedder/vector store/chunking") and the per-agent `contextDir`/`memoryFile` field (todos.md:299-300, "Static, human/Claude-edited — NO embeddings, NO recall, NO in-loop mutation"). A glossary.md is a textbook `knowledge[]` entry; a documentation.md is the same. So this is not a new feature — it is one concrete use of L1's knowledge attachment, already designed with the fleet-default scope this candidate wants. The documentation.md trigger-keyword behavior is the only genuinely non-mappable piece, and it is exactly the in-loop autonomous injection the mismatch rules out — drop it; a static doc-standards note attached via `--append-system-prompt` is the local equivalent and is also already L1. Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L1; do not build a glossary-specific subsystem (that would re-add the CLAUDE.md-duplicate surface the pad batch declined at todos.md:151). The only marginal enrichment is a project-root fleet-default knowledge file (attach a project's glossary to every session spawned into that folder), but L1's `knowledge[]` already names "fleet-default vs per-profile scope," so even that is covered.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 (Agent Profiles registry), specifically the existing `knowledge[]` field (todos.md:279) and per-agent `contextDir`/`memoryFile` (todos.md:299): a glossary.md / documentation.md is just a validated local path added to a profile's `knowledge[]` (or a project-scoped fleet-default knowledge path), threaded by `spawnFlags` (cc-core.lua:3001) into `--add-dir` for the file plus a short inline term-list / doc-standards block into `--append-system-prompt`. Path goes through L1's safe-path resolver ([A-Za-z0-9._-], reject-outside-root, todos.md:297) and pre-flight validation (todos.md:285) so a missing glossary refuses spawn with a clear error. No new schema field, no new system, no per-turn injection. Curation of the actual terms stays in the repo's CLAUDE.md / the file itself (human/Claude-edited), consistent with the pad-batch decision; Shepherd only attaches it.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; the ADAPT/reinforces-L1 ruling holds, and every citation checks out against the repo. todos.md:279-280 defines `knowledge[]` = "validated local paths → --add-dir at spawn (+ small inline refs via --append-system-prompt); fleet-default vs per-profile scope. Claude Code does the retrieval — NO embedder/vector store/chunking," and todos.md:299-300 defines per-agent `contextDir`/`memoryFile` as "Static, human/Claude-edited — NO embeddings, NO recall, NO in-loop mutation." A glossary.md is a textbook `knowledge[]` path, and the fleet-default scope this feature wants is named verbatim in L1. The feature's OpenHands value splits exactly along the double mismatch: (a) auto-injecting the file into the IN-PROCESS agent's context and the documentation.md trigger-keyword (knowledge-microagent) mid-loop activation are the executor side — Shepherd runs no loop and only reads status JSON, so it cannot watch turns and inject on keyword match; the judge correctly DROPS this. (b) "serve the glossary across a repo for contributors" is the server/multi-user side — also dropped. What survives after stripping both halves is purely "ship a curated static file into every session spawned into a project," which is one concrete use of L1's already-designed `knowledge[]`/`contextDir` field threaded through spawnFlags into --add-dir; Claude Code does the retrieval, so no loop is needed and the residue is genuinely local + useful. I could only refute by showing the residue is full ADD (it isn't — it lands on an existing queued primitive, no new schema field) or DONT_ADD (it isn't — the static-file attachment has real local value). The judge even pre-empts the strongest DONT_ADD angle: the pad-batch declined "durable conventions/notes/playbooks as CLAUDE.md duplicates" (todos.md:151), and correctly distinguishes building a glossary-specific SUBSYSTEM (which would re-add that declined surface) from merely ATTACHING the file via the existing `knowledge[]` path (allowed). One caveat: spawnFlags at cc-core.lua:3001 currently emits only --permission-mode/--remote-control; the --add-dir/--append-system-prompt wiring is L1 DESIGN, not yet shipped — but that is consistent with L1 being queued, not built, and does not change the verdict.

#### Send a follow-up message / nudge an existing session (state-gated error vocabulary)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The transferable kernel is the STATE-GATED ERROR VOCABULARY, and Shepherd already half-built it but surfaces it poorly. Shepherd's `nudge` action (cc-core.lua handleAction) pastes-and-submits text into a running session — exactly "send a follow-up message" minus the HTTP/sandbox. It even has a delivery contract: the `delivered` helper distinguishes "positively not delivered" (no-window-match guard) from success, and `queueFeedCommit` emits `task_feed` vs `task_feed_skipped`. But today those failures vanish into a log line — the operator who clicks "nudge" on a tile gets no structured reason why it didn't land. OpenHands' insight is that "send to a conversation" has a small, knowable failure taxonomy keyed on lifecycle state, and the caller deserves a precise, actionable reason. Shepherd has the raw signals to compute the equivalent reasons WITHOUT any server: 404→tile gone/removeStatus; 409 "sandbox not running, resume hint"→stale tile or dead window (give the operator a relaunch/respawn hint instead of a silent drop); 410 gone→archived/closed session; 503 error-state→the magenta `error` status (frozen API error — the natural reason is "resume with continue first"). Adapting nudge/queue-feed to return one of these reasons (and showing it on the tile) turns silent no-ops into honest, steer-able feedback — pure-local, fits the watch/steer console, and dovetails with L1's pre-flight validation (validate the target is nudge-able before dispatching).
- **Against:** As specified, the feature is almost entirely server/sandbox plumbing Shepherd structurally cannot and should not host: a POST HTTP endpoint, a sandbox-status gate, and a proxy that forwards role+content+run to an agent-server `/events` endpoint — the in-process executor's event bus. The 409 "sandbox not running" and 503 "unreachable" codes are literally about a managed Docker/remote sandbox's lifecycle, which Shepherd has no concept of. The image-content forwarding and "optionally auto-run the agent" run-flag presuppose an agent loop Shepherd does not drive (Claude Code owns execution; nudge just pastes keystrokes). And the core capability — send text to a running session — is already shipped as `nudge` plus the queue feed path, so copying it as-is would be redundant. There's a real risk of over-engineering: minting a four-code error taxonomy onto a Hammerspoon keystroke-injector could be gold-plating a clipboard paste. The genuine delivery-failure surface Shepherd faces is narrow (no window match, stale tile, dead kitty target), not the rich HTTP lifecycle OpenHands models.
- **Ruling:** Double mismatch applies hard to the WRAPPER (HTTP POST, sandbox-status gate, agent-server /events proxy, run-the-agent flag) — all DONT_ADD plumbing. But the PATTERN underneath ("sending into a running conversation has a small, lifecycle-keyed failure taxonomy the operator should see, not silently swallow") is real and Shepherd is the natural home for it: it already has nudge, the `delivered` contract, `BULK_RULES` status-gating, status states (idle/working/done/approval/error), stale detection, and `task_feed_skipped` events. The capability exists; what's missing is turning silent drops into a structured, tile-visible reason with a hint (resume/relaunch). That's a reshape onto existing primitives = ADAPT, not a new system and not redundant. It reinforces L1's pre-flight validation (check nudge-ability before dispatch) rather than spawning a parallel mechanism. Not DONT_ADD because the value does NOT depend on the executor/sandbox/server/users/secrets — it depends only on local status files Shepherd already reads.
- **How (Shepherd):** In cc-core.lua, extend the nudge/queue-feed delivery path from a boolean `delivered` into a small reason enum computed from the target's existing status: nudgeReason(item) → ok | gone (no tile / removed) | not_running (stale or dead window, hint: relaunch/respawn) | error_state (item.status=="error", hint: send `continue` first) | wrong_state (sitting at an approval prompt — already excluded by BULK_RULES nudge.exclude). handleAction's nudge branch and queueFeedCommit return that reason instead of nil/false; the dashboard surfaces it on the tile (a toast/badge: "nudge not delivered — session frozen on API error, resume first") and ledgers it (extends the existing `task_feed_skipped` audit event with a reason field). No HTTP, no run-flag (nudge already pastes-and-submits = send-and-run; queue feed is the send-into-free-session variant), no sandbox. Pairs with L1 pre-flight validation: same reason resolver gates "is this target nudge-able?" before a batch fan-out.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; the ADAPT(L1) ruling holds. I verified every primitive the judge cites is real in the code, and that the DONT_ADD escape hatches do NOT apply. WHAT I CHECKED (cc-core.lua / claude-dashboard.lua): - `delivered` contract: cc-core.lua:104-115 (boolean "positively not delivered" guard, shared by nudge paste + set-mode). - nudge action: cc-core.lua:138-148 — on a skipped paste it returns nil and the caller never records it. - queueFeedCommit / task_feed_skipped: cc-core.lua:1960-1968 — a skip yields `{persist=false, event="task_feed_skipped"}`. - BULK_RULES.nudge.exclude="approval": cc-core.lua:395-399 (the wrong_state exclusion already exists). - status states incl. `error`: cc-core.lua:1479; stale detection cc-core.lua:73; respawnSpec already returns a surfaceable `reason` (cc-core.lua:2687-2700). - Current operator-visible behavior on a skipped feed (claude-dashboard.lua:1814-1822): a console `print("[cc-queue] feed skipped (no window match)")` + a BARE `task_feed_skipped` ledger event with NO reason field, NO tile toast/badge, NO hint. So the surfacing layer is genuinely missing, not redundant. REFUTATION DOES NOT LAND: (a) executor/sandbox: N/A. The reason enum is computed purely from local status files Shepherd already reads (item.stale, item.status=="error", missing tile, BULK_RULES exclusion). Nudge is keyboard paste-and-submit into an already-running GUI session — no agent loop, no sandbox, no runtime. The OpenHands 409 "sandbox not running"/410/503 codes are dropped; only their LOCAL analogues (not_running via stale, error_state via status=="error", gone via removed tile) survive, all derivable today. (b) server/multi-user/secrets: N/A. The whole OpenHands WRAPPER (POST /{id}/send-message, agent-server /events proxy, run-the-agent flag) is correctly DONT_ADD plumbing — the judge already strips all of it. Nudge=send-and-run and queue-feed=send-into-free-session are the existing local equivalents; no HTTP, no auth, no stored secrets. (c) redundant/queued: NO. The capability is half-built: delivery is a boolean that drops silently to console + a reasonless ledger event. The added value (structured reason {ok|gone|not_running|error_state|wrong_state} + resume/relaunch hint, surfaced on the tile, reason field added to task_feed_skipped) does not exist. todos.md L1 explicitly lists "pre-flight validation" as a queued item this reason-resolver reinforces (gate nudge-ability before a batch fan-out) — so it folds into L1 rather than spawning a parallel system. todos.md:393 also confirms "the conversational/continue half is already the live nudge textarea + per-session queue," matching the send-and-run mapping. (d) library/plumbing-as-feature: NO. The surfaced reason+hint is an end-user console affordance (tile toast/badge), exactly Shepherd's human-in-the-loop reason to exist. NET: ADD is wrongly over-scoped (the HTTP/sandbox wrapper must be dropped), and DONT_ADD is wrong (value is independent of executor/server/secrets AND the surfacing/hint layer is genuinely absent). The reshape onto existing primitives reinforcing L1 = ADAPT, exactly as the prior judge ruled.

#### Read-only preset/profile registry (list/get/batch/default, paginated, with a marked default)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The feature's real value is a read-only registry-access SHAPE over a list of spawn templates — search/get(id)/batch_get([ids])/get_default() with one marked default. Stripped of the word "sandbox," that is exactly Shepherd's preset/agent-profile primitive seen through its read operations. It directly reinforces L1 (Agent Profiles registry, cc-agents.json), whose CRUD already mirrors presetPush/presetList (cc-core.lua ~3658-3722). It also contributes two read-side affordances L1 currently under-specifies: (1) get_default() — a single MARKED default profile so the New-Session modal can pre-select a sensible agent instead of nothing, and the batch fan-out spawn / project-routing dispatcher can resolve a fleet default; (2) batch_get([ids]) — resolve a NAMED SET of profiles in one call, which is the natural backbone for L1's "batch fan-out spawn" (pick N profiles → spawn one session each) and for the L4 routing topology resolving role-addressed members. The get-by-id pattern is already half-built (providerById:2632, resolveGateTools:2537, presetForProject) so it's cheap, pure cc-core, zero hs.*, and unit-testable.
- **Against:** Nearly all of this is already specified or shipped. Shepherd has the preset registry (presetList/presetPush/presetRemove + presetForProject for per-project recall) and L1 already declares agentList/agentPush/agentRemove plus a config-by-reference resolver that dereferences names — i.e. get(id) by another name. Adding a formal abstract "service" interface would be ceremony Shepherd's flat-Lua, fx-funnel architecture doesn't want. The two genuinely new bits are small: get_default() is a one-field marker (default = true on a profile, or "first in list" exactly as OpenHands does), and batch_get is a trivial filter over agentList — neither warrants its own design. Worse, the headline affordances are a poor fit for the local single-user model: PAGINATION over an in-memory list of a handful of profiles is pure server-product baggage (OpenHands paginates because it serves multi-tenant lists over HTTP); a macOS panel just renders the whole list. The abstract-base/concrete-PresetService split exists in OpenHands so the SaaS can swap in a DB-backed multi-tenant implementation — exactly the (b) multi-user-server half of the double mismatch, with no analog here. So the transferable delta is ~one boolean and one list filter, not a feature.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: "sandbox spec" names a Docker/remote image to RUN an agent loop in (the (a) executor/sandbox half), and the abstract-base/concrete split + pagination exist to let the SaaS serve a multi-tenant list over HTTP (the (b) server half). Both of those are DONT_ADD on their own. But the PATTERN being judged is the management-layer read API: a registry of spawn templates with list/get/batch/default. That is squarely a SKILLS/registry pattern, and it lands on Shepherd's strongest existing primitive (presets) and its #1 queued item (L1 Agent Profiles), which the user explicitly wants ("pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory"). So it cannot be DONT_ADD. It is also not ADD-as-is: pagination and the abstract service interface are server baggage with no local value, and get/list already exist. The honest verdict is ADAPT into L1, harvesting precisely two things L1 omitted: a marked-default profile (get_default) and batch_get([ids]) as the resolver backbone for L1's batch fan-out spawn (and later L4 role-addressing). Drop pagination and the abstract-service ceremony.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 (cc-agents.json). (1) Add an isDefault boolean to the agent-profile schema and a pure core.agentDefault(state) helper that returns the marked profile or falls back to the first in agentList (OpenHands' specs[0] semantics) — used by the New-Session modal to pre-select, and by the routing dispatcher / batch fan-out to resolve a fleet default; surface a "set as default" toggle on the profile card. (2) Add core.agentBatchGet(state, names[]) — a pure filter over agentList returning the resolved profiles (skip/flag unknown names) — and wire it as the input to L1's already-queued "batch fan-out spawn" (pick a named set of profiles → render template → spawn one session each, by:"batch" ledger event). get(id) is already covered by the config-by-reference resolver (extends providerById:2632 / resolveGateTools:2537 / presetForProject:3720). DROP: pagination, the abstract SandboxSpecService base + concrete-impl split, and the SandboxError-if-empty (a local empty list just shows the empty-state modal). All pure cc-core, fx for any reads, unit-tested like presetList.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails on every common-error axis; ADAPT reinforcing L1 is correct. (a) Executor/sandbox: "sandbox spec" names a Docker image to RUN an agent in, but the PATTERN judged is only the read API (list/get/batch/default) over a template list — the adapted form (an isDefault flag + a pure agentBatchGet filter over cc-agents.json) runs no agent loop and manages no sandbox; the judge already dropped the sandbox half. (b) Server/secrets/multi-user: pagination, the abstract SandboxSpecService base + concrete split, and SandboxError-if-empty are exactly the multi-tenant-HTTP baggage — and the judge explicitly DROPS all three; the two harvested bits carry no server/secrets dependency. (c) Redundancy — the strongest refutation angle — does NOT hold: I verified get(id) is already M.providerById (cc-core.lua:2632) and list is M.presetList (cc-core.lua:3658), and the judge correctly declines to re-add them. The two harvested bits are genuinely NOT in L1: grepping todos.md shows L1 has a default PROVIDER (spawn.provider, cc-core.lua:2649) and a default POLICY BUNDLE, but NO marked-default-PROFILE concept (no get_default / isDefault / pre-select anywhere), and L1's queued 'batch fan-out spawn' (todos.md:294) specifies no agentBatchGet([names]) resolver as its input. So get_default (marked default → pre-select in the New-Session modal; OpenHands specs[0] fallback semantics) and batch_get (pure filter resolving a named set → batch fan-out input) are real, small, non-redundant L1 enrichments. (d) Not plumbing: pre-selecting a default profile and resolving a named profile set are legitimate local single-user console behaviors. ADD-as-is is wrong (pagination + abstract ceremony are server baggage, get/list exist); DONT_ADD is wrong (the harvest lands on the user's #1 priority, L1 cc-agents.json). The honest verdict and the Shepherd mapping (pure cc-core agentDefault/agentBatchGet, fx for reads, unit-tested like presetList) both hold.

#### Rule-based env assembly into spawned runtime (auto-forward prefixes + explicit override merge)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** Deciding WHICH env a spawned agent inherits is a genuine spawn-time concern Shepherd already lives in: provider profiles inject ANTHROPIC_* env, and L1's profile schema already declares requiredEnv. The "explicit override wins over inherited defaults" precedence rule is clean, declarative, and directly expressible as a per-profile env block layered over fleet/host defaults — it sharpens L1's requiredEnv from a mere presence-check into a real assembled env spec, and the prefix idea offers an ergonomic shorthand (declare forwardPrefixes:["LLM_"] once instead of listing every LLM_ var) for users wiring gateway providers.
- **Against:** The literal mechanism copies host env VALUES into a process-held initial_env dict — exactly the stored-secrets pattern Shepherd forbids (it stores names, expands $VAR in the spawned login shell, never sees a key). And the auto-forward half is largely redundant: a Claude Code session spawned under loginShellWrap's interactive login zsh already inherits the entire host environment, so blanket prefix forwarding buys almost nothing Shepherd doesn't already get for free. Stripped of value-ingestion and the redundant auto-forward, what remains is just "let a profile declare named env to export" — which is already inside L1's requiredEnv + config-by-reference resolver. So the standalone feature is mostly redundant with the existing provider-env path plus queued L1 work.
- **Ruling:** The OpenHands mechanism (get_agent_server_env) does two things: (1) scan os.environ for prefixed keys and auto-forward their VALUES, (2) merge an OH_AGENT_SERVER_ENV JSON override on top, then spread the result into every spec's initial_env. Its core value is "declaratively decide which env a spawned agent inherits, with explicit overrides winning." That need is real and Shepherd-relevant at spawn time — but the IMPLEMENTATION trips the secrets half of the double mismatch: it copies env VALUES into a process-managed initial_env dict. Shepherd structurally refuses to ingest secret values; its whole env story (providerEnv/envPrefix/loginShellWrap, cc-core.lua ~2710-2757) stores NAMES (authTokenEnv) and emits $VAR for the spawned LOGIN shell to expand, so cc-config.json holds no key and the Hammerspoon process never sees one. So this is not an ADD — the literal mechanism violates scope — but it is not DONT_ADD either, because the management-layer pattern (rule-based, override-wins env assembly into the spawn) maps directly onto two things Shepherd already has/queued: provider profiles (the env-injection prefix builder) and, more precisely, L1's profile schema which ALREADY carries requiredEnv:[{name,description,required,default}] plus core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv) NAME-presence checks and a config-by-reference resolver. The OpenHands "explicit override merge, override wins" is exactly the kind of precedence rule L1's resolver and the existing resolveGateTools/providerById precedence chains express. The auto-forward-by-prefix half is the weakest part: a Claude Code session spawned under an interactive login zsh (loginShellWrap -lic) ALREADY inherits the full host environment, so blanket prefix forwarding is largely redundant in Shepherd's model — the durable value is the EXPLICIT, named, per-profile override that L1 already plans. Net: ADAPT, reinforcing L1, reshaped to name-only references (no value ingestion). Small effort because it's a schema enrichment + one merge/precedence helper on top of L1's already-planned requiredEnv/resolver, not a new system.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's profile schema: extend the already-planned requiredEnv:[{name,description,required,default}] into an assembled env block that supports (a) optional forwardPrefixes:[string] rendered as NAME-ONLY $VAR exports via the existing envPrefix/loginShellWrap path (cc-core.lua ~2738-2757), never value ingestion, and (b) an explicit per-profile env override that WINS over provider/fleet defaults. Implement as one pure precedence/merge helper (override > profile > provider > fleet) alongside L1's config-by-reference resolver and core.missingEnv, mirroring resolveGateTools/providerById precedence. cc-config.json/cc-agents.json stays value-free; secrets remain $VAR refs resolved by the spawned shell.
- **Skeptic:** Tried to refute down to DONT_ADD; could not. The feature has two halves and the judge already discounted the bad one correctly. (1) The auto-forward-by-prefix half copies env VALUES from os.environ into a process-managed initial_env dict — that is exactly secrets-into-process ingestion, which Shepherd structurally refuses (context.md:13; cc-core.lua:2620-2622 stores NAMES and emits $VAR). It is also redundant: spawns run under `zsh -lic` (loginShellWrap, cc-core.lua:2754-2757), so the spawned Claude Code session ALREADY inherits the full host env plus whatever .zshenv/.zprofile/.zshrc export — blanket LLM_*/LMNR_* forwarding adds nothing. So that half alone is DONT_ADD. (2) But the feature's durable core — an EXPLICIT per-profile env override merged with override-wins precedence — survives. It maps directly onto already-queued L1 primitives: requiredEnv:[{name,description,required,default}] + pure core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv) NAME-presence checks (todos.md:282-283) and the config-by-reference resolver that stores NAMES (todos.md:290-293), with the exact precedence pattern L2 already documents (session override > attached bundle > fleet default, todos.md:313, mirroring resolveGateTools). Reshaped to name-only $VAR exports through the existing envPrefix/loginShellWrap path, this is a scope-respecting schema enrichment plus one merge/precedence helper — an extension of an existing queued feature, not a new system and not the literal value-ingesting port. Because the whole value is neither fully redundant nor fully scope-violating (the name-only override merge is real and not yet expressed for env), it cannot be pushed to DONT_ADD; and because the literal mechanism (value ingestion) does violate scope, it is not a clean ADD. ADAPT reinforcing L1 is the correct verdict, and my attempt to break it instead confirmed the judge had already stripped exactly the half I would have rejected.

#### Pre-flight readiness validation before launch (asset pre-pull + prerequisite check, progress logging)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The candidate's own generalization states the case: "validate a profile's prerequisites and surface a progress/blocked state before allowing spawn." That is a genuine, transferable need for any launcher of preconfigured agents. Shepherd is about to gain saved agent profiles (L1) that reference providers, policy bundles, skills[], mcpServers[], plugins[], and requiredEnv[] — each a thing that can be missing or unresolvable at spawn time. Spawning a profile whose MCP server doesn't exist, whose skill name doesn't resolve under ~/.claude/skills, or whose requiredEnv var is absent in the login shell produces a silent broken session — exactly the "cold miss" failure OpenHands prevents by checking and blocking first. A pre-flight gate that aggregates structured errors and refuses to emit any flag until they're cleared directly improves the local console's spawn reliability. The 5s "still working" progress-logging idea also has a faint analog: a long folder scan or batch fan-out could surface a non-blocking "still preparing" state instead of appearing hung.
- **Against:** The literal OpenHands implementation is pure sandbox plumbing on the wrong side of the double mismatch. pull_missing_specs / _pull_with_progress_logging exist because OpenHands MANAGES a Docker sandbox it must provision before its agent loop can execute actions inside it — the image pull, the docker APIError, the SandboxError, the pull_if_missing cache flag. Shepherd manages no sandbox, pulls no images, and runs no agent loop; native Claude Code owns execution. So the asset-pre-pull half is structurally DONT_ADD — there is no asset for Shepherd to pre-pull. Worse, the validation half is already explicitly queued: todos.md L1 lines 285-289 ("Pre-flight validation/lint: a pure validator family... refuses spawn/attach with the full list before emitting any flag") and lines 282-284 (requiredEnv NAME-presence checks → New-Session preflight warning). Treating this as a new feature risks proposing a parallel system instead of reinforcing existing scope.
- **Ruling:** Strip the sandbox plumbing and judge the pattern, as instructed. The pattern is "check prerequisites, surface a blocked/progress state, refuse launch until clear" — and Shepherd has already converged on exactly this independently. The image pull half is sandbox-only and DONT_ADD in isolation, but the candidate explicitly abstracts to prerequisite validation, which lands squarely on L1's already-queued "Pre-flight validation/lint" validator family plus the requiredEnv NAME-presence check, both of which "refuse spawn before emitting any flag." Per the rubric, when an OpenHands feature matches a queued Lx item the verdict is ADAPT with reinforces set — not a new build. So this is ADAPT/L1: corroborating prior art (a second independent source validating the pre-flight gate design), not a new system. It also fits the user's stated priority (saved agents/skills/MCP in a directory) since validation is what makes a config-by-reference registry safe. Effort is folded into L1's already-estimated L; the marginal addition (a structured-error aggregator + refuse-spawn + optional progress state for batch/scan) is small. I scored it S as a standalone increment over what L1 already specs.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's "Pre-flight validation/lint" (cc-core.lua ~3590-3722, sibling to presetList/templateList): a pure validator family that aggregates structured errors — unknown-field, missing-required, unresolvable cross-refs (policyBundle / mcpServers[] / skills[] under ~/.claude/skills / plugins[]) — and refuses spawn/attach with the full list before spawnFlags emits anything. Pairs with core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv) for requiredEnv NAME-presence (never values, via loginShellWrap) → New-Session preflight warning + optional tile/profile-card badge. Shared with L2 attach and L3 render-refuse. The "blocked/progress state" surfaces as the preflight warning + a refuse-spawn dialog listing errors; the 5s progress-logging idea maps only to a non-blocking "still preparing" indicator for long batch fan-out / folder scans, not an image pull. DROP outright: pull_missing_specs / _pull_with_progress_logging / docker APIError / SandboxError / pull_if_missing — no sandbox, no asset to pull.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails on every common-error axis; the ruling holds as ADAPT reinforcing L1. (a) Sandbox/executor — the strongest angle, and it kills the LITERAL feature: the docker image pre-pull, _pull_with_progress_logging, docker APIError, SandboxError, pull_if_missing all depend on a managed Docker/remote sandbox Shepherd does not have, and the 5s progress logging is the cold-pull's own log. But the candidate text itself abstracts to "validate a profile's prerequisites and surface a progress/blocked state before allowing spawn," and the harness rule is explicit: judge the pattern, not the sandbox plumbing. The surviving pattern is sandbox-free (validate against local files, refuse spawn), so (a) cannot carry a downgrade. The judge already drops the pull half outright — correct. (b) Auth/secrets/server — clean. The env half is core.missingEnv checking NAME-presence only, never values, via loginShellWrap (verified against the no-secrets scope in context.md); no server, no users. Cannot refute here. (c) Redundant/queued — this is the decisive axis and it points AT ADAPT, not DONT_ADD. The abstracted pattern matches L1 almost verbatim: todos.md:285-289 "Pre-flight validation/lint: a pure validator family (sibling to presetList/templateList) returns aggregated structured errors ... and refuses spawn/attach with the full list before emitting any flag," plus todos.md:282-284 requiredEnv + core.missingEnv NAME-presence -> New-Session preflight warning. I confirmed this is QUEUED, not yet built (no missingEnv/preflight validator exists in cc-core.lua today — verified via grep), so it is not redundant-with-shipped DONT_ADD either. The harness rubric: when an OpenHands feature matches a queued Lx, verdict is ADAPT with reinforces=Lx. Exactly this. (d) Plumbing-mistaken-for-feature — true of the image pull (correctly dropped), but the abstracted gate is a real end-user console behavior (preflight warning + refuse-spawn dialog in the New Session modal; non-blocking "still preparing" indicator for long batch fan-out/folder scans, NOT an image pull). Net: nothing the sandbox-strip leaves is novel beyond what L1 already specs; the value is corroboration (a second independent source validating L1's pre-flight gate), which is precisely what ADAPT/reinforces encodes. Effort folds into L1's already-estimated L; marginal add is S (structured-error aggregator + refuse-spawn + optional progress state). No basis to flip to DONT_ADD or up to ADD. Shepherd mapping: cc-core.lua ~3590-3722 validator family + core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv), shared with L2 attach / L3 render-refuse. Relevant files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md (L1, lines 242-301), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua (presetList/templateList siblings ~3590-3722), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/context.md (no sandbox/server/secrets scope).

#### Health-checked readiness with a startup grace window then error  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The literal mechanism is foreign, but the NEED is real and currently unmet. Shepherd's spawn flow (claude-dashboard.lua ~1411-1574, core.spawnSpec ~1522) launches a session BEST-EFFORT and never confirms it actually came up: there is no pendingSpawn/spawnPending/firstSeen tracking anywhere (grep confirms zero). If a spawn silently fails (wrong dir, the editor didn't open, the claude CLI errored at launch, a bad provider env), the result is just... no tile, with no signal to the operator — the one thing a watch/steer/spawn console exists to catch. OpenHands' insight is exactly the right framing for this gap: a freshly-launched agent gets a GRACE WINDOW before it's judged failed, distinguishing "still booting" from "genuinely never came up." Shepherd even already has the two halves of this pattern for RUNNING sessions — the error grace clock (core.shouldAutoRespawn / since[key] stamping ~2211, and the magenta Error state ~2193) and the watchdog's grace-then-flag logic (isHung/trackProgress/applyProgress ~2375). Generalizing that grace-window reasoning to the post-spawn window (a transient "Starting…" tile that flips to a distinct "Spawn failed / never came up" state if no status file appears within a grace budget) is a small, well-fitting addition that reuses existing primitives and directly serves the spawn console. It also dovetails with L1's pre-flight validation: pre-flight catches bad config before launch; this catches a launch that produced nothing after.
- **Against:** As implemented, this is the OpenHands double mismatch in its purest form — it is ALL sandbox + server plumbing. Its three load-bearing parts each name something Shepherd structurally cannot host: (1) an HTTP /alive + configurable health_check_path endpoint — Shepherd runs NO server and makes NO network/HTTP calls; (2) a managed Docker/remote container with a StartedAt timestamp to compare against — Shepherd manages no sandbox and owns no container lifecycle; (3) an agent SERVER that must become "serviceable" before the agent is trusted RUNNING — in Shepherd the agent is an already-running Claude Code GUI session that Claude Code itself drives, not a process Shepherd boots and waits on. wait_for_sandbox_running() and _check_agent_server_alive() are inherently executor/sandbox code. Copied as-is it is meaningless. And one could argue the residual need is already covered: the stuck-session watchdog flags hung working sessions, the Error state flags frozen API errors, and shouldPrune/staleness cleans up dead tiles — so the marginal value of a NEW post-spawn state is modest, and risks adding another off-by-default state machine to an app that already has several.
- **Ruling:** DONT_ADD for the literal feature (HTTP /alive poll + container StartedAt vs grace → STARTING/ERROR + raise-on-timeout = sandbox+server plumbing Shepherd can't host), but the underlying need — don't misjudge a session in its startup window; distinguish still-coming-up from genuinely-failed — is real and currently UNMET, so ADAPT. The decisive finding: Shepherd has NO post-spawn confirmation at all (grep for pendingSpawn/firstSeen/confirmSpawn/spawn.*timeout returns nothing), so a silently-failed spawn produces an invisible no-op — a genuine gap in a console whose whole job is watch/steer/spawn. Crucially, Shepherd already owns BOTH halves of the OpenHands pattern, just not for the spawn window: a grace-clock-then-Error path (since[key] stamp + shouldAutoRespawn ~2211, magenta Error ~2193) and a grace-then-flag watchdog (isHung/trackProgress/applyProgress ~2375). So the adaptation is to GENERALIZE that grace-window reasoning to the post-spawn moment, not invent a new system — and the signal is filesystem-native (did ~/.claude/cc-status/<id>.json appear?), never HTTP. It reinforces L1: pre-flight validation refuses a bad config before launch; this is the symmetric post-flight check that a launch which passed pre-flight actually produced a live tile. Not the watchdog (that keys on a session already "working"), not the Error state (that needs a transcript api_error line) — both require a session that already exists. This is the one window neither covers. Marked the marginal-value counterargument honestly, but the unmet-gap + clean primitive reuse + L1 fit outweigh it. Effort S: a pure helper (e.g. core.spawnReadiness over {spawnedAt, sawStatusFile, graceSeconds} → "starting"|"failed") plus a transient tile state and a one-shot spawn-watch timer in the dashboard; default-on for spawns Shepherd itself fired (it has the launch timestamp), no config surface required.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a post-spawn readiness window to the spawn lifecycle, reusing the existing grace-clock idiom. (1) When Shepherd fires a spawn (claude-dashboard.lua ~1522 orchSpawn / spawnSpec), record spawnedAt + the expected projectKey in a small pending-spawn table (mirrors the respawnAttempts/watchdog dashboard state tables). (2) New PURE helper core.spawnReadiness({spawnedAt, sawStatusFile, now, graceSeconds=15}) → "starting" | "failed", unit-tested alongside isHung/shouldAutoRespawn — sawStatusFile is set the first refresh tick that a matching ~/.claude/cc-status/<id>.json for that projectKey appears (filesystem signal, NEVER HTTP, never /alive). (3) Render a transient grey "Starting…" tile (or status chip) during the grace window; flip to a distinct "Spawn failed — never came up" state (sibling to the magenta Error tile ~2193, reusing frontSession/jump ordering ~1465) once the budget expires with no status file. (4) Clear the pending entry the moment the real tile appears (sawStatusFile true) or on failure acknowledgement. Reinforces L1: pre-flight validation (todos.md ~285) refuses bad config BEFORE emitting flags; spawnReadiness is the symmetric POST-flight check that a validated spawn actually materialized a session — same lifecycle, opposite end. Default-on for Shepherd-initiated spawns only (it owns the timestamp); no Settings surface needed beyond the grace seconds.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt FAILS — the ADAPT ruling holds. The literal OpenHands feature (HTTP /alive poll, container StartedAt vs grace, wait_for_sandbox_running raising on timeout) IS sandbox+server plumbing and is correctly conceded as DONT_ADD. But the adaptation survives every common-error test, verified against the code: (a) No executor/sandbox: the adaptation replaces the HTTP /alive probe with a FILESYSTEM signal — "did ~/.claude/cc-status/<id>.json appear?" Shepherd already reads STATUS_DIR (claude-dashboard.lua:54) every ~1s refresh tick. Proposed core.spawnReadiness({spawnedAt, sawStatusFile, now, graceSeconds}) is pure logic, no hs.*, a sibling to the existing isHung/shouldAutoRespawn/stepAutoContinue family. No agent loop, no network call. (b) No multi-user/secrets/server: default-on only for Shepherd-fired spawns; Shepherd owns the timestamp (spawn ledger event at claude-dashboard.lua:1554; FX.now() at :255). No auth, no secrets, no server. No config surface. (c) NOT redundant / not already-have-it — the decisive finding, confirmed: grep for pendingSpawn/firstSeen/confirmSpawn/spawnReadiness/spawn.*timeout returns nothing. isHung (cc-core.lua:2376) returns false unless status=="working" — it cannot see a spawn that never produced a tile. The magenta Error state (claude-dashboard.lua:2704, 5421-5427) is derived from a transcript api_error line — needs an already-existing session+transcript. FX.spawnSession (1523-1578) fires-and-forgets: no spawnedAt retained, no tile-appearance tracking. The only near-miss (todos.md:195 'Spawn-then-appear') is an acceptance-test observation with NO failure branch. The post-spawn window is genuinely unmet — a silently-failed spawn is an invisible no-op in a console whose whole job is watch/steer/spawn. (d) Not server plumbing mistaken for a console feature: a grey 'Starting…' tile that flips to 'Spawn failed — never came up' is a legitimate local-console affordance. ADD->ADAPT is the right direction: it reuses the grace-clock idiom (the since[key] stamp + grace-then-fire in stepAutoContinue 2231-2237 and stepAutoRespawn 2227-2237) and is the symmetric POST-flight counterpart to L1's pre-flight validation (todos.md 285-289, which refuses a bad config BEFORE emitting flags). Same spawn lifecycle, opposite end — a reinforcement/extension, not a new parallel system. Effort S. Ruling stands.

#### Session-key-scoped secret lookup (sandbox fetches its own secret names/values at runtime, names-only listing)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** Buried inside the server/auth plumbing is one principle that is real and directly applicable: "expose secret NAMES/descriptions, never values." Shepherd's spawn UX touches this exact seam — a provider profile or (queued) agent profile needs a credential (e.g. ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN), and the operator benefits from seeing WHICH env-var names a session depends on and whether they are present, without Shepherd ever holding a value. The names-only listing + a per-record description is precisely the missing affordance for a pre-flight "is this agent's required env exported?" check. The candidate also validates a design Shepherd already chose: credentials are referenced by NAME and resolved fresh at the moment of use (OpenHands resolves provider tokens per call; Shepherd resolves $VAR in the spawned login shell at launch) rather than baked into a stored spec. That convergence is a useful sanity check on the providerEnv/requiredEnv direction.
- **Against:** The feature AS BUILT trips the OpenHands DOUBLE MISMATCH on both axes simultaneously. Axis (a): the entity that fetches the secret is a RUNNING sandbox — an in-process agent executor making an HTTP callback to retrieve its own credentials at runtime. Shepherd runs no agent loop and no sandbox; nothing inside a session calls back to Shepherd for a token, and Claude Code itself owns credential access. Axis (b): the value being protected lives in a per-user secrets STORE, the request is gated by X-Session-API-Key -> AuthUserContext -> created_by_user_id==caller — i.e. multi-user auth + stored secrets, the two things Shepherd's hard-scope explicitly forbids (NO server, NO users, NO secrets storage). Shepherd has no session-key endpoint to authenticate, no owner to match, and no value to return because it never stores one (it stores only the env-var NAME and lets the spawned shell expand $VAR). The entire runtime-fetch/serve/ownership-gate mechanism is therefore unbuildable and pointless here. Building a literal "fetch a secret value as plaintext" path would actively violate the no-secrets invariant.
- **Ruling:** Split the feature in two. (1) The runtime credential-serving mechanism (session-key auth, AuthUserContext, RUNNING+owner gate, sandbox-fetches-its-own-value-as-plaintext) is pure OpenHands executor + server + multi-user + secrets-store — all four things Shepherd structurally cannot host — so that half is DONT_ADD on its own. (2) The "safe-surfacing principle" (list NAMES/descriptions, never values; reference credentials by name, resolve fresh at point of use) is a genuine pattern Shepherd already embodies and already has queued to extend. providerEnv (cc-core.lua ~2710/2714) stores authTokenEnv = the env-var NAME and emits a $VAR shell-ref the login shell expands, so Shepherd's process never sees a key. L1 already queues requiredEnv:[{name,description,required,default}] plus a pure core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv) doing NAME-presence-only checks for a New-Session pre-flight warning + tile badge — which IS the local, no-secrets reshaping of "list available secret names and tell me what's missing." Verdict is therefore ADAPT reinforcing L1: adopt the names+descriptions surfacing and point it at requiredEnv/missingEnv; do NOT build any value-fetch, endpoint, key auth, or ownership gate. There is no new system to create — the underlying need is already covered by the existing providerEnv pattern and the queued L1 requiredEnv/preflight work; this candidate is corroborating evidence that the queued shape is right, not a reason to add anything parallel.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's queued requiredEnv work, no new primitive. (1) Add an optional `description` (alongside existing `required`/`default`) to L1's requiredEnv:[{name,description,required,default}] records on agent profiles + cc-mcp.json entries, so a profile card SURFACES the env-var names a session needs and what each is for — the "names + descriptions, never values" half of the candidate. (2) The pre-flight is the queued pure core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv) (todos.md ~282-284): NAME-presence-only against the spawn shell's env, feeding a New-Session preflight warning + optional tile badge — the local analog of "list available secret names." (3) Reaffirm the no-value invariant: credentials stay referenced by NAME via authTokenEnv/providerEnv (cc-core.lua ~2710-2733) and expanded by loginShellWrap at spawn (cc-core.lua ~2754); Shepherd NEVER stores or returns a value, so there is explicitly NO value-fetch path, NO endpoint, NO session-key auth, NO ownership gate to build. Net: a description field plus reuse of the already-queued missingEnv/preflight; the value-serving mechanism is dropped.
- **Skeptic:** Could not refute; ADAPT (reinforces L1) holds. Verified both halves of the judge's split against the codebase. HALF 1 — the runtime credential-serving mechanism (X-Session-API-Key auth, AuthUserContext, RUNNING+owner gate, sandbox-fetches-its-own-value-as-plaintext) is pure OpenHands executor + server + multi-user + secrets-store, the full double-mismatch: Shepherd runs no agent loop, manages no sandbox, has no server/users/stored secrets. Confirmed empirically: grep for X-Session-API-Key / AuthUserContext / secret-value-fetch / getSecret across cc-core.lua + claude-dashboard.lua finds NOTHING; the only 'session key' hits are the local status-file basename used as a session identifier (cc-core.lua:277,401,1875,2449), not an auth API key. And todos.md:270-274 (DROP list) already explicitly rejects 'stored auth values', 'server-side health pings/endpoints', and stored auth — so this half is structurally DONT_ADD and already foreclosed. HALF 2 — the 'list NAMES/descriptions, never values; resolve fresh at point of use' safe-surfacing principle is already embodied AND already queued, so it is corroborating evidence, not a new system. providerEnv (cc-core.lua:2714-2733) stores authTokenEnv = the env-var NAME and at 2728-2729 emits a $VAR shell-ref (secret=true) that the spawned interactive login shell expands via loginShellWrap 'zsh -lic' (2754-2756) + envPrefix double-quoting (2738-2746); the header (2620-2622) states 'Secrets are NEVER stored... Shepherd's own process never sees one.' L1 already queues requiredEnv:[{name,description,required,default}] on profiles + cc-mcp.json records plus a pure core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv) doing NAME-presence-only checks → New-Session preflight warning + optional tile badge (todos.md:282-284) — the exact local, no-secrets, no-server reshaping of 'list available secret names and tell me what's missing', including the description field. So the underlying need is already covered by the existing providerEnv pattern and the queued L1 requiredEnv/missingEnv preflight; nothing parallel needs to be built (no value-fetch, no endpoint, no key auth, no ownership gate). ADD->ADAPT because it extends/reinforces existing+queued work rather than introducing a new primitive; cannot drop to DONT_ADD because the names+descriptions surfacing genuinely improves the local console and is exactly what L1 plans.
