#### Named LLM profiles registry (saved model configs: list/get/save/delete/rename, one active)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The underlying NEED — a named, persisted, editable collection of LLM configurations (model + base_url + knobs) you can list/get/save/delete/rename with one marked default/active — is squarely a watch/steer/SPAWN concern and Shepherd already lives here. Shepherd's shipped `providers` registry (cc-core.lua: providerById:2632, providerByModel:2659, providerEnv:2714; cc-config.example.json `providers[]` + `spawn.provider` default) IS a named-LLM-config registry today: each provider profile carries {id, kind, model, baseUrl, smallFastModel, headers, authTokenEnv, ssh} and `spawn.provider` is exactly the "one active/default" pointer the candidate wants. The candidate's CRUD verbs (save/delete/rename) are the one gap — Shepherd's providers are hand-edited config-by-Settings rather than first-class CRUD with rename. Folding rename + a clean activate-pointer into the registry, and rolling the per-profile knobs up into the queued L1 agent-profile schema, is a real ergonomic win for "spawn from a saved config" — the user's stated #1 priority.
- **Against:** The candidate's load-bearing, defining trait is that each profile stores a FULL LLM config INCLUDING the api_key, persisted per-user, with the org variant masking keys on read and lifting the real key into an ENCRYPTED COLUMN on activate. That is precisely the half of OpenHands' double mismatch Shepherd structurally cannot host: it has a HARD scope limit of NO stored secrets (resolves $ENV via the spawned shell, never stores keys), NO multi-user, NO server. The per-user asyncio lock / SELECT...FOR UPDATE / encrypted-column machinery exists only to serve multi-tenant concurrent web writes — a single-user local Hammerspoon panel has no concurrent writers and no users to scope to, so the locking and the org variant are pure dead weight. Strip the secrets, the multi-tenancy, and the locks, and what remains (a named list of model configs with a default) is a feature Shepherd ALREADY SHIPPED as `providers` and has already queued the superset of as L1. So the as-is feature is mostly DONT_ADD material, and the salvageable kernel is redundant/already-planned.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch precisely. The candidate is NOT executor-bound (no agent loop / sandbox needed to maintain a config list), so it escapes mismatch (a). But its DEFINING value is mismatch (b): stored secrets (api_key in the profile, encrypted key column on activate), multi-user persistence (per-user store, per-user locks), and a server (REST routes, org row, SELECT...FOR UPDATE). Those are exactly Shepherd's NO list. Yet the PATTERN underneath — a named, persisted, CRUD-able registry of LLM configs with one active default, that you spawn FROM — is real, local, and already Shepherd's wheelhouse. Shepherd implements it the only sound way for a no-secrets console: the `providers[]` registry stores the env-var NAME (authTokenEnv -> "$VAR"), expanded by the spawned login shell (providerEnv:2714), with `spawn.provider` as the active pointer and providerByModel for respawn matching. The queued L1 Agent Profiles registry is the explicit superset (profile = provider + model + permMode + seedPrompt + skills[] + mcpServers[] + policyBundle + versions[]), and todos.md ~235 already commits to reusing the authTokenEnv no-secrets pattern and to pure cc-core CRUD (agentList/agentPush/agentRemove). So the verdict is ADAPT reinforcing L1, not ADD: take ONLY the management ergonomics (first-class save/delete/RENAME + a clean activate/default-pointer over the registry), DROP every secrets/lock/org/REST element. Net new work over what L1 already plans is small — mainly a rename verb and an explicit activate-pointer toggle on the providers/agents registry, both pure cc-core functions mirroring presetPush/presetList. Not L because L1 already accounts for the bulk; the delta this candidate adds is S.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 + the shipped `providers` registry. (1) Add pure cc-core CRUD-with-rename mirroring presetPush/presetList (cc-core.lua ~3640-3722): agentRename/providerRename + agentSetActive, validate + cap + replace-in-place, zero hs.*. (2) Promote `spawn.provider` (and an agent equivalent) into an explicit "active/default profile" pointer surfaced in the New Session modal (one marked active, like the candidate's active flag). (3) Each profile = the L1 superset {name, folder, provider, model, permMode, seedPrompt, skills[], mcpServers[], policyBundle, versions[]}; the LLM-config knobs (model/baseUrl/smallFastModel/headers) ride the existing provider record. (4) Secrets: store env-var NAME via authTokenEnv only (cc-core.lua:2728), expanded by loginShellWrap/envPrefix at spawn — NEVER the api_key value. (5) Registries live as operator-data JSON (cc-agents.json / providers in cc-config.json) outside the Settings round-trip, git-versioned. DROP: stored api_key, encrypted column, masking, per-user asyncio locks, SELECT...FOR UPDATE, the org row variant, FastAPI/REST routes, FileSettingsStore — all server/secrets/multi-user plumbing Shepherd cannot and must not host.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute the ADAPT ruling and could not; it holds. Walking the double mismatch: (a) Executor/sandbox — NO mismatch. Maintaining a list of model configs runs no agent loop and needs no Docker/remote runtime, so this does not get killed by (a). The judge is correct. (b) Secrets/multi-user/server — the candidate's LITERAL form is squarely on Shepherd's NO list: stored api_key in each profile, encrypted-key column on activate, key masking, per-user persistence, per-user asyncio locks, SELECT...FOR UPDATE, the org row, and FastAPI/REST routes. None of that can or should live in Shepherd (local, single-user, no server, no stored secrets per context.md:9). So a literal ADD is correctly rejected. (c) Redundancy with existing/queued — this is the hinge, and it pushes toward ADAPT, not DONT_ADD, because the candidate is NOT fully redundant: the PATTERN underneath (a named, persisted, CRUD-able registry of LLM configs with one active default that you spawn FROM) is already Shepherd's wheelhouse AND has a genuine small delta. Verified in the tree: the shipped `providers[]` registry IS the saved-model-config store — providerById (cc-core.lua:2632), providerByModel (2659), providerEnv (2714), with each record carrying model/baseUrl/smallFastModel/headers/contextLimit (cc-config.example.json providers block); `spawn.provider` via spawnProviderKey (cc-core.lua:2648) IS the 'one active/default' pointer; secrets are handled the only sound no-secrets way — authTokenEnv stores the env-var NAME, never the key, expanded by the spawned login shell (cc-core.lua:2728, README/todos.md:235). The save/delete CRUD primitive ships for presets (presetPush/presetList/presetRemove, cc-core.lua:3658-3717), and todos.md L1 (~249) explicitly commits to mirroring it as agentList/agentPush/agentRemove, reusing the authTokenEnv no-secrets pattern and pure cc-core CRUD. The net-new work the candidate justifies is real but small (effort S): a first-class RENAME verb (grep confirms NO rename verb exists today — presets/providers only do replace-in-place by same-name, cc-core.lua:3602/3688) and an explicit activate/default-pointer toggle as a user gesture over the registry. Both are pure cc-core functions mirroring presetPush — zero hs.*, no server, no secrets. This is the exact rubric case for ADD->ADAPT: an extension/reinforcement of an existing/queued feature, taking only the management ergonomics and DROPPING every secrets/lock/org/REST element. One minor mapping nuance: the rename/activate ergonomics arguably reinforce the SHIPPED `providers` registry even more directly than queued L1 (L1 already plans the bulk of the CRUD), but that is a mapping detail, not a verdict change. Verdict stands: ADAPT.

#### Activate / switch a session to a saved profile (apply model + base_url backfill)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The underlying NEED — "pick a saved profile by name and apply it" — is the user's #1 stated priority ("pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory") and is already the spine of queued L1 (Agent Profiles registry + "Spawn from agent"). Two of the three sub-behaviors map cleanly onto primitives Shepherd already owns: (1) base_url back-fill from the provider default when omitted is a pure, deterministic normalization that drops straight into L1's config-by-reference resolver (which generalizes the existing providerById:2632 / providerByModel:2659 / providerEnv:2714 chain — Shepherd already distinguishes anthropic vs gateway profiles and exports ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL); today providerEnv only emits base_url when profile.baseUrl is explicitly set, so "fill from the provider default if omitted" is a genuine, small gap worth closing. (2) "Pre-load the profile into the spawn modal" is exactly L1's "Spawn from agent" action plus the existing per-project last-used recall (presetPush/lastByProject ~3707). And for an ALREADY-running session, Shepherd even has a partial live-apply: the /model <id> switch (modelCommand:236, the d-model dropdown) hot-changes the tier within the same backend, with relaunch (respawnSpec:2688) covering a base_url/provider change. So the operator-facing goal — "make this session use profile X" — is achievable on Shepherd's own steering primitives.
- **Against:** The headline mechanism is squarely the OpenHands EXECUTOR (mismatch a). switch_to_profile copies the saved LLM onto agent_settings.llm, which the in-process agent loop reads on its NEXT LLM call — the SAME running conversation silently starts calling a different model/endpoint mid-flight, with no new process. Shepherd runs no agent loop and makes no API calls; it cannot hot-swap a live session's LLM config in-place. Its nearest equivalents are lossy: /model only switches the tier within the current backend (the code is explicit at cc-core.lua:162-164/232-233 that a base_url change "needs a fresh session (relaunch), not /model"), and a true profile swap (different provider/base_url/auth) requires killing and relaunching the session — which loses conversation state, the very thing OpenHands preserves. The "mark llm_profiles.active + reconcile_active_profile" bookkeeping is server-side single-source-of-truth state for a multi-user settings store that Shepherd doesn't have. So building the feature "close to as-is" is impossible; only the spawn-side slice survives, which is L1, not a new system.
- **Ruling:** DOUBLE-MISMATCH applied: the core verb "activate a saved profile onto the agent's live LLM config" depends on the in-process executor reading agent_settings.llm — Shepherd has no such loop, so an in-place live swap is structurally out (DONT_ADD for that slice). But the feature decomposes: (a) live in-place swap = executor, drop; (b) base_url back-fill from provider default = pure normalization, ADD into L1's resolver; (c) pre-load into spawn modal / spawn-from-profile = already L1. The real, transferable need is "name a saved profile, apply its model+base_url to a NEW or RELAUNCHED session, with base_url inferred from the provider when omitted." That is a refinement of L1 (the Agent Profiles registry + Spawn-from-agent + config-by-reference resolver) and of the existing providers registry, not a parallel system — hence ADAPT, reinforces L1, not ADD and not DONT_ADD. The base_url-backfill normalization is the one net-new pure helper worth lifting verbatim; everything else already exists or is queued. Off-by-default scope is respected (it's spawn/relaunch flag emission, no automation). No secrets stored — base_url is a non-secret literal; auth still rides $VAR resolved by the spawned shell (providerEnv already does this).
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's config-by-reference resolver (the generalization of providerById:2632/resolveGateTools:2537). Add ONE pure helper — core.resolveProviderBaseUrl(profile)/normalizeProviderBaseUrl — that, when a profile/agent omits baseUrl, fills it from a provider-default table keyed by provider id (and strips a base_url that equals the provider default, mirroring OpenHands' rstrip('/') + "matches default -> drop" rule), then have providerEnv:2714 consume the resolved value. Surface "Spawn from this agent/profile" as the modal pre-load (L1 spawn-from-agent + the existing lastByProject recall ~3707); for a running tile keep the current /model live-switch for same-backend tier changes and route a provider/base_url change through respawnSpec:2688 relaunch (with the existing "unknown gateway provider -> canRespawn false" guard). DROP: in-place live LLM hot-swap of a running session (no executor), llm_profiles.active server-state + reconcile_active_profile (no multi-user settings store), ActivateProfileResponse endpoint (no server). Honor the cc-core.lua <-> cc-approve.sh keep-in-sync contract only if a bundle rides along (it doesn't here).
- **Skeptic:** Attempted refutation; could not break the ruling. Verified against the actual code. (1) The headline verb — apply a profile onto the agent's LIVE LLM config (agent_settings.llm) and hot-swap a running session — genuinely needs an in-process executor Shepherd lacks: grep for `agent_settings` returns nothing, there is no agent loop, and the only live mechanism is typing `/model <id>` into a window (cc-core.lua:232-238 modelCommand), whose own comment says "same provider only -- a base-URL change needs a relaunch." So the in-place live provider/base_url swap is correctly DROP (error type (a), executor). (2) The surviving slices are NOT net-new parallel systems: spawn-from-profile / modal pre-load is already L1 (todos.md:254, "Spawn from agent: new action via the New Session modal -> spawnSpec"); the config-by-reference resolver is already queued in L1 (todos.md:290-293, explicitly "extends providerById:2632 / resolveGateTools:2537" — the judge's exact mapping); a provider/base_url change of a running tile routes through respawnSpec:2688 with the existing "unknown gateway provider -> canRespawn false" guard (cc-core.lua:2698-2700, verified). That is reinforcement of a queued feature => ADD->ADAPT, not DONT_ADD. (3) The one net-new pure helper (resolveProviderBaseUrl/normalizeProviderBaseUrl) is legitimate and non-redundant: I searched cc-core.lua, claude-dashboard.lua, todos.md, context.md, and cc-config.example.json and found NO provider-default base_url table and NO backfill/normalize logic — providerEnv:2723 emits profile.baseUrl raw. (4) No scope violation: base_url is a non-secret literal; auth still rides $VAR via authTokenEnv expanded by the spawned shell (cc-core.lua:2728-2729), so no stored secrets, no server, no multi-user. The dropped pieces (llm_profiles.active server-state, reconcile_active_profile, ActivateProfileResponse endpoint) are correctly excluded. None of the common errors (a/b/c/d) defeat the residual feature; it is a faithful refinement of the existing providers registry + queued L1 resolver. ADAPT reinforcing L1 holds.

#### Active-profile pointer reconciliation (don't claim a profile is in use that diverged)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is pure MANAGEMENT-layer logic, not executor/sandbox/server/secrets — it clears the double mismatch cleanly. Both halves are deterministic data hygiene that Shepherd's cc-core architecture lives for: (1) referential integrity — drop an "active/default" pointer that names a record no longer in the registry (a dangling foreign key), and (2) drift detection — stop asserting "profile X is in use" once the live thing it described no longer matches the saved record. Shepherd already half-built this: `providerByModel` (cc-core.lua:2660) reconciles a RUNNING session's live `model`/`base_url` (captured by cc-status.sh:213-214) back to the saved provider profile and REFUSES the respawn with a surfaced reason ("unknown gateway provider") when the live config diverged — exactly reconcile_active_profile's "don't claim a match that no longer holds." The L1 plan already specs a pre-flight validator that "refuses spawn/attach... when policyBundle/mcpServers[]/skills[] don't resolve" — the dangling-pointer half generalizes to "spawn.provider default points at a deleted provider id," a real footgun the L1 registry introduces. Once L1 adds a saved-agents registry plus a default/last-used pointer, the integrity gap becomes live: a deleted provider/agent leaves spawn.provider or presetForProject pointing at nothing, and the UI would silently spawn bare `claude` on the wrong endpoint (the spawnProviderKey:2643 comment already warns this exact collapse is "wrong endpoint/auth/model"). Cheap, pure, on-architecture.
- **Against:** The literal OpenHands feature reconciles a SINGLE live LLM config against ONE saved active profile — a 1:1 "which profile is the session running" relationship that exists because OpenHands is a per-conversation agent executor that mutates agent_settings.llm in place. Shepherd has no single "active profile" pointer and no in-process config to diverge: each tile is an independently-spawned session that carries its own live model/base_url, and there are N of them, not one. So the direct port has no home — there is no agent_settings.llm for a saved profile to drift from, and Shepherd deliberately doesn't proxy or own the running config. The divergence case Shepherd CAN observe (a session whose live model no longer matches the profile it spawned from, e.g. after a live /model switch) is arguably WORKING AS INTENDED — Shepherd explicitly supports live /model and /effort switching, so a session "diverging" from its launch profile is a feature, not a stale claim to clear. And the genuinely-portable half (drop a default/active pointer that names a deleted registry record) is small enough to be a one-line guard folded into L1's validator rather than a feature worth tracking — risk of over-engineering a parallel "reconciliation" system Shepherd doesn't need.
- **Ruling:** The against-case correctly kills the literal port (no single active pointer, no in-process config; per-session live /model switching is intended, not drift). But the underlying NEED — a saved pointer must not assert a binding that reality has abandoned — is real and becomes live precisely when L1 lands the saved-agents registry plus the existing spawn.provider default and presetForProject/lastByProject recall pointers. The honest reshape is the referential-integrity half (a default/active pointer naming a deleted provider/agent/preset must be dropped or flagged, never silently used to spawn the wrong endpoint), which folds directly into L1's already-specced pre-flight validator and config-by-ref resolver. The drift half maps to a SAFE, observation-only variant Shepherd can already source: tile-level provenance — when a running session's live model/base_url no longer matches the agent profile it spawned from (data Shepherd already has via cc-status + providerByModel), show it as informational ("launched from <profile>, now running <model>"), NOT auto-cleared, because live switching is legitimate. So: ADAPT, reinforcing L1, as a validator guard plus an optional provenance note — not a new reconciliation engine. Not DONT_ADD because it needs none of the missing primitives (pure cc-core data hygiene); not ADD because the 1:1 active-pointer shape and auto-clear-on-divergence semantics must be reshaped onto N independent tiles and Shepherd's intentional live-switching.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's pre-flight validator (todos.md ~L286-296, the validator family sibling to presetList/templateList): add a referential-integrity pass that flags/drops any default-or-active pointer naming a record absent from its registry — spawn.provider -> providers[].id (extend providerById:2632), L1's default-agent pointer -> cc-agents.json, presetForProject/lastByProject -> presetList, mcpServers[]/skills[]/policyBundle cross-refs (already specced). Refuse spawn-from-default with the dangling name surfaced rather than silently spawning bare claude (the spawnProviderKey:2643 collapse). For the drift half, reuse providerByModel (cc-core.lua:2660) + the live model/base_url already on each tile to compute an OPTIONAL, off-by-default detail-panel provenance note ("launched from <agentProfile>, now on <model>") — observation only, never auto-cleared, because live /model switching is supported. Pure cc-core; no FX beyond reads Shepherd already does.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt failed; the ADAPT (reinforces L1) ruling holds. Checked the four standard kill-paths against the actual code: (a) NO executor/sandbox. The surviving feature is a pure cc-core referential-integrity pass over JSON config (cc-config.json / cc-agents.json / cc-mcp.json) plus an optional read-only provenance note. The provenance half's inputs already exist on every tile — item.model + item.base_url — and the resolver providerByModel is already implemented (cc-core.lua:2659, 2696-2697). No agent loop, no Docker, no action execution. Fails this kill-path. (b) NO multi-user/secrets/server. Single local config, one user. It checks pointer-target PRESENCE (does this name resolve to a record), never values/keys. Fails this kill-path. (c) NOT redundant — this is the strongest refutation angle and it still fails. The literal OpenHands port (one `active` pointer + a model_validator that auto-clears `active` on live-config divergence) IS dead: Shepherd has no single active pointer (grep for active-profile/reconcile = zero hits) and per-session live /model switching is intentional, so auto-clear-on-divergence is wrong. That correctly kills ADD. But the underlying NEED is real and currently UNGUARDED: presets already ship with a `provider` NAME-pointer that NOTHING validates — presetList (cc-core.lua:3658) passes `provider = p.provider` through verbatim, presetForProject/lastByProject recall the same unchecked pointer, and the spawn.provider default (2649) is read raw. A provider the user later deletes leaves a dangling id that spawnProviderKey forwards and providerById (2632) resolves to nil, silently collapsing to bare claude on the wrong endpoint — exactly the failure the 2643 comment warns about. L1's specced pre-flight validator (todos.md L285-296) only enumerates L1-profile-internal cross-refs (policyBundle/mcpServers[]/skills[]); extending that same validator family to the spawn.provider default and preset/recall provider pointers is a genuine incremental reinforcement, not a duplicate. (d) NOT server/library plumbing — it surfaces as a spawn-refusal/modal warning + an off-by-default detail-panel provenance note ("launched from <profile>, now on <model>"), real end-user console behavior. Net: pure cc-core data hygiene needing none of Shepherd's missing primitives -> not DONT_ADD. The 1:1 active-pointer + auto-clear semantics must be reshaped onto N independent tiles and Shepherd's intentional live switching (validator-guard half + observation-only provenance half) -> not ADD. ADAPT, reinforcing L1, stands. Mapping is accurate: fold the referential-integrity pass into L1's validator family (~todos.md L285-296 / cc-core.lua ~3590-3722), extending providerById:2632, and compute the optional provenance note from providerByModel:2659 + tile model/base_url.

#### Profile readiness badges without exposing secret values (api_key_set, masking, names-only listing)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** A "key ready / needs key" badge per agent/provider profile is genuinely useful in a spawn console: before you "Spawn from agent" you want to know, at a glance, whether the credential the profile depends on will actually resolve in the shell, or whether the spawn will silently 401 (exactly the failure mode the loginShellWrap comment at cc-core.lua:2750 documents — a missing authTokenEnv expands to "" and every gateway call 401s). This is the single most operationally valuable slice of the OpenHands feature and it maps cleanly onto an item L1 already queued: requiredEnv:[{name,description,required,default}] plus a pure core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv) preflight that checks NAME-presence only (todos.md ~L282). Rendering that as a green "key ready" vs amber "needs $MY_LITELLM_KEY" badge on the New-Session preflight and an optional tile/detail badge is a small, well-fitting addition. The structural "listing omits the value field so it can't leak" discipline is also a sound design rule Shepherd already honors and could state explicitly: provider/agent registry DTOs surface authTokenEnv (the NAME) and never a value.
- **Against:** The feature's actual substance — SecretStr, a context-checking field_serializer that masks to '**********' unless expose_secrets is set, CustomSecretWithoutValue, encryption_key.serialize_key, api_key=None + api_key_set on the single-profile GET — is entirely downstream of OpenHands STORING the user's API key (encrypted, per-user, in a DB it serves over a multi-tenant FastAPI app). All of that masking/expose-context/value-omission machinery exists ONLY to safely emit a secret the server holds. Shepherd structurally holds no secret: its hard scope is NO secrets storage; a provider profile stores authTokenEnv (an env-var NAME, not a value), cc-config.json holds no key, and the process never sees one (cc-core.lua:2620-2622). There is nothing to mask, no value to null out, no field to structurally omit a value from, no expose_secrets context to gate, no encryption key to serialize. Porting the masking/serializer layer would be building a guard for a danger Shepherd designed itself out of — pure dead complexity. Shepherd is already strictly safer than the pattern: it never ingests the secret in the first place.
- **Ruling:** Double-mismatch on the (b) axis: this feature is multi-user-server secret-storage hygiene. Its value depends on OpenHands holding encrypted per-user API keys and needing to mask them on the wire (SecretStr + expose_secrets field_serializer + value-omitting list DTOs + serialize_key). Shepherd's hard scope is NO secrets storage — it stores only env-var NAMES (authTokenEnv) that the spawned login shell expands, holds no key in cc-config.json, and its process never sees one; the OAuth usage token is read read-only and never logged. So the masking/serializer/expose-context machinery is DONT_ADD: there is no secret in-process to protect, and porting it would guard a risk Shepherd deliberately engineered away. But the feature's user-facing payoff — the api_key_set boolean rendered as a "key stored / needs key" badge plus a resolved base_url — does transfer, and it maps onto an item L1 already queued: requiredEnv + the pure core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv) NAME-presence preflight (todos.md ~L282), with base_url already surfaced via the gateway provider's baseUrl. Hence ADAPT/reinforces L1: do the readiness badge by name-presence, skip everything that assumes a stored value. The value-omitting-DTO discipline is already honored (provider cards expose authTokenEnv the NAME, never a key) and at most warrants a one-line design note, not a system.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's requiredEnv/missingEnv preflight: extend core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv) (queued sibling to providerById:2632 / resolveGateTools:2537) to return per-required-env {name, present:bool} computed by NAME-presence via loginShellWrap (never reading the value), and render it as a "key ready" (green) vs "needs $NAME" (amber) badge on the New-Session / Spawn-from-agent preflight and an optional off-by-default tile/detail badge (sibling to stale/hung). Surface the resolved base_url from the gateway provider's baseUrl on the same card. Adopt the structural rule explicitly (likely zero code, since already true): provider/agent registry DTOs returned to the panel JS expose authTokenEnv (the NAME) and never a value. Do NOT build SecretStr/field_serializer/expose_secrets/CustomSecretWithoutValue/serialize_key — Shepherd holds no secret to mask.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; ADAPT/reinforces-L1 holds. The feature is a bundle and the judge's split is correct and verified against the code. HALF 1 (the masking machinery: SecretStr, expose_secrets field_serializer, '**********' rendering, serialize_key, CustomSecretWithoutValue, value-omitting list DTOs, the api_key-null-but-api_key_set-true single-GET) is failure-mode (b) — multi-user-server secret-storage hygiene — and is correctly DONT_ADD. Verified: Shepherd stores NO secret value. The provider editor/DTO (claude-dashboard.lua:3787-3858) carries only label/kind/model/baseUrl/authTokenEnv(the NAME)/smallFastModel/headers, with the UI label at :3814 literally reading 'Auth-token env var NAME (not the key) — e.g. MY_LITELLM_KEY'. cc-core.lua:2618-2622 documents the invariant: 'Secrets are NEVER stored: a profile names an env var (authTokenEnv) that the spawned LOGIN SHELL expands at launch, so cc-config.json holds no key and Shepherd's own process never sees one.' There is no in-process secret to mask and no value field to omit — porting SecretStr/expose_secrets/serialize_key would guard a risk Shepherd deliberately engineered away. The 'value-omitting DTO discipline' is therefore already structurally honored (zero code), worth at most a one-line design note. HALF 2 (the user-facing payoff: an api_key_set boolean rendered as a 'key stored / needs key' badge + a resolved base_url) genuinely transfers and maps onto an already-queued L1 item — NAME-presence preflight via core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv) (todos.md:282-284), with base_url already surfaced from the gateway provider's baseUrl. This is not redundant: grep confirms missingEnv does NOT exist yet (only loginShellWrap, its NAME-expansion engine, exists at cc-core.lua:2754; providerById:2632 / resolveGateTools:2537 are the cited sibling primitives). So it's a real, queued, not-yet-built reinforcement of L1. Both downgrade directions fail. DONT_ADD fails because the readiness-badge payoff is real, local, and queued (the whole feature is not scope-violating/redundant). ADD fails because the residual is not a new standalone system — it maps onto the existing L1 requiredEnv/missingEnv preflight. A near-identical candidate ('Reusable named credentials store (encrypted + masked display)') was already adjudicated this exact way in docs/feature-mining/litellm.md:473 — encrypted-store+masking core = DONT_ADD, named-reference DRY layer onto provider profiles = thin ADAPT — which corroborates this ruling. Implementation: extend core.missingEnv to return per-required-env {name, present:bool} computed by NAME-presence via loginShellWrap, render as green 'key ready' vs amber 'needs $NAME' on the New-Session/spawn-from-agent preflight + optional off-by-default tile/detail badge (sibling to stale/hung), and surface base_url from the gateway profile on the same card. Do NOT build SecretStr/field_serializer/expose_secrets/CustomSecretWithoutValue/serialize_key.

#### Profile/secret name validation + count cap (safe charset, env-var-name rule, collision check)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** Shepherd is about to grow exactly the registries this guards. L1 (Agent Profiles registry, cc-agents.json) and the planned cc-mcp.json bring user-authored named records into the spawn path, and those names already flow to disk and onto the command line: a profile's authTokenEnv becomes `$VAR` injected into a shell prefix (cc-core.lua:2728-2729 providerEnv/envPrefix), skill/MCP/plugin names get dereferenced against ~/.claude/skills and config roots, and L1's requiredEnv:[{name,...}] (todos.md:282) is literally a list of user-supplied env-var names. An invalid env-var name there is a genuine correctness/shell-safety bug, and a duplicate profile name silently clobbers an existing saved agent. A small pure validator family — safe-charset name check, env-var-identifier fullmatch for any env-name field, and a collision check against the existing list — closes those holes deterministically in cc-core with zero hs.*, and is exactly what L1's own "Pre-flight validation/lint" and "Safe-path hardening" sub-items (todos.md:285-298) already promised. The count cap is also already Shepherd's house idiom (PRESET_CAP=20, TEMPLATE_CAP=50, RECENT_CAP=12), so a per-registry cap on agents/MCP records is a free extension.
- **Against:** Most of this feature is already shipped, just under different names, so copying it as-is would be redundant. safeFolderName (cc-core.lua:3727) already rejects empty/'.'/'..'/leading-dash and anything outside [%w%-_. ] — functionally OpenHands' ^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,64}$. templatePush/presetPush already trim, reject blanks, replace-same-name-in-place, and cap (lines 3604/3673). And the half of the OpenHands feature that is about SECRET names (validate_secret_name in secrets_models.py, the per-user secrets store it protects) is structurally impossible: Shepherd stores no secrets — it only stores the NAME of an env var that the spawned login shell expands, never the value. So the "secret name validation" framing and the 409/HTTP-conflict machinery (FastAPI typed errors, multi-user save()) ride entirely on the server+secrets-store half of the double mismatch and must be dropped.
- **Ruling:** The double mismatch splits this feature cleanly. The SERVER/SECRETS half — FastAPI Path/Field regex at the route, typed 409 HTTP errors, per-user save() limit, and Pydantic secret-name validation guarding a stored secrets table — is DONT_ADD: Shepherd has no server, no users, and (decisively) no secrets store; it injects env-var NAMES the shell expands, never values. But the MANAGEMENT-layer PATTERN underneath — constrain a saved record's name to a safe charset, cap how many you can save, refuse a name that would collide, and additionally require env-name fields to be legal shell identifiers — is real and lands squarely on Shepherd's registries. Two of the three sub-rules are already Shepherd idioms (safeFolderName's charset reject; the *_CAP constants), which is why this is ADAPT not ADD: the new work is (1) generalizing the existing name guard into a reusable validator shared by cc-agents.json / cc-mcp.json, (2) a collision/duplicate-name check (today saves silently replace-in-place — fine for presets, wrong once "save a new agent" should warn before clobbering), and (3) the one genuinely additive rule, an env-var-identifier fullmatch (^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$) for authTokenEnv and L1's requiredEnv[].name, since those names are emitted into a shell command and a bad one breaks the gateway launch. All of this is already enumerated inside L1 ("Pre-flight validation/lint" returning aggregated structured errors, "Safe-path hardening" validating against [A-Za-z0-9._-]), so it reinforces L1 rather than standing alone. Effort S: a handful of pure cc-core functions plus unit tests, no new UI surface beyond a rejection reason in the existing New-Session/registry flows.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's existing "Pre-flight validation/lint" + "Safe-path hardening" sub-items (todos.md:285-298). Add to cc-core: (1) M.safeRegistryName(name) generalizing safeFolderName (cc-core.lua:3727) to a [A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,64} fullmatch, shared by agentPush (the planned preset++ CRUD ~3640) and the cc-mcp.json validator; (2) M.envVarNameOk(name) = ^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$ fullmatch, called on provider authTokenEnv (consumed by providerEnv at cc-core.lua:2714) and on every L1 requiredEnv[].name BEFORE envPrefix (cc-core.lua:2738) emits it into the shell prefix; (3) a duplicate-name check in agentPush that returns a "would overwrite <name>" reason (mirroring newProjectPath's single-source rejection-reason pattern at cc-core.lua:3744) instead of silently replacing; (4) an AGENT_CAP / MCP_CAP constant alongside PRESET_CAP=20 / TEMPLATE_CAP=50. No 409/HTTP, no secret-value validation, no FastAPI — rejection surfaces as a reason string in the New-Session modal and the registry editor.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails; the ADAPT-reinforces-L1 ruling holds. I tried each common-error angle and none sinks the ADAPTED half the judge kept: (b) secrets/server: This is the obvious attack — the OpenHands feature is largely "Pydantic validation guarding a stored secrets table" + per-user save() 409s. But the judge already carved that half off as DONT_ADD. The KEPT half validates env-var NAMES, not values. Verified Shepherd genuinely emits those names into a shell: providerEnv (cc-core.lua:2714) builds "$"..authTokenEnv (2728-2729) and envPrefix (cc-core.lua:2738-2746) concatenates e.name straight into the command prefix with ZERO validation. A malformed authTokenEnv (e.g. "foo bar" or "a;rm") corrupts the prefix / breaks the gateway launch. So an env-var-identifier rule (^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$) for authTokenEnv and L1's requiredEnv[].name has a real LOCAL consequence — no secrets store needed. context.md (line 13) and todos.md:235 ("Secrets never stored... store the env-var NAME") confirm the no-secrets model the kept half respects. (a) executor/sandbox: N/A — all proposed work is pure cc-core functions (safeRegistryName, envVarNameOk, a cap constant, a collision check), no agent loop/sandbox. (c) fully redundant -> DONT_ADD: tested and rejected. It is NOT a parallel system (correctly ADAPT not ADD): the charset guard mirrors safeFolderName (3727), capping mirrors PRESET_CAP=20 (3646)/TEMPLATE_CAP=50 (3581), and aggregated pre-flight validation is already queued at todos.md:285-289; safe-path [A-Za-z0-9._-] at todos.md:297. But it is NOT 100% redundant either: (i) no env-var-identifier check exists anywhere (grep for envVarName/safeRegistryName/fullmatch found nothing) and authTokenEnv/requiredEnv[].name currently reach envPrefix unvalidated — genuinely new; (ii) saves silently clobber same-name in place (presetPush:2688 / templatePush:2613 both return saved=true, no warn) so a collision/would-overwrite reason is a real gap; (iii) even the charset rule needs a NEW safeRegistryName, since safeFolderName uses a different charset (allows spaces, bans leading '-') — not literal reuse. So real, non-redundant work remains, but it lands inside L1, hence ADAPT. (d) server plumbing mistaken for console feature: the FastAPI Path/Field + 409 IS server plumbing and is dropped; the judge maps the outcome to a rejection-reason string in the New-Session modal / registry editor, mirroring newProjectPath's single-source rejection-reason pattern (3744-3753) — a real local UX surface. Mapping verified accurate end to end: safeFolderName:3727, newProjectPath:3744, PRESET_CAP:3646, TEMPLATE_CAP:3581, providerEnv:2714, authTokenEnv:2728-2729, envPrefix:2738, providerById:2632, agentPush spec todos.md:249-252, requiredEnv todos.md:282, pre-flight todos.md:285-289, safe-path todos.md:297. Verdict stays ADAPT (reinforces L1), effort S.

#### Schema-drift / fail-safe config loading (skip one broken entry, keep the rest)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is pure local config-file hygiene with zero dependence on the executor/sandbox/server/secrets axis, so the OpenHands double mismatch doesn't bite. The NEED is real and growing: Shepherd persists a stack of human-curated JSON registries (cc-presets.json, cc-templates.json, cc-config.json, cc-labels.json, cc-groups.json, queues) and L1 is about to add cc-agents.json + cc-mcp.json with a much richer, longer-lived schema (skills[], mcpServers[], plugins[], versions[], requiredEnv[]). These files are hand- and git-edited and survive across app versions, so schema drift is a when-not-if. Today most readers are all-or-nothing: readPresets/readQueue/readTemplates do a single pcall(json.decode) over the WHOLE file and on any failure fall back to an EMPTY struct (claude-dashboard.lua ~738-746, ~466, ~734) — meaning one fat-fingered comma in entry #7 silently nukes all 20 of your saved presets. The fix exactly matches the candidate: validate per-entry on read and keep the survivors. presetList (cc-core.lua ~3658) already proves the pattern works — it iterates and skips any entry that isn't {name, absolute folder} — so generalizing it to the new registries is low-risk and directly protects the user's #1 stated interest (a directory of saved agents/skills/MCPs that must not vanish wholesale on drift).
- **Against:** Shepherd already substantially HAS this, so the marginal delta is thin enough to flirt with DONT_ADD-as-redundant. The strict-on-write / soft-on-read asymmetry the candidate touts is already shipped: presetPush hard-rejects a nameless/relative-folder entry (returns saved=false + a user alert) while presetList silently drops bad entries on read — strict in, soft out, exactly the StrictLLM-vs-base split. The per-entry skip loop is already the idiom in presetList, and L1's queued "Pre-flight validation/lint" validator family already promises aggregated structured errors that refuse spawn before emitting flags. The only genuine increment OpenHands adds is the explicit operator-facing warning (logger.warning / a surfaced "1 entry dropped" notice) instead of Shepherd's current silent drop — and even that risks being noise in a single-user console where the human edited the file and can just look. There's also a real anti-goal: making the WHOLE-FILE reader best-effort-merge instead of empty-fallback adds parsing complexity (JSON can't be salvaged entry-by-entry without a tolerant per-line/per-object decode), so a faithful copy of the Pydantic before-validator (which gets a pre-parsed dict) doesn't map cleanly onto Lua's monolithic json.decode.
- **Ruling:** The double mismatch is irrelevant here — there's no agent loop, no sandbox, no server, no users, no secrets; this is read-path config hygiene that lives entirely in pcall-guarded pure cc-core functions. The pattern is real and partially present, which lands it squarely on ADAPT rather than ADD (don't rebuild what presetList already does) or DONT_ADD (not redundant — the protection is uneven across registries and silent). Two concrete, bounded improvements are worth folding into L1: (1) generalize the presetList per-entry skip-and-keep discipline into the new richer registries (cc-agents.json/cc-mcp.json) with a per-registry known-fields allowlist so adding an optional field like versions[] never invalidates a whole record — L1 already calls for exactly this validator family; (2) add the one thing OpenHands has that Shepherd lacks: a surfaced warning when an entry is dropped (a count + names via the existing hs.alert/notification path, optionally a ledger event by:"config-drift"), so a silent vanish becomes a visible "skipped N invalid agent profiles." Note the Lua-vs-Pydantic caveat: OpenHands validates a pre-parsed dict per key, whereas Shepherd's json.decode is monolithic — so per-ENTRY salvage only works AFTER a successful whole-file decode; if the file itself is malformed JSON it still falls back wholesale (acceptable, and git history is the backstop). Scope the work to: structural decode succeeds, then drop-and-warn invalid records. This reinforces L1 rather than spawning a parallel system.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's "Pre-flight validation/lint" validator family. (1) Generalize presetList's per-entry skip (cc-core.lua ~3658) into agentList/mcpList read paths for cc-agents.json/cc-mcp.json: validate each record against a per-registry known-fields allowlist, keep valid ones, drop invalid — never empty the whole struct when only one record is bad. (2) Keep the existing strict write path (agentPush mirrors presetPush's reject-on-bad-name/folder, returning saved=false + alert) — strict-in/soft-out asymmetry already matches the candidate. (3) NEW increment: have the soft read path RETURN the count+names of dropped entries so the FX glue surfaces a one-time hs.alert/notification ("Shepherd: skipped 2 invalid agent profiles") and optionally appends a ledger event (by:"config-drift") — replacing today's silent drop in readPresets/readTemplates/readQueue (claude-dashboard.lua ~738/734/466). Caveat: whole-file json.decode stays pcall-guarded with empty fallback (a truly malformed file can't be salvaged per-entry); per-entry skip applies once the object decodes.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute and could not — the ADAPT ruling holds, and reinforces L1. None of the four common-error escape hatches apply. NO double mismatch. This is read-path config hygiene living entirely in pure, pcall-guarded cc-core functions. There is no agent loop, no Docker/remote sandbox executing actions, no FastAPI server, no users, no stored secrets. Confirmed by reading the actual code: presetList (cc-core.lua:3658) and templateList (cc-core.lua:3590) are pure list builders with zero hs.*; the read glue (readPresets dash:738, readTemplates dash:726, readQueue dash:463, loadLabels dash:498, loadGroups dash:511) just pcall core.json.decode over a local JSON file and fall back to an empty struct. NOT redundant, so NOT DONT_ADD. The pattern is only PARTIALLY present and unevenly applied. presetList/templateList do per-entry skip-and-keep (iterate, validate fields like name/folder, drop bad records, keep the rest). But readQueue/loadLabels/loadGroups only guard the WHOLE-FILE decode (pcall + type==table) with no per-entry salvage, and the richer L1 registries (cc-agents.json/cc-mcp.json) don't exist yet so they have no validators at all. The drop is also entirely SILENT in every case — no count/names surfaced. So there is a genuine, bounded gap: (1) generalize presetList's per-entry skip into the L1 registries with a per-registry known-fields allowlist (so an optional field like versions[] never invalidates a whole record), and (2) the one thing OpenHands has that Shepherd lacks — surface a one-time warning ("skipped N invalid agent profiles") via the existing hs.alert path (dash:212/1056) and optionally a ledger event by:"config-drift" (the by:"router"/by:"batch" tagging seam already exists, dash:5656). NOT ADD (don't over-promote). The strict-write/soft-read asymmetry already matches the candidate: presetPush/templatePush reject bad name/folder on the write path and return saved=false, while presetList/templateList are soft on read. Rebuilding what already exists would be wrong; the work is generalization + a surfaced warning, i.e. an extension/reinforcement, which is precisely ADAPT. Maps to a QUEUED item, confirming the reinforces-L1 framing. todos.md:285-289 ("Pre-flight validation/lint") already calls for a pure validator family sibling to presetList/templateList with "a per-registry known-fields allowlist so adding optional fields (e.g. versions[]) doesn't trip it." The feature folds straight into that line — no parallel system. The judge's Lua-vs-Pydantic caveat is accurate and important: core.json.decode is monolithic (dash:466/729/741), so per-ENTRY salvage only works AFTER a successful whole-file decode; a truly malformed file still falls back wholesale (acceptable — git history is the backstop). Scoping the work to "structural decode succeeds, then drop-and-warn invalid records" is correct. Net: this directly improves a local single-user console's robustness against its own config drift, with no execution/sandbox/server/secrets dependency. ADAPT (high), reinforces L1, holds.

#### Pluggable file-backed store with legacy auto-migration (settings/secrets)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The feature is two patterns stapled together, and the second one is genuinely worth stealing. (1) The store abstraction maps to something Shepherd has organically reinvented: a family of per-concern JSON documents (cc-config.json, cc-labels.json, cc-presets.json, cc-templates.json, cc-groups.json, cc-recent-dirs.json) each with a load-or-default helper (loadConfig at claude-dashboard.lua:5262 decodes-or-{}; FX.loadLabels/savePresets/loadTemplates follow the same shape). So the "one JSON document with a load/store helper per concern" idea is already validated as Shepherd's persistence model. (2) The far more valuable half is the legacy auto-migration: on upgrade, a pre-profiles settings file auto-seeds a 'Default' profile from the existing single config and marks it active so the new UI isn't empty. That is exactly the gap the QUEUED L1 Agent Profiles registry will hit — when "spawn from a saved agent" ships, an operator who already has cc-presets.json / a working spawn config should see a pre-seeded Default agent rather than an empty registry. Shepherd already proves it values this idiom: labels migrate cwd-keyed→projectKey-keyed (cc-core.lua:297), focus.popEditor back-compat-seeds both new flags (claude-dashboard.lua:3762), and overlayConfig (cc-core.lua:2498) preserves keep-subkeys across config rewrites. Adding a "seed Default agent profile from existing presets/spawn config on first L1 run" is a small, pure cc-core enrichment that makes the headline user-priority feature (saved agents) land smoothly.
- **Against:** The literal OpenHands feature is mostly DONT_ADD plumbing. The ABC + get_impl() DI + "swappable DB backend chosen by class name" + get_instance(user_id) exist solely to serve OpenHands' multi-user FastAPI server: per-user instances, a database backend for SaaS scale, and a parallel secrets_store.py / secrets.json. Shepherd is single-user, has no server, no DB, and structurally stores NO secrets (it resolves $ENV via the spawned shell, never persists keys) — so the entire pluggability/DI/secrets-store half is dead weight: there is no second backend to swap to and no user_id to key on. Shepherd already has a working file-store-per-concern with defaults-on-miss; an abstract SettingsStore base class with a class-name-selected backend would be ceremony with zero payoff in a Lua single-user app, and the secrets.json side is explicitly out of scope (a hard limit in context.md). Building "the store abstraction" as-is would be redundant-and-out-of-scope; only the migration-seed idea survives contact with Shepherd's primitives.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: the store-abstraction + DI + DB-backend + secrets.json half depends on the multi-user web server (per-user get_instance, pluggable persistence for SaaS) and on stored secrets — both structurally absent in Shepherd, so that half is DONT_ADD. But the candidate also carries a real management-layer pattern — upgrade-time legacy migration that seeds a Default profile so a newly-introduced profiles UI isn't empty — which transfers cleanly. Shepherd doesn't need a new store: loadConfig/loadLabels/loadPresets already ARE the file-backed default, and it already has the migration idiom (cwd→projectKey labels, focus.popEditor dual-seed, overlayConfig keep-subkeys). The right move is not to build a store abstraction (redundant) nor add secrets persistence (out of scope), but to fold the auto-migration-seed into the queued L1 Agent Profiles registry: when L1 introduces cc-agents.json + "spawn from a saved agent," seed an initial Default agent from the operator's existing presets/spawn config and mark it active. That reinforces L1 directly and serves the user's #1 stated interest (saved agents in a directory) by avoiding an empty-registry first-run. Hence ADAPT, reinforces L1, not a parallel system. Effort is S because it's one pure seed-on-first-run function in cc-core riding L1's already-planned schema and Shepherd's existing migration patterns — no new store, no IO beyond what L1 brings.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 (Agent Profiles registry, todos.md:242). When L1's cc-agents.json + agentList/agentPush land, add a pure cc-core seed-on-first-run: if cc-agents.json is absent/empty AND a usable prior config exists (cc-presets.json entries and/or the current spawn defaults: folder/provider/model/permMode in cc-config.json), synthesize one 'Default' agent profile from it and mark it active, so the "Spawn from a saved agent" UI isn't empty on first open. Model it on the existing migration idioms — labels cwd→projectKey (cc-core.lua:297), focus.popEditor dual-seed (claude-dashboard.lua:3762), overlayConfig keep-subkeys (cc-core.lua:2498) — and the load-or-default store helpers (loadConfig:5262, FX.loadPresets/savePresets). Pure logic in cc-core, IO via the existing FX.readFile/writeFile. Explicitly do NOT port the ABC/get_impl DI, the class-name-selected DB backend, get_instance(user_id), or secrets_store.py/secrets.json (no server, no users, no stored secrets).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted but the ADAPT ruling holds. The feature is a clean double-half and the judge already split it correctly. Half one (the store abstraction: ABC load/store/get_instance(user_id) + DI + class-name-selected DB backend + secrets.json) is the OpenHands double mismatch — per-user get_instance and pluggable persistence exist only to serve the multi-tenant SaaS web server, and secrets.json stores API keys. Shepherd has no server, no users, and never stores secrets (todos.md:235, providers store the env-var NAME and expand via the spawned shell). It also ALREADY has the file-backed-default this half describes: FX.readPresets (claude-dashboard.lua:738-746) and loadConfig are the load-or-empty store, with cc-presets.json/cc-templates.json as operator-data JSON. So half one is correctly DONT_ADD/redundant. But the feature is not only that half. Half two — upgrade-time legacy migration that synthesizes a 'Default' profile from the prior single config and marks it active so the newly-introduced profiles UI isn't empty — is a genuine management-layer pattern that passes all four refutation tests: (a) it is pure data transformation (read old config -> write one profile record), no agent loop or sandbox; (b) in adaptation the secrets coupling is shed — it seeds from cc-presets.json / spawn defaults (folder/provider/model/permMode), never key values; (c) it is NOT redundant — I confirmed Shepherd has no existing first-run/seed-Default logic (grep for firstRun/seed-Default/empty-registry found nothing) — and it is NOT a parallel system because it is wholly subordinate to queued L1 (cc-agents.json + agentList/agentPush + 'spawn from a saved agent', todos.md:242-301), which must land first; (d) it is user-facing (de-empties L1's first-open UI), not server plumbing. Shepherd has the exact migration idioms to model it on: the popEditor dual-seed back-compat (claude-dashboard.lua:3762-3764, literally 'seed both so the new toggles aren't unset'), overlayConfig SETTINGS_KEEP_SUBKEYS (cc-core.lua:2491-2510), and labels cwd->projectKey legacy fallback (cc-core.lua:296-297). The refutation prompt's own rule says ADD->ADAPT when a candidate is really an extension/reinforcement of an existing/queued feature; that is exactly this. Effort S: one pure seed-on-first-run function in cc-core riding L1's already-planned schema, IO via existing FX.readFile/writeFile. Verdict: ADAPT, reinforces L1.

#### Named custom secrets store (name + value + description, CRUD, injected as env vars)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** A fleet of saved agents (L1) legitimately need named env vars — gateway tokens, MCP server creds, API keys — and operators forget which ones a given agent expects. The valuable, transferable core of this feature is its METADATA shape: a named slot carrying a human description and an env-var name, plus a way to surface "this is what the agent needs." That maps directly onto the user's #1 stated priority (pre-configured agents saved into a directory) and onto the already-queued L1 sub-item requiredEnv: [{name, description, required, default}] with core.missingEnv() doing NAME-presence preflight. CRUD-with-create-or-overwrite, rename collision guards, and name__contains search are reasonable UX for editing that declared set in a small registry (cc-agents.json / cc-mcp.json), exactly the editing pattern presetPush/presetList/templateList already establish. So the need is real and there is a clean home for the safe part.
- **Against:** The feature's defining value is the part Shepherd structurally cannot host. It persists the actual secret VALUE (SecretStr) to secrets.json and, via get_env_vars()+expose_secrets(), injects those literal values into a runtime it controls. Shepherd has NO such runtime to inject into (it does not run the agent loop or manage a sandbox), and its single hardest invariant is no stored secrets: context.md:13, the provider comment at cc-core.lua:2618-2622, and todos.md:235 all state secrets are NEVER stored — Shepherd stores only the env-var NAME (authTokenEnv) and lets the spawned interactive login shell expand $VAR from ~/.zshrc, so its own process never sees a key (providerEnv marks secret refs and emits "$VAR", loginShellWrap does zsh -lic). Adopting a value store + a get_env_vars dump is precisely the multi-user-server secrets subsystem the double mismatch rules out. Build it as-is and you violate the no-secrets scope limit and re-introduce a key-handling surface the design deliberately refuses.
- **Ruling:** Clean split on the OpenHands double mismatch. The storage/injection half belongs to side (b) — a per-user secrets store that dumps values into an agent runtime — which Shepherd cannot and will not host (no executor to inject into, hard no-secrets invariant). That half is DONT_ADD. But the prompt itself identifies the salvageable remnant: the metadata pattern (a named slot with a description and an env-var name, surfaced safely). That remnant is not new — it is already queued as L1's requiredEnv sub-item with the pure core.missingEnv NAME-only check, which is exactly Shepherd's existing authTokenEnv philosophy (store the NAME, expand from the shell, never the value). Because the genuine need extends an already-queued primitive rather than warranting a parallel system, the rule is ADAPT reinforcing L1, not a flat DONT_ADD and certainly not ADD. The CRUD/search ergonomics transfer only insofar as they edit env-var-name declarations on agent/MCP records — never values.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's existing requiredEnv work — do NOT build a secrets store. Keep the metadata shape only: extend the per-profile requiredEnv: [{name, description, required, default}] (todos.md:282) so each entry carries the human description (this feature's safe contribution) and an env-var NAME (NOT a value). Reuse the providerEnv authTokenEnv pattern (cc-core.lua:2710-2733): records hold "$VAR", loginShellWrap/envPrefix let the spawned login shell expand it from ~/.zshrc; Shepherd never stores or sees the value. Editing UX (the CRUD/rename-guard/name__contains parts) attaches to the cc-agents.json / cc-mcp.json registries via the established presetPush/presetList replace-in-place + validator family (~3590-3722), editing NAME+description records, not secrets. Surfacing = core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv) NAME-presence-only check → New-Session preflight warning + optional tile badge (already specced at todos.md:282-284). Explicitly DROP: secrets.json persistence, SecretStr storage, get_env_vars()/expose_secrets value dump, any router-style load-mutate-restore of secret VALUES.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt FAILS — the ADAPT (reinforces L1) ruling holds. I tried to push it to DONT_ADD on the OpenHands side-(b) mismatch, but the split survives scrutiny. THE OUT-OF-SCOPE HALF (correctly killed): The feature's load-bearing behavior is persisting secret VALUES (SecretStr in secrets.json) and INJECTING them into a running agent runtime as env vars. Shepherd has neither: (1) no agent runtime to inject into — it runs no agent loop; Claude Code drives the session, Shepherd only watches status files + steers via keystrokes/hooks; (2) a hard no-secrets-storage invariant (context.md:13). Grep confirms ZERO secret-value storage: the only 'secret' code is providerEnv/envPrefix emitting a $VAR REFERENCE (cc-core.lua:2728-2729 `put('ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN','$'..authTokenEnv,true)`; :2742 emits it double-quoted) that the SPAWNED LOGIN SHELL expands from ~/.zshrc via loginShellWrap (:2754-2757). No secrets.json, no setenv, no value persistence anywhere. That half is DONT_ADD — and the judge's mapping already DROPS it explicitly (secrets.json/SecretStr/get_env_vars/expose_secrets/value dump). WHY IT CANNOT GO TO A FLAT DONT_ADD: The feature carries one genuinely salvageable, in-scope remnant — the metadata pattern: a named slot with a human DESCRIPTION and an env-var NAME (not a value). That is not invented; it is already QUEUED. todos.md:282-284 specs `requiredEnv: [{name, description, required, default}]` on profiles + cc-mcp.json records, with pure `core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv)` doing a NAME-presence-only check → New-Session preflight warning + optional tile badge. The 'description' subfield is exactly this feature's safe contribution, and it sits squarely on Shepherd's existing authTokenEnv philosophy (cc-core.lua:2620-2621: store the NAME, the login shell expands the value, Shepherd never sees it). The CRUD/rename-guard/name__contains ergonomics map onto the existing presetPush/presetList replace-in-place validator family (cc-core.lua:3658-3722) — editing NAME+description records, never values. WHY NOT ADD: The genuine value is purely an extension of L1's already-queued requiredEnv sub-item, so a standalone parallel 'secrets registry' would be wrong; reinforcement of L1 is the right call. ADAPT (high), reinforces L1, is correct.

#### MCP config assembly + UI<->SDK round-trip serialization  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The feature splits into two halves and the SERIALIZATION half is directly useful. L1 already commits Shepherd to a `cc-mcp.json` MCP server registry with transport-typed records `{transport(stdio|sse|http), command, args, url, allowedTools, authTokenEnv}` plus "a pure validator + mcp-config-file builder" that emits `--mcp-config` / `--strict-mcp-config` flags at spawn. OpenHands' `parseMcpConfig`/`toSdkMcpConfig`/`getUniqueName` is the cleanest available prior art for the exact shape Shepherd must produce: it classifies each server by transport into typed arrays (the registry's stored form), reverses that back into the canonical on-disk `{mcpServers:{name:{...}}}` blob (the literal format the `--mcp-config` file the CLI reads expects), preserves stable server NAMES across edits, and generates collision-free names only for new servers — which is precisely L1's "MCP picker, attach to a profile, name-validate, dedupe" need plus the "safe-path hardening: name resolvers reject names outside their root" sub-bullet. The merge-layering idea (a fleet-default set of servers + per-profile/per-session custom servers deep-merged) is a genuinely useful refinement to L1: it lets a profile inherit a baseline MCP set and add to it, instead of every profile re-listing servers. And the fail-soft principle (a malformed custom MCP entry must not block the spawn) is operationally right for a fleet console — it maps onto L1's pre-flight validator, just reshaped to skip-and-warn rather than hard-refuse for the optional-MCP case. This is round-trip JSON munging with zero executor/sandbox/server/secret dependency, so it transfers cleanly.
- **Against:** The headline half — "assemble the config and HAND IT TO THE AGENT at conversation start, injecting the built-in 'default' OpenHands system server" — is pure (a)-side double-mismatch plumbing. The whole point of `_add_system_mcp_servers`/`_configure_llm_and_mcp` is that OpenHands RUNS the agent loop in-process and feeds it a live MCP client config; Shepherd runs no agent, opens no MCP clients, and has no in-process conversation to inject into. Native Claude Code owns MCP execution end to end and already reads project `.mcp.json` and its own `~/.claude.json` registry. The "inject a default system server" concept has no Shepherd analog at all (Shepherd has no server it would inject). The deep-merge-and-hand-off lifecycle, the SDK `mcpServers` wrapping done at agent-start, and the silent try/except fallback that lets a half-broken config still boot the agent are all artifacts of being the executor. Worse, OpenHands' SILENT fail-soft (drop the bad custom config, log a sanitized version, start anyway) is a mild anti-pattern for Shepherd, whose entire ethos is fail-loud + refuse-with-the-full-error-list (L1's validator "refuses spawn/attach with the full list before emitting any flag"). Copying the assembly half as-is would be both impossible (no agent) and philosophically wrong (silent degradation). Only the data-transform half survives, and that data-transform is already enumerated inside L1, so there is no net-new system here — at most a small enrichment.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch by halves. Half 1 (config assembly + inject system server + deep-merge + wrap-as-SDK-shape + hand-to-agent, fail-soft) derives its value entirely from OpenHands being the in-process executor that runs the agent and opens MCP clients — the (a) side. Shepherd never hands config to an agent; it emits a `--mcp-config` file and lets native Claude Code do MCP. The "default system server" injection has no analog. That half is DONT_ADD on its own. Half 2 (parseMcpConfig/toSdkMcpConfig/getUniqueName — transport classification, name-stable round-trip, collision-free dedupe) is pure JSON transform with no executor/sandbox/server/secret dependency and is exactly the work L1 already commits to: the `cc-mcp.json` transport-typed registry, its "mcp-config-file builder", the modal MCP picker, name validation, and safe-path name hardening. So the underlying NEED (build the canonical `{mcpServers:{...}}` file from a typed registry and round-trip names across edits) is real and reshapes cleanly onto L1's primitives. Net: ADAPT, reinforces L1, not a new system. Two design notes carry over from the FOR/AGAINST: (1) adopt the merge-layering idea (fleet-default MCP set + per-profile additive servers) as a small L1 enrichment; (2) explicitly REJECT OpenHands' SILENT fail-soft in favor of Shepherd's fail-loud/refuse-with-full-error-list stance for required entries, while allowing skip-with-warning only for explicitly-optional servers. High confidence because the queued L1 spec already names every load-bearing piece (transport types, file builder, name dedupe/hardening); this candidate just supplies a vetted reference implementation and one schema refinement.
- **How (Shepherd):** Folds into L1's MCP server registry. (1) `parseMcpConfig`/`toSdkMcpConfig` -> the L1 "pure validator + mcp-config-file builder": a pure cc-core function that takes resolved `cc-mcp.json` records (the transport-typed arrays) and emits the canonical `{mcpServers:{<name>:{command,args,...}}}` JSON that FX writes to a temp `--mcp-config` file at spawn (threaded through `spawnFlags` ~cc-core.lua:3001, consumed by `spawnSpec` ~cc-core.lua:3061). (2) `getUniqueName`/name-dedupe -> L1's "MCP picker attachable to a profile" + the "safe-path hardening: name resolvers validate `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` and reject names resolving outside root"; preserve stored record names on edit, mint collision-free names only for new records. (3) The merge idea -> add an L1 enrichment: a fleet-default MCP set deep-merged with a profile's `mcpServers[]` in the config-by-reference resolver, so profiles inherit a baseline. (4) Fail-soft -> map to L1's pre-flight validator but INVERT the stance: required servers fail loud (refuse spawn with the aggregated error list), only explicitly-optional servers skip-with-warning. DROP entirely: the "default system server" injection, the agent-start hand-off / SDK in-process wrapping, and the silent try/except continue.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails; ADAPT (reinforces L1) holds. I verified the codebase: there is NO cc-mcp.json, NO --mcp-config threading, and spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) emits only --permission-mode/--remote-control today, so L1's MCP work is genuinely queued, not shipped — this candidate is not redundant-with-shipped. The judge correctly splits the double-half feature and amputates the (a)-trap half. Half 1 (assemble config, inject the 'default' OpenHands SYSTEM server, deep-merge, wrap as SDK {mcpServers:{...}}, HAND TO THE AGENT at start, SILENT fail-soft) is pure in-process-executor value: it exists only because OpenHands runs the agent loop and opens MCP clients itself. Shepherd runs no agent loop and opens no clients — it emits a --mcp-config file native Claude Code consumes. The judge DROPS that half entirely (system-server injection, agent hand-off, SDK in-process wrapping, silent try/except). That is the right call and I cannot revive it. Half 2 (parseMcpConfig/toSdkMcpConfig/getUniqueName: transport classification, name-stable round-trip, collision-free dedupe) survives every skeptic test: it is pure JSON transform — no executor, no sandbox, no server, no multi-user auth, no stored secrets (auth rides authTokenEnv = an env-var NAME, never a value, consistent with Shepherd's no-secrets rule). And it is exactly the work L1 already names: todos.md:258-260 'transport(stdio|sse|http)... Pure validator + mcp-config-file builder. MCP picker in the modal; attachable to a profile'; :290-293 'config-by-reference resolver: the profile stores NAMES; ONE pure resolver dereferences ... mcpServers[] against ... cc-mcp.json'; :297-298 'Safe-path hardening: skill/MCP/plugin name resolvers validate against [A-Za-z0-9._-]'; :285-289 'Pre-flight validation/lint ... refuses spawn/attach with the full list'. The mapped primitives exist in cc-core.lua (presetList/presetPush 3658/3673, providerById 2632, resolveGateTools 2537, atomic FX.writeFile 1064), and the CLI exposes --mcp-config per context.md. So this is not a NEW capability (rules out ADD) and the surviving NEED — build the canonical {mcpServers:{...}} file from a typed registry and round-trip names across edits — is real and reshapes onto L1 (rules out DONT_ADD). The two carry-over notes are sound L1 enrichments, not new systems: (1) fleet-default MCP set deep-merged with per-profile mcpServers[] in the config-by-ref resolver; (2) INVERT OpenHands' silent fail-soft to Shepherd's fail-loud — required servers refuse spawn with the aggregated error list, only explicitly-optional servers skip-with-warning (matches the existing pre-flight-validator stance). Net: ADAPT, high confidence, reinforces L1; the candidate supplies a vetted reference implementation plus one schema refinement.

#### Per-conversation MCP auth + built-in tool-serving server (session key + conversation-ID headers; Tavily search proxy)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The transferable kernel is real and squarely matches the user's #1 stated interest ("pre-configured agents... same with skills and MCPs"): an agent should be given a set of MCP servers to connect to — including a *hosted/remote* one like Tavily web-search — without baking a raw key into the spawn. That maps directly onto the already-queued L1 cc-mcp.json registry: named records {id,label,transport(stdio|sse|http),command,args,url,allowedTools,authTokenEnv} emitted at spawn as --mcp-config. The "remote search MCP" is a concrete, compelling entry for that registry (transport=http/sse, url=mcp.tavily.com, authTokenEnv naming the key var). The secret-hygiene instinct behind the proxy — keep the API key out of the agent's reach — is exactly what L1 already encodes via the authTokenEnv NAME pattern (resolved by the spawned login shell, never stored), so the *motivation* validates an existing design choice. The claude CLI's --mcp-config/--strict-mcp-config makes the flag-wiring feasible today.
- **Against:** The feature's distinctive mechanism is a textbook DOUBLE MISMATCH on both axes. (a) Executor/sandbox: OpenHands HOSTS its own FastMCP server, injects it into the agent's MCP config, and the in-process agent loop connects to it — Shepherd hosts no server, runs no agent loop, and the native Claude Code session owns its own MCP connections; there is nothing for Shepherd to serve tools to. (b) Server/auth/secrets: the X-Session-API-Key + X-OpenHands-ServerConversation-ID headers are multi-tenant request authorization/attribution for a shared FastAPI server with per-user auth — Shepherd is single-user, has no server, no users, no session keys, and scopes 'which conversation' for free via filesystem projectKey/session_id. The Tavily create_proxy exists specifically to keep an API key SERVER-SIDE so it never reaches the sandbox — Shepherd's hard scope limit is NO secrets storage and NO proxying/API calls; it would do the inverse (name the env var, let the login shell expand it). Even the tavily_* namespacing is redundant — Claude Code namespaces MCP tools itself. Building any of the server/auth/proxy half is structurally impossible and out of scope.
- **Ruling:** Split the feature: Half A (host-your-own FastMCP server serving the VCS tool set + a Tavily proxy that keeps the key server-side) and Half B (per-conversation/per-session auth headers) are both DONT_ADD — they need the in-process executor connecting to a server, a server to authenticate against, multiple users, and stored/proxied secrets, none of which Shepherd has. The ONLY surviving need is 'point a spawned agent at MCP servers, including a remote hosted one,' which is already the queued L1 cc-mcp.json registry. So the verdict is ADAPT/reinforces-L1, not a new system: add a remote-transport (http/sse + url + authTokenEnv) MCP record type to the L1 registry and use Tavily as the canonical example. Drop the FastMCP host, the create_proxy mount, the two headers, the session-key dependency, and any server-side key storage. The secret stays a NAME (authTokenEnv) resolved by the spawned shell — the opposite of the proxy model. This is a small enrichment of an already-large item, not standalone work.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's MCP server registry (todos.md ~258-260; cc-mcp.json). Concretely: (1) ensure the cc-mcp.json record schema's transport supports http/sse with a url field and an authTokenEnv NAME field (already specified) — add Tavily as a documented example record {transport:http, url:mcp.tavily.com, authTokenEnv:TAVILY_API_KEY, allowedTools}. (2) The L1 pure mcp-config-file builder emits this as a --mcp-config entry at spawn via spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) / spawnSpec (cc-core.lua:3061). (3) authTokenEnv expands through loginShellWrap/envPrefix (cc-core.lua:2714/2738/2754) — the existing providerEnv secret pattern — so no key is stored or proxied. (4) requiredEnv preflight (core.missingEnv) warns if TAVILY_API_KEY is absent. No header injection, no server, no proxy. allowedTools maps to the record's allowedTools (claude already namespaces tools).
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute the ADAPT ruling and could not. The full OpenHands feature has three substantive parts, and the judge correctly strips all three as out-of-scope: (1) the host-your-own FastMCP server serving the VCS tool set needs a network SERVER + an in-process executor connecting to it (Shepherd has neither — context.md:13 "No network, no multi-user, no secrets"); (2) the Tavily proxy that mounts Tavily's hosted MCP server-side so the key "never reaches the sandbox" needs a SANDBOX + a server-side PROXY that holds/proxies the secret (Shepherd runs no agent loop, no sandbox, and stores no secrets); (3) the X-Session-API-Key / X-OpenHands-ServerConversation-ID headers need a server to authenticate against and multi-tenant conversation scoping (Shepherd is single-user, no server). All three are common-error (a) executor/sandbox and (b) server/multi-user/stored-secrets — DONT_ADD. What survives is exactly the judge's kernel: "point a spawned agent at a remote hosted MCP server, key stays a NAME." I verified this maps onto already-queued L1, and onto real code: the cc-mcp.json schema is queued at todos.md:258-260 with `transport(stdio|sse|http)`, `url`, `allowedTools`, `authTokenEnv` ALREADY listed — i.e. remote sse/http transport + url + a secret-NAME field are already specified. The secret-NAME pattern (the inverse of OpenHands' server-side proxy) is real in cc-core.lua: providerEnv stores the env-var NAME and emits `$VAR` (line 2728-2729, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN = "$"..authTokenEnv`, secret=true), envPrefix renders it (2738), loginShellWrap expands it via the spawned interactive login shell (2754) — no key ever stored or proxied. spawnFlags (3001) and spawnSpec (3061) are the real flag-emission points (today they only emit --permission-mode/--remote-control; --mcp-config emission is queued, not built). The requiredEnv/missingEnv preflight is documented in L1 (todos.md:282-284) but not yet implemented (grep for missingEnv/cc-mcp/mcpConfig in cc-core returns nothing — L1 is genuinely unbuilt). Could this be downgraded ADAPT->DONT_ADD as pure redundancy with L1? No: the remote-transport schema is already covered, but Tavily-as-canonical-remote-MCP example + documenting the remote-hosted path (key resolved as a NAME by the spawned shell, with requiredEnv preflight warning) is NOT yet written anywhere (no "Tavily"/"remote/hosted MCP" reference exists in todos.md or docs/ — only the generic transport enum). Per the rubric, ADD->ADAPT when it is "really an extension/reinforcement of an existing/queued feature." This is precisely a small reinforcement of L1's MCP registry, not a new system. Strip the FastMCP host, the create_proxy mount, the two headers, and any server-side key storage; keep only the remote (http/sse + url + authTokenEnv NAME) MCP record using Tavily as the documented example. ADAPT/reinforces-L1 holds.

#### MCP tool-call observation rendering in chat (arguments + output, collapsible JSON, approve/reject)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The user has explicitly named MCP as a priority ("pre-configured agents... same with skills and MCPs"), and Shepherd already touches every seam this feature lives on. MCP tools flow through the SAME PreToolUse approval gate as every other tool (cc-approve.sh), so an operator already gets approve/deny on an MCP call — but today it is rendered identically to a Bash call: a single truncated SUMMARY line pulled from .tool_input.command/.file_path/.url (cc-approve.sh:104-107), with no awareness that this is `mcp__github__create_pr` carrying a structured arguments object. A richer, MCP-aware card would: (a) parse the `mcp__<server>__<tool>` name into server + tool, (b) pretty-print/collapse the full tool_input JSON in the approval surface and the ledger detail row instead of a one-line truncation, and (c) badge it against the L1 MCP inventory so the operator knows WHICH configured server is being hit. The ledger already records tool_request {tool, summary} (cc-status.sh:270-273) and renders it via narrateEvent/timeline (cc-core.lua:646/686), so an MCP-detail enrichment slots into shipped render paths with no new infrastructure. This is exactly the kind of human-in-the-loop transparency that is Shepherd's reason to exist — when an agent reaches outside the box via MCP, the operator should see what it's asking for clearly before approving.
- **Against:** The candidate is fundamentally an OBSERVATION-stream renderer: its core value (per the evidence) is showing the tool's OUTPUT as collapsible JSON with success/failure styling — ReactJsonView of extras.arguments AND `content`, routed by isMcpObservation through GenericEventMessage. That output/observation half hits the double mismatch dead-on: Shepherd runs no agent loop and streams no actions/observations. MCP tool RESULTS are never written to ~/.claude/cc-status/<id>.json nor passed to any hook Shepherd reads — they exist only inside the Claude Code session's own chat UI/transcript, which Shepherd watches for status, not result content. Reproducing "output as collapsible JSON, success/failure" would require the executor Shepherd structurally lacks, or scraping/parsing arbitrary MCP result blocks out of the transcript jsonl (brittle, and redundant — the user already sees the result in the real session window Shepherd can jump them to). The approve/reject + arguments-view halves are also largely REDUNDANT: the gate already gives approve/deny on MCP calls and already extracts the tool input.
