So the genuinely-new, in-scope delta is narrow (pretty-print MCP args + parse the server/tool name), and the most eye-catching part of the OpenHands feature (the observation/output pane) is the part Shepherd can't and shouldn't host.
- **Ruling:** Split the feature on the double mismatch. (1) Approve/reject on an MCP call: ALREADY SHIPPED — MCP is just-another-tool through cc-approve.sh's PreToolUse gate; do not re-add. (2) Rendering the tool's OUTPUT/observation as collapsible JSON with success/failure: DONT_ADD in isolation — it needs the executor's action/observation stream, which Shepherd has no access to (results aren't in the status files or hook payloads Shepherd reads; they live in the session's own UI). (3) The salvageable kernel: when the tool awaiting approval (or logged in the ledger) is an MCP tool, render a richer MCP-AWARE card — parse mcp__server__tool, show full tool_input as pretty/collapsible JSON instead of the current one-line truncation, badge against the configured server. That kernel is real, in-scope, leans into the user's stated MCP priority, and reinforces L1's MCP server registry / inventory badge (cc-mcp.json) rather than spawning a parallel system. Net verdict: ADAPT — fold the args-rendering kernel into the gate's approval surface + ledger detail under L1, and drop the output/observation pane.
- **How (Shepherd):** Reinforces L1 (MCP server registry + inventory badge). Concretely: (1) Add a pure helper in cc-core.lua, e.g. core.parseMcpTool(name) -> {server, tool, isMcp} matching `^mcp__([^_]+)__(.+)$`, plus a pretty-printer for tool_input JSON (reuse the existing jsString/JSON helpers already in cc-core). (2) In cc-approve.sh / the panel's approval detail, when isMcp, surface server+tool and the full canonicalized tool_input (it already does `jq -cS '.tool_input'` at line 114 for SIG) as a collapsible pretty block instead of the single SUMMARY line (cc-approve.sh:104-107). (3) In the ledger, enrich the tool_request event (cc-status.sh:270-273) with the parsed mcp server and render the MCP badge in narrateEvent/timeline (cc-core.lua:646/686). (4) Cross-link the server to the L1 cc-mcp.json inventory badge so an unknown/unconfigured MCP server is flagged. Explicitly DROP the OpenHands observation/output pane (extras.content / ReactJsonView of results) — that data isn't available to a watch-only console.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt FAILS — the ADAPT (high, reinforces L1) ruling holds. I verified the three-way split against the actual code. (1) Approve/reject on an MCP call is ALREADY SHIPPED. cc-approve.sh is a tool-agnostic PreToolUse gate: it gates whatever is in GATE_TOOLS (cc-approve.sh:28,97-100) and SIGs/approves by tool name + tool_input digest (cc-approve.sh:104-116). An MCP tool name (mcp__server__tool) flows through this path identically to Bash/Edit. Re-adding approve/reject would be redundant (error c). (2) The OUTPUT/observation pane is correctly DONT_ADD — it needs the executor's action/observation stream Shepherd cannot see (error a). Confirmed conclusively: the PostToolUse hook (cc-status.sh:119-131) sets STATUS=working and NEVER reads .tool_response/.tool_result; no status field or ledger event carries tool output. Rendering tool results "as collapsible JSON, success/failure" depends on data that lives only in the session's own UI — a watch-only console has no access. So the OpenHands-flavored observation pane (ReactJsonView of results) is genuinely dead-on-arrival. (3) A real, in-scope kernel survives skepticism: render a richer MCP-AWARE approval/ledger card. The data it needs (tool_input) IS already in the PreToolUse/PermissionRequest payload Shepherd reads. Today an unrecognized tool collapses to a one-line summary = just the tool name (cc-status.sh:52 fallback; cc-approve.sh:115), and the panel shows only "Wants: <summary>" (claude-dashboard.lua:4346-4347). Parsing mcp__server__tool and pretty-printing the full tool_input as a collapsible block is a genuine improvement to the LOCAL human-in-the-loop approval surface, and leans into the user's stated MCP priority. The judge maps the kernel onto L1's MCP server registry / inventory badge (cc-mcp.json). Caveat I confirmed: cc-mcp.json, cc-agents.json, and ANY mcp__/parseMcp/MCP reference do NOT exist anywhere in the codebase yet — L1 is QUEUED, not shipped (todos.md:242-301). But that is exactly the prescribed handling: the brief says when an OpenHands feature matches queued Lx work, the verdict is ADAPT with reinforces=Lx rather than proposing a parallel system. The judge did this correctly. Therefore ADD is wrong (the whole feature is not wholesale addable — its output pane is impossible and its approve/reject is already shipped) and DONT_ADD is too strong (a genuine, data-available, in-scope kernel exists that reinforces queued L1 and the user's MCP priority). ADAPT is the right verdict; the refutation does not stand. Relevant files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-approve.sh (gate, lines 97-116), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-status.sh (summarize_tool 39-55; posttooluse 119-131; ledger tool_request 268-273), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua (narrateEvent 670-692), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/claude-dashboard.lua (pending render 4345-4349), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md (L1 242-301).

#### Meta-skills that interview-then-author a definition (agent-builder, add_agent, add_repo_inst, onboarding, /remember)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is the purest expression of the user's #1 stated priority: "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs." OpenHands' agent-builder/add_agent are exactly the authoring front-end that L1 (the Agent Profiles registry, cc-agents.json) lacks — L1 specs the *schema* and the *spawn* but not how a profile gets created. add_agent's frontmatter-default rules (type=knowledge, version=1.0.0, agent=CodeActAgent, triggers) map almost line-for-line onto Shepherd's known-skill frontmatter (name/display_title/description, todos.md:230) and L1's structured profile {name, role/goal/backstory, provider, model, permMode, skills[], mcpServers[], versions[]}. Crucially, NONE of these five skills need the executor or sandbox to deliver their value: they are *prompts that drive a Claude session to interview-then-write-a-file*, which is identical in kind to Shepherd's already-shipped auditReviewPrompt (cc-core.lua:715) and the Improve button — Shepherd composes a task prompt in pure cc-core and pastes it into a real Claude Code GUI session, which does the interview and the authoring natively. The output artifacts (a cc-agents.json profile, a ~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md, a .openhands-equivalent repo CONTEXT.md → L1's per-agent contextDir/memoryFile at todos.md:299) land right where L1's read-mostly enumerators already look. /remember reinforces the ledger: it is auditReviewPrompt's write-mode sibling (review recent ledger/transcript activity → list exact items numbered → persist confirmed durable knowledge), and its "ALWAYS ask for confirmation, numbered" guardrail is native to Shepherd's human-in-the-loop reason-to-exist. /onboard's guardrails (<=5 questions, <=8-commit steps, never-push-to-main, acceptance criteria) become a high-value seedPrompt template under L3 — a parameterized, versioned definition the operator renders before spawn. So a single small "author from a guided prompt" seam (a curated set of seed-prompt/templates that L1 spawn-from-agent and L3 render can emit) turns these five OpenHands skills into Shepherd-native authoring of the very directory of agents/skills the user asked for.
- **Against:** The literal OpenHands skills are multi-turn interactive agent runs: agent-builder conducts a one-question-at-a-time conversation, add_repo_inst browses the repo and parses .github/ CI workflows, /onboard asks up to 5 progressive questions, /remember diffs recent actions — every one of these is an in-process agent LOOP that reads context, decides the next question, and writes files. Shepherd structurally has no executor and no agent loop; it cannot conduct an interview or read/parse a repo itself, so it can never *be* these skills — at best it hands a prompt to a session that already has those capabilities, which means most of the candidate's machinery (the interview state, the CI-workflow parsing, the file-writing) lives in Claude Code, not Shepherd. That makes the genuine Shepherd-side delta thin: a handful of curated seed-prompt strings. There is a real risk of scope-bleed — trying to model "interview flows" or render diagrams (agent-builder's flow diagram, SDK_PLAN.md) inside a watch/steer/spawn console would duplicate things Claude Code skills already do better, and Shepherd has no business owning skill authoring (L1 explicitly keeps skill authoring as "drop a folder in ~/.claude/skills — no DB", todos.md:262). One could argue this is already covered: L1 ships the registry + spawn, L3 ships templates with input-schema collection, and the Improve/auditReviewPrompt mechanism already proves the "paste a curated prompt into a session" pattern — so adding named meta-skills risks being redundant packaging rather than new capability.
- **Ruling:** Judge the pattern, not the server/executor it ships in. Stripped to its transferable core, every one of these five skills is "compose a curated task prompt, hand it to a real Claude Code session, get a declarative artifact back." Shepherd ALREADY does exactly this with auditReviewPrompt/Improve — a pure cc-core prompt-builder + a paste effect, with the LLM work done by the spawned session, not Shepherd. So the double mismatch does NOT block it: there is no agent loop, sandbox, server, user-auth, or stored-secret requirement in the transferable part; the interview and authoring happen inside the Claude Code session Shepherd is steering, which is precisely the division of labor context.md prescribes ("native Claude Code owns the actual agent/skill execution; Shepherd only emits flags/files"). It is not DONT_ADD because it neither needs the executor nor is fully redundant — L1 specs the agent-profile schema and spawn but has NO authoring front-end, and that gap is exactly what agent-builder/add_agent fill. It is not ADD-as-is because Shepherd cannot host the interactive loop and must not own skill authoring; the OpenHands frontmatter-defaults and onboarding guardrails must be reshaped into Shepherd's primitives (L1 profile schema defaults + L3 versioned seed-prompt templates + the existing ledger-review prompt seam). Hence ADAPT, reinforcing L1 (with L3 for the onboarding/template flavor and a nod to the ledger for /remember). Confidence high because the mapping is concrete and a working precedent (auditReviewPrompt) already exists in-tree.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 (Agent Profiles registry) as its missing AUTHORING front-end, with L3 + the ledger as supporting seams. Concretely: (1) add_agent → L1 profile-creation defaults: when the New Session modal "Save as agent" path writes a cc-agents.json record, pre-seed sensible frontmatter-style defaults (version 1.0.0, structured {role,goal,backstory} from crewAI enrichment at todos.md:276, empty skills[]/mcpServers[]) and run the existing L1 pre-flight validator (todos.md:285) before persist. (2) agent-builder / onboarding → L3 versioned, parameterized seed-prompt TEMPLATES: ship a small curated set of "author-a-spec" prompts (an agent-builder interview prompt that ends by writing a cc-agents.json profile + a plan file into the session's folder; an onboarding prompt carrying the <=5-questions / <=8-commits / acceptance-criteria / never-push-to-main guardrails). These are pure templateRender'd strings (cc-core, todos.md:360) the operator spawns-from or feeds into a session — Claude Code runs the interview and writes the files; Shepherd only composes the prompt and pastes it (same as Improve). The never-push-to-main guardrail also lines up with L2 read-only/no-Bash starter bundles for the spawned session. (3) add_repo_inst → an onboarding-template variant whose output target is a per-agent contextDir/memoryFile (L1, todos.md:299) or a project CONTEXT.md threaded via --add-dir/--append-system-prompt at next spawn. (4) /remember → a WRITE-mode sibling of auditReviewPrompt (cc-core.lua:715): a new pure prompt-builder that renders the session's recent ledger/transcript slice (reuse sessionTimeline:732 / renderNarrative:697) and instructs the session to list exact durable items NUMBERED for human confirmation before appending to the agent's memoryFile — keeping Shepherd's numbered-confirmation human-in-the-loop intact and excluding issue-specific noise. Everything stays off-by-default, dry-run behind spawn.live, with authoring done by the session, not Shepherd. DROP: the SDK flow-diagram rendering, any attempt to model interview state inside Shepherd, and OpenHands' server-side skill storage.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails on every skeptical axis; the ADAPT (high, reinforces L1) ruling holds. (a) No in-process executor/sandbox: in all five skills the interview and the artifact-authoring run INSIDE the running Claude Code session, which the LLM drives. Shepherd's transferable share is only "compose a curated prompt -> paste into a session -> session writes a declarative file," which already exists in-tree as the precedent auditReviewPrompt (cc-core.lua:715, the Improve button) — a pure prompt-builder + paste effect with zero agent loop. Verified: auditReviewPrompt/renderNarrative/sessionTimeline live at the cited lines 715/697/732 and are pure. (b) No auth/secrets/server: OpenHands' server-side skill storage is explicitly dropped; outputs target LOCAL files (cc-agents.json profiles, per-agent memoryFile/contextDir at todos.md:299). No secrets. (c) Not redundant — and this is the load-bearing finding: L1 (todos.md:242-301) specs the agent-profile SCHEMA ({role,goal,backstory} at :276), the pre-flight validator (:285), spawn-from-agent, and contextDir/memoryFile (:299), but has NO interview-driven AUTHORING front-end — skills authoring is literally 'drop a folder in ~/.claude/skills, no DB' (:262) and profiles are written via a bare 'Save as agent' modal field-fill. The guided interview that emits a profile is genuinely absent, so it's neither redundant (not DONT_ADD) nor portable as-is. (d) Not server plumbing: a guided 'build me an agent' flow on the local console is a real operator feature. It cannot be ADD because Shepherd cannot host the interactive interview loop (it watches/steers; the loop runs in the session) and must reshape OpenHands' frontmatter-defaults into L1 schema defaults + L3 versioned seed-prompt templates (todos.md:354-371) and a /remember WRITE-mode sibling of auditReviewPrompt that keeps the numbered human-in-the-loop confirmation. Hence reshaped-onto-primitives = ADAPT, reinforcing L1 with L3/ledger seams — exactly the prescribed verdict when an OpenHands pattern matches a queued Lx. Correctly drops the SDK flow-diagram, modeling interview state in Shepherd, and server-side skill storage.

#### Four-scope skill resolution with layered precedence + per-conversation merge  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** Multi-scope skill resolution is real and local for Claude Code. The user's #1 stated priority is "pre-configured agents/skills/MCPs saved into a directory," and skills genuinely live in MORE than one on-disk scope that Claude Code itself reads: user-global ~/.claude/skills (verified present on this Mac, with deno-fresh2/rune installed there exactly as SKILL.md packages) AND per-project/repo skills + project .mcp.json. So the candidate's CORE insight — "a session's EFFECTIVE skill/microagent set is the merge of several layers, user-global plus this repo's, later-wins by name" — describes something operators genuinely can't see today. The shipped L1 skills card only enumerates the single ~/.claude/skills global dir; it does NOT show that a session spawned in /repo/foo also picks up /repo/foo/.claude/skills, nor that a project skill of the same name shadows a global one. Surfacing the resolved per-session (per-tile/per-conversation) effective skill set — "what does THIS agent actually have in scope, and which layer won" — is a precise, local, read-only enrichment of the L1 enumerator that maps cleanly onto Shepherd's "a conversation is a session/tile" primitive. The layered-precedence model also strengthens L1's config-by-reference resolver: when an agent profile pins skills[], Shepherd should resolve names across BOTH the user-global and the target project's skill roots, not just the global dir.
- **Against:** The feature as specified is mostly server/sandbox plumbing that Shepherd structurally cannot host. Two of its five sources are pure DONT_ADD: the ORG source is a cloned org .openhands repo merged via build_org_config posted to an agent-server (that's the multi-tenant/VCS-server half of OpenHands), and the SANDBOX-exposed source only exists because OpenHands runs the agent inside a managed Docker/remote sandbox — Shepherd has neither a server nor a sandbox. More fundamentally, the per-conversation MERGE that computes the "effective set" is an inference-time concern owned by the executor: OpenHands needs load_and_merge_all_skills because IT runs the agent loop and must hand the model a final skill list. Shepherd runs no agent loop — native Claude Code performs its own multi-scope skill resolution at runtime from disk, so Shepherd reimplementing the merge/precedence as authoritative would be redundant (and could drift from Claude Code's actual rules). The /api/skills POST with per-source load flags, the /skills/search router, and the type/trigger classification service are all HTTP-server endpoints with no local single-user home. Build it as-specified and you ship a skills micro-service that nothing in Shepherd's architecture calls.
- **Ruling:** The DOUBLE MISMATCH cleanly bisects this feature. The value that depends on the executor (the per-conversation merge as authoritative input to an agent loop) and the server/sandbox (the org-cloned-repo source, the sandbox-exposed source, the /api/skills POST, the /skills/search router) is DONT_ADD — Claude Code already does its own runtime resolution from disk, so Shepherd duplicating it would be both impossible-to-host and redundant. But the underlying NEED is real and explicitly user-prioritized, and it survives reshaping: skills DO live in multiple local scopes Claude Code reads (verified: user-global ~/.claude/skills present; project .claude/skills + .mcp.json are standard), and Shepherd already tracks each tile's project/cwd (projectKey, the very identity used for relabels/collisions/routing). So the adapted shape is a READ-ONLY, multi-scope skills inventory: enumerate user-global + the session's project skill roots, mark which layer each came from and which name shadows which (later-wins), and show the resolved effective set per tile/detail-panel and in the New-session modal. This is not a parallel system — it is a direct widening of L1's already-queued read-mostly skills card and its config-by-reference resolver from one scope to several, with the same FX.listSkills / FX.readDir effects (which already exist). It corroborates L1 from an independent source (OpenHands), exactly the ADAPT-and-set-reinforces pattern. Drop the org/sandbox sources, the agent-server POST, the search router, and any claim of being the authoritative merge; keep the multi-scope local enumeration + precedence-display. Not ADD (would import server/sandbox plumbing); not DONT_ADD (the user named skills as high interest and the local multi-scope layout is verified on disk). High confidence: the skill scopes are confirmed on this machine and the L1 attach points are confirmed in-repo.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's skills layer. (1) Generalize the queued read-mostly skills enumerator from a single dir to a SCOPE LIST: a pure cc-core fn (sibling to presetList ~3640-3722) that takes [user-global ~/.claude/skills, the session's project root + its .claude/skills] and returns merged records tagged {name, scope, shadowedBy?}, computing later-wins-by-name precedence; FX.listSkills/FX.readDir (claude-dashboard.lua ~269-696) supply the reads — no new infra. (2) Show the resolved effective set per session: in the detail panel for a selected tile (keyed by projectKey/cwd Shepherd already tracks) render "skills in scope: N (M global, K from this repo; X shadowed)", and in the New-session modal show what a chosen folder will pick up. Pure derivation, read-only, off by default like other badges — sibling to the stale/hung/MCP-inventory badges already planned in L1. (3) Extend L1's config-by-reference resolver so a profile's skills[] resolve names across BOTH the user-global root and the target project's skill root (with the same [A-Za-z0-9._-] safe-path hardening), not just the global dir. DROP: the org-cloned-repo source, the sandbox-exposed source, the /api/skills POST + per-source load flags, the /skills/search router, the trigger/type classification service, and any notion of Shepherd being the authoritative inference-time merge (Claude Code owns that at runtime).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt FAILS — ADAPT (reinforces L1) holds. But the judge already DROPPED exactly that (Claude Code owns runtime resolution). The surviving residue is read-only enumeration of local dirs; no agent loop, no sandbox. Not refutable on (a). (b) server/auth/secrets: org-cloned-repo source, sandbox-exposed source, /api/skills POST, /skills/search router, trigger/type classification service — all explicitly DROPPED. (d) plumbing-vs-feature: the survivor is a console-facing per-tile badge ("skills in scope: N global, K repo, X shadowed") — a real end-user feature, sibling to the resolved-effective gate-tools list already rendered in the detail panel (claude-dashboard.lua:5442-5446 via core.resolveGateTools). #### Skills inspector modal — per-conversation 'skills loaded' view  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This lands squarely on the user's #1 stated priority ("pre-configured agents/skills/MCPs saved into a directory") and goes one useful step further than the queued L1 skills card. L1 gives you the INVENTORY (all skills authored in ~/.claude/skills). This candidate gives you the EFFECTIVE set for one running session — "what is this tile actually working with right now?" — which is exactly the watch-the-fleet question Shepherd exists to answer. The feasibility is real on Shepherd's own primitives: (1) the spawn side is already half-built in L1 — `spawnFlags` threads `skills[]`/`--agent`/`--add-dir`/`--mcp-config`, so Shepherd already KNOWS what it requested per session; rendering that as a per-tile "attached skills/MCP/knowledge" detail panel is a pure derivation over the agent-profile it spawned from, no new I/O. (2) The runtime "triggered skill knowledge" half is genuinely sourceable LOCALLY at zero token cost: Shepherd already tails the transcript JSONL (`transcriptError` at cc-core.lua:1588, token usage at :1611) and Claude Code writes skill-loaded `<system-reminder>` / `<EXTRA_INFO>` blocks into that same transcript when a keyword skill activates mid-run. A pure parser (sibling to `transcriptError`) could detect activation and emit a ledger event, then surface it via the EXISTING `sessionTimeline` (:732) — no new overlay needed. So both halves reshape onto shipped Shepherd primitives (spawn flags + transcript tail + ledger timeline) and directly enrich L1's skills card from a static catalog into a live per-session "what's loaded / what fired" view.
- **Against:** The OpenHands implementation's value rests on a server endpoint (`get_conversation_skills`, app_conversation_router.py:1302) that loads and MERGES all skill sources for a conversation and returns name/type/content — and it can do that authoritatively only because OpenHands IS the executor that injected those skills into the prompt and IS the server tracking per-conversation state. Shepherd is neither: native Claude Code owns skill resolution/loading, so Shepherd can never report the TRUE effective set with OpenHands' fidelity — only what it itself requested at spawn (and sessions a human started in a terminal, or that Claude auto-loaded, are invisible to it). The "merge Repository + Knowledge + AgentSkills" grouping is OpenHands' own taxonomy and microagent system, not Claude Code's. The fancy modal (expandable SkillItem, refresh, loading/empty/error/"waiting for runtime" guards) is web-app UI ceremony for an async server fetch Shepherd doesn't make — it reads files synchronously off disk. And the runtime-event half depends on Claude Code emitting stable, parseable skill-activation markers in the transcript; that format is undocumented and could change, making the timeline event fragile. There's a redundancy risk too: building a separate "skills modal" parallel to L1's skills card and the existing audit/timeline overlays fragments the UI.
- **Ruling:** The underlying NEED — "which skills/knowledge/MCP is THIS session actually running with, and which keyword skills fired during the run" — is real and fits Shepherd's watch/steer mandate perfectly, and it directly extends the QUEUED L1 skills card from a static catalog into a per-session live view. That makes it ADAPT/reinforces L1, not a parallel system, and not DONT_ADD. The double mismatch trims the OpenHands shape but does NOT kill the value: the server endpoint and merge-engine are server/executor plumbing Shepherd drops, but Shepherd already has the two local sources it needs — (1) the spawn flags it emitted from the agent profile (the "requested/attached" set, authoritative for Shepherd-spawned sessions), and (2) the transcript JSONL it already tails at zero cost (where skill-loaded reminder blocks land, the "actually fired" set). The honest limitation — Shepherd reports what it requested + what it can parse from the transcript, not Claude Code's internal authoritative set — is acceptable for a local single-user console and should be stated as a scope note, exactly like the existing MCP "inventory not health" narrowing in L1. Reshape: drop the React modal and "waiting for runtime" async ceremony; instead add an "Attached: N skills · M MCP · K dirs" section to the per-tile detail panel (pure derivation over the spawned agent profile + L1's `FX.listSkills`), each expandable to frontmatter/triggers, and add a pure `transcriptSkillActivations(text)` parser (sibling to `transcriptError`) that ledgers a `skill_triggered` event surfaced through the existing `sessionTimeline`/audit overlay — no new overlay. Effort is M because the spawn-flag derivation and the skills enumerator (`FX.listSkills`) are already L1 deliverables; the net-new work is the per-session merge view + the transcript activation parser + ledger event + timeline wiring, all on existing primitives. Build it as part of (or immediately after) L1, not standalone.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 (Agent Profiles registry). (1) Per-session "Attached" view: in the detail panel, render the effective skills[]/mcpServers[]/knowledge[]/plugins[] resolved from the agent profile the session was spawned from (the config-by-ref resolver L1 already specifies, dereferencing names against `~/.claude/skills` via the new `FX.listSkills`, `cc-mcp.json`, L2 bundles). Each row expandable to SKILL.md frontmatter (name/description/trigger keywords) + body — pure read, no server fetch, no "waiting for runtime" state. State it honestly: "what Shepherd attached at spawn," with an "(human-started; not visible to Shepherd)" note for sessions it didn't spawn. (2) Runtime activation timeline: add a pure `core.transcriptSkillActivations(text)` (sibling to `transcriptError` at cc-core.lua:1588) parsing the already-tailed transcript JSONL for skill-loaded `<system-reminder>`/`<EXTRA_INFO>` markers; emit a `skill_triggered` ledger event (off-by-default, like the rest of the ledger) and surface it through the existing `sessionTimeline` (cc-core.lua:732) and the 📜 audit / 🔔 alerts overlays — no new modal. Reuse, don't fork, the existing overlay UI.
- **Skeptic:** Tried to refute and could not knock it below ADAPT. (a) No executor/sandbox: both halves are pure local reads — the "Attached" view is derivation over the spawned agent profile + L1's queued FX.listSkills file read; the activation parser reads the already-tailed transcript JSONL (zero added cost). (b) No server/auth/secrets: the OpenHands server endpoint, merge-engine, React modal, and "waiting for runtime" async ceremony are explicitly dropped. (c) Not fully redundant, but correctly an ADAPT into L1, not a parallel system: L1 already queues a skills card from ~/.claude/skills, a config-by-ref resolver that dereferences skills[]/mcpServers[]/plugins[], and FX.listSkills — I confirmed NONE of these are implemented yet (they appear only in todos.md L1, never in cc-core.lua/claude-dashboard.lua), so this is a thin per-session rendering increment on queued L1 deliverables, the textbook ADAPT/reinforces-L1 case. (d) Not misclassified plumbing: the judge separated the console value from the server/modal plumbing. The reshape is grounded in real primitives I verified: FX.appendLedger (off unless ledger.enabled, claude-dashboard.lua:528/532), sessionTimeline (cc-core.lua:732), renderDetail() (claude-dashboard.lua:4334), the 📜 Timeline button, and transcriptError (cc-core.lua:1588) as the exact sibling-parser precedent. ONE empirical correction that REINFORCES (does not downgrade) the ruling: the OpenHands "keyword skill knowledge auto-trigger" mechanism does NOT exist in Claude Code's transcript. Grepping ~800 local JSONL transcripts I found ZERO skill-loaded <system-reminder>/<EXTRA_INFO> markers (the judge's mapping assumed these "land" in the transcript — that's an OpenHands-microagent assumption). What IS reliably parseable is the explicit Skill tool_use block ("name":"Skill","input":{"skill":...}, 15 real occurrences), and the static available-skills list lives in the system prompt, not the tailed JSONL (also 0 occurrences). So transcriptSkillActivations(text) must parse the Skill tool_use signal, not the non-existent keyword reminder; the feature's value (a skill_triggered ledger/timeline event for THIS session) survives intact — only the parsed signal changes. This is precisely the honest-scope narrowing the judge already applied to MCP ("inventory not health"), so the ADAPT/reinforces-L1 verdict holds — fold it into (or immediately after) L1, with the activation parser narrowed to explicit Skill invocations.

#### Async multi-stage start TASK with named spawn-progress stages + error detail  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The underlying NEED is real and currently unmet. Shepherd's spawn is a blind, multi-second keystroke "ladder" of timed beats (claude-dashboard.lua spawnEditorWindow: open window 3.0s -> focus -> open palette 0.8s -> type "Terminal: Create New Terminal" 0.6s -> ... -> type the claude launch line -> return), yet the operator's only feedback is ONE hs.alert ("opening X — best-effort") and console print lines they never see. There is no in-panel "this spawn is at stage N" and — critically — no failure surfacing: if a cold-start beat lands keystrokes in the wrong window (a documented hazard, hence the superseding-ladder logic and the field-verified ~2s shell-readiness miss), the claude launch silently fails and the operator only discovers it when no tile ever appears. OpenHands' named-stage model maps almost 1:1 onto beats Shepherd ALREADY runs (WAITING_FOR_SANDBOX≈window cold-start, SETTING_UP_SKILLS≈the planned L1 spawnFlags emit, STARTING_CONVERSATION≈the typed claude line, READY≈first status file appears, ERROR≈ladder timed out / no tile). Shepherd even has the exact pure primitive to build the resolver: routePendingDone (cc-core.lua:2007) already models a pending marker that "resolves when the session leaves done/idle (the paste landed)" or "expires as a lost feed" — the spawn-progress tracker is the same shape keyed by projectKey. Adding a "spawn-in-flight" sub-state to tiles (current beat label + spinner + a clear timeout->ERROR with detail) is a genuine watch/steer-console improvement, and it becomes essential under L1's batch fan-out spawn, where N blind ladders fire and the operator MUST see which ones landed.
- **Against:** Most of the feature is the OpenHands double mismatch incarnate. AppConversationStartTask exists because OpenHands does real provisioning Shepherd structurally cannot and must not do: WAITING_FOR_SANDBOX (no Docker sandbox), PREPARING_REPOSITORY/SETTING_UP_GIT_HOOKS (no managed repo clone — Shepherd spawns into an existing local folder), RUNNING_SETUP_SCRIPT (no execution loop). The transport is pure server plumbing: an async generator, a SQL start-task service persisting each yield, a POST /stream-start streaming endpoint, and searchable/countable/batch start-tasks — all of which require the FastAPI server, a database, and the request/response model Shepherd explicitly rejects (NO network server, NO persistence beyond status files + opt-in JSONL ledger). Shepherd's spawn is also fundamentally un-instrumentable at OpenHands' fidelity: it doesn't OWN the agent process, it fires keyboard events at a GUI it can't introspect, so it can only INFER stages from its own beat timeline (which beat fired) plus the status file appearing — it cannot truly observe "PREPARING_REPOSITORY done." A faithful copy (9-state enum, stream endpoint, persisted tasks) would be over-engineering for a single-user local panel where a spawn takes ~6 seconds and there's exactly one operator watching. The honest scope is small.
- **Ruling:** DONT_ADD is wrong because the need is real and unmet; ADD is wrong because building it "close to as-is" would import the server/SQL/stream-endpoint/sandbox-stage plumbing Shepherd's hard scope forbids. ADAPT is the fit: keep ONLY the watch-a-spawn-proceed-and-see-errors pattern, reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives. Concretely — Shepherd already KNOWS its spawn stages (the beats in runSequence are named/printed) and already has the resolver shape (routePendingDone's pending-marker-or-timeout). The adaptation is a pure spawn-progress tracker keyed by projectKey: record the current beat label as the ladder advances, render it as a transient tile sub-state (spinner + "opening terminal…/typing launch…"), resolve to READY when the project's first status file appears, and resolve to ERROR-with-detail when the ladder finishes but no tile materialized within a timeout (the silent-misfire case that today is invisible). This rides existing logic: the ~1s refresh loop already watches for new status files; the superseding-ladder machinery already tracks the in-flight beats; the ledger already records spawn events (extend with a spawn-failed event + reason). It directly strengthens L1's batch fan-out spawn, where blind parallel ladders make per-spawn landing visibility necessary, not just nice. Drop everything server-shaped: no async generator, no /stream-start, no SQL, no searchable start-tasks, no sandbox/repo/git-hook/setup-script stages.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a pure spawn-progress tracker in cc-core.lua modeled on routePendingDone (cc-core.lua:2007): state = projectKey -> { stage, startedAt }; functions to advance the stage and to resolve (READY when a fresh status file for the project appears; ERROR when now-startedAt exceeds a spawn timeout with no tile). claude-dashboard.lua spawnEditorWindow already runs the named beats via core.runSequence — have each beat call a tracker.setStage(projectKey, label) instead of (in addition to) the lone hs.alert/print. The ~1s refresh loop resolves in-flight spawns to READY/ERROR. Tile rendering gets a transient "spawning" sub-state (spinner + current-stage label) ahead of the normal idle/working tiles, and on ERROR surfaces the detail (e.g. "no session appeared after 12s — keystrokes may have missed the window") via the existing notification path. Emit a new spawn_failed event into the opt-in ledger (alongside the existing spawn event). Wire the same tracker into L1 batch fan-out spawn so each of the N parallel spawns reports landed/failed independently. Strictly NO HTTP endpoint, NO SQL/persisted task table, NO async-generator/stream transport, NO sandbox/repo/git-hook/setup-script stages.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute and could not; the ADAPT ruling survives all four standard refutation triggers, verified against the actual code. (a) No executor/sandbox needed. The judge DROPS exactly the server/sandbox pieces (async generator, /stream-start, SQL task table, and the sandbox/repo/git-hook/setup-script stages). The "stages" retained are NOT OpenHands' sandbox stages — they are Shepherd's OWN named beats already firing in spawnEditorWindow (claude-dashboard.lua:1437-1486 via core.runSequence, cc-core.lua:441): focus / open-extension (⌘Esc) / type-task / submit. A spawn-progress tracker recording the current beat label runs no agent loop and manages no sandbox. (b) No server/auth/secrets. The judge strips the HTTP endpoint, SQL, and stream transport. The retained tracker is in-process Lua state keyed by projectKey, modeled on routePendingDone (cc-core.lua:2007). Local, single-user, no secrets — within scope (context.md:13). (c) Not redundant; reinforces L1. The gap is real and unmet: spawnEditorWindow fires beats and merely prints; a focusProject miss inside a spawn beat (claude-dashboard.lua:1441/1467) just prints 'no window match' with NO operator-visible signal that the spawn silently misfired and produced no session. Existing auto-respawn death detection (5576-5603) and routePendingDone cover EXISTING-tile deaths and routed-paste landing, NOT spawn-landing. The resolution mechanism the judge relies on already exists for free: refreshList (5202-5207) scans STATUS_DIR every ~1s, so a newly-spawned session's first <id>.json surfaces as a READY signal; a timeout with no new file is the ERROR case. The ledger (FX.appendLedger, spawn event at 1554) extends naturally with a spawn_failed event. This directly strengthens L1's batch fan-out spawn (todos.md:294) where N blind parallel ladders make per-spawn landing visibility necessary. (d) Genuine console feature. The retained value — watch a spawn proceed and see error detail on a silent misfire — is end-user console UX (a transient 'spawning' tile sub-state + notification), not server plumbing. Conclusion: ADAPT (reinforces L1) is correct. It keeps only the watch-a-spawn-land-or-fail pattern reshaped onto existing primitives (named beats, ~1s status scan, ledger) and discards all server/sandbox shape. Refutation fails. Relevant files (absolute): /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/claude-dashboard.lua (spawnEditorWindow 1410-1487; refreshList 5202; auto-respawn 5576-5603; appendLedger spawn event 1554); /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua (runSequence 441; routePendingDone 2007); /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md (L1 batch fan-out 294); /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/context.md (scope 13).

#### Live LLM profile / model switch on a running session  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** "Apply a saved agent/profile to a session you're already running, and keep the model chip live" is a real fleet-console need and directly serves the user's #1 stated priority (saved agents in a directory we hand work off to). The mid-run-reflection-onto-the-tile pattern is exactly Shepherd's mandate: surface live state without a full refresh. It extends the queued L1 Agent Profiles registry from spawn-time-only to "re-point a running session at a saved profile," and the same-backend model slice is genuinely achievable via the live /model switch Shepherd already ships.
- **Against:** Most of the candidate's mechanism is server/executor plumbing Shepherd structurally lacks. switch_acp_model repoints an external agent's backend mid-loop — there is no agent loop, no ACP agent, no persisted backend in Shepherd. The "fingerprint into a fresh usage_id to bust the agent-server's first-write-wins registry" exists only to defeat an OpenHands-internal in-process registry; Shepherd has no server and no such registry, so it's pure dead weight. And a true "switch profile" implies changing provider/base-URL/system-prompt live, which Claude Code can't do in-session — cc-core.lua explicitly notes a base-URL change "needs a fresh session (relaunch), not /model." Strip the executor/server parts and what's left (live /model + chip reflection) is already shipped.
- **Ruling:** The double mismatch cleaves this candidate cleanly. (1) Live model chip reflection is ALREADY SHIPPED: cc-status.sh captures the live model/base_url (cc-status.sh:213-214) and the dashboard does optimistic local patching (claude-dashboard.lua:1091, :2456) so the model badge updates mid-run, not after a refresh — re-adding it is out of scope. (2) Live same-backend model switch is ALSO SHIPPED: core.modelCommand → /model (cc-core.lua:234), wired through handleAction "model" (cc-core.lua:161) and onModelChange (claude-dashboard.lua:4248), with the providers list feeding the dropdown. (3) switch_acp_model + the usage_id/registry-cache-busting are DONT_ADD slices — they presuppose the in-process executor and the agent-server registry Shepherd does not have. (4) The one genuinely-new, transferable idea is "apply a SAVED PROFILE (not just a bare model id) to a running session." That is not a new system; it's a small extension of the queued L1 Agent Profiles registry, which today only spawns-from-agent. For a same-backend profile, the live slice is /model (+ optionally /effort, set-mode) re-based from the profile's fields. For a profile whose provider/base-URL differs, there is no live path — it degrades to "relaunch this session from the saved agent in the same cwd," which Shepherd's graceful-drain + respawn primitive already covers (core.shouldDrainClose/respawnSpec). So: ADAPT, reinforcing L1, with the persisted-backend / ACP / cache-bust portions explicitly dropped per the mismatch.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend L1 with a detail-panel "Apply agent profile to this session" action alongside the existing live /model dropdown. Pure cc-core: resolve the chosen L1 profile via the config-by-ref resolver (the one L1 already plans, extending providerById:2632), then split into (a) same-backend, live-applicable fields → emit the existing serialized keystrokes (/model via modelCommand:234, /effort via effortCommand:226, set-mode via modeCycleSteps), reusing the optimistic re-base + delivery-guard path so the chip/mode badges (renderMeta, claude-dashboard.lua:4305) stay current mid-run exactly as today; and (b) NOT-live-applicable fields (different provider/base_url/system-prompt/skills/MCP) → because cc-core.lua:163-164 already documents that a base-URL change needs a relaunch, surface "relaunch as <profile>" routed through the existing respawnSpec/shouldDrainClose drain+respawn in the same cwd. DROP: switch_acp_model (no ACP/executor), the usage_id fingerprint / first-write-wins registry cache-bust (no server/registry), and any persisted-backend mutation. Off by default per scope.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT (reinforces L1) ruling holds. All of the judge's load-bearing claims verify against the code: (1) Live SAME-BACKEND model switch is SHIPPED, so the bare-model part is redundant: core.modelCommand builds `/model <id>` (cc-core.lua:234-237), routed by handleAction's `model` branch (cc-core.lua:161-166), wired to the panel via onModelChange -> send("model",...) (claude-dashboard.lua:4248-4251). The comment at cc-core.lua:162-164 explicitly states a base-URL/provider change needs a relaunch, not /model — matching the judge's degrade-to-relaunch mapping. (2) Live chip reflection mid-run is SHIPPED, so SwitchLLMObservation is redundant: cc-status.sh writes live model/base_url onto the status patch (cc-status.sh:213-214); renderMeta shows the model badge and re-bases the dropdown (claude-dashboard.lua:4305-4331), the dropdown being fed from PANEL_PROVIDERS plus the live it.model (4326-4327). The optimistic-patch primitive exists (FX.patchStatus, claude-dashboard.lua:1091-1096) and is already used to re-base mode on dispatch without waiting for a refresh (2457-2459). (3) The DOUBLE-MISMATCH correctly kills the rest: switch_acp_model presupposes an in-process ACP/agent executor Shepherd does not have; the usage_id fingerprint / first-write-wins registry-cache-bust presupposes an agent-server registry/server; persisting the session's backend presupposes mutable server-side conversation state. Shepherd has none of these (no executor, no sandbox, no server, no secrets) — all three are DONT_ADD slices, exactly as the judge dropped them. (4) The one genuinely-transferable kernel is NOT redundant and does NOT need the executor/server: apply a SAVED L1 PROFILE (not just a bare model id) to a RUNNING session. L1 today (todos.md:242-301) is spawn-from-agent ONLY — there is no live apply-profile-to-running-session action — and the live model dropdown today is bare-id-only. So this is a small, real extension of a QUEUED feature, reusing the config-by-ref resolver L1 already plans (todos.md:290-293, extending providerById at cc-core.lua:2632): split the profile into same-backend live-applicable fields (-> existing /model + /effort + set-mode keystrokes) vs provider/base_url-differing fields (-> degrade to relaunch via the shipped respawnSpec/shouldDrainClose drain+respawn, cc-core.lua:2092/2688). It cannot stand as a full ADD (the persisted-backend/ACP/cache-bust portions are DONT_ADD and the chip-reflection is shipped), and it cannot be pushed to DONT_ADD (the apply-saved-profile-live kernel is a legitimate, executor-free, server-free reinforcement of queued L1). ADAPT, reinforcing L1, with switch_acp_model / cache-bust / persisted-backend dropped, is correct.

#### Custom MCP server registry CRUD (add/edit/delete; SSE/stdio/streamable-HTTP) with validation  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is the single closest external-feature match to Shepherd's #1 stated user priority: "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs." L1 already explicitly carves out an "MCP server registry (cc-mcp.json): named records {id, label, transport(stdio|sse|http), command, args, url, allowedTools, authTokenEnv}, modeled on the providers registry. Pure validator + mcp-config-file builder. MCP picker in the modal; attachable to a profile." The candidate is essentially the concrete, battle-tested spec for that bullet: a CRUD list/edit/delete screen over three transports, with a real pre-save validation suite that Shepherd's bullet only gestures at. The fit on every axis is clean: (1) No executor needed — the records are config, and native Claude Code owns the actual MCP runtime; Shepherd only serializes them into an --mcp-config file at spawn (the claude CLI at ~/.local/bin/claude supports --mcp-config / --strict-mcp-config). (2) No sandbox. (3) No server/multi-user — cc-mcp.json is operator-data JSON living outside the Settings round-trip, exactly like cc-presets.json / cc-providers. (4) No stored secrets — the SSE/shttp api_key and stdio env values map onto the established authTokenEnv pattern (store the env-var NAME, let loginShellWrap expand it in the spawned login shell), so the api_key field becomes an env-var-name field, not a secret. The validation logic (URL http/https only, name required + charset ^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+$ + uniqueness, command no-spaces, KEY=VALUE env-line format, timeout 1..3600) is pure deterministic string work — a perfect citizen for cc-core.lua's zero-hs.* tested layer, a sibling validator to presetList/presetPush (cc-core.lua ~3658) and the L1 pre-flight validator family. It also enriches the queued design with a real third transport (shttp / streamable-HTTP, distinct from plain http) and a stricter validation contract than the bullet specified.
- **Against:** Taken as-built, the OpenHands implementation is pure double-mismatch plumbing: a React route + form components calling mutation hooks that PATCH agent_settings_diff.mcp_config on a FastAPI multi-user server, persisting into a per-user Settings blob (settings_models.py:430) so a remote authenticated user's agent loop can dial those servers from inside its sandbox. Every one of those mechanics — React/hooks, server PATCH, per-user settings, the agent that actually consumes the MCP connection — is something Shepherd structurally cannot and should not host. The stored api_key field is precisely the "stored secrets" Shepherd's hard scope forbids; copied literally it would violate the no-secrets limit. There's also a real redundancy risk: native Claude Code already reads project .mcp.json and keeps its own MCP registry, and claude mcp add exists — so a Shepherd-side registry is a thin fleet-convenience layer at best, and over-building it (health pings, per-server status, discovery) would reimplement what the CLI/runtime already owns and what L1 explicitly DROPs (server-side health pings, public registry/discovery, ClientSessions). The validation suite, while pure, is low-value relative to L1's bigger payload (agent profiles + skills card + spawn-from-agent); shipping the MCP CRUD in isolation gives a registry with nothing yet attaching to it.
- **Ruling:** The NEED (a named MCP server registry the operator can CRUD and attach to a spawn) is real, user-prioritized, and already queued as a sub-component of L1. So this is ADAPT/reinforces-L1, not a parallel ADD and not DONT_ADD. The double mismatch only kills the IMPLEMENTATION (React + server PATCH + per-user settings + the consuming agent loop), not the PATTERN. Reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives the pattern survives intact: cc-mcp.json operator JSON (like cc-presets/cc-providers), pure cc-core CRUD + validators (mcpList/mcpPush/mcpRemove, sibling to presetPush), and an mcp-config-file builder whose output feeds spawnFlags' --mcp-config / --strict-mcp-config. The one genuine landmine — the stored api_key — is neutralized by Shepherd's existing rule: store the env-var NAME (authTokenEnv), never the value; loginShellWrap expands it at spawn. The candidate usefully sharpens L1 in two concrete ways worth folding into the backlog: (a) add shttp/streamable-HTTP as a transport distinct from sse and stdio (L1 currently lists stdio|sse|http), and (b) adopt the stricter pre-save validation contract (name charset ^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$ + uniqueness, command no-spaces, env KEY=VALUE format, timeout 1..3600, url http/https-only) into the L1 pure validator family. Build only the config/registry + flag-emission; do NOT build health pings, discovery, or any runtime status (L1 already DROPs those and the runtime isn't locally sourceable). Verdict: ADAPT, reinforces L1, high confidence.
- **How (Shepherd):** Folds into L1's existing "MCP server registry (cc-mcp.json)" bullet. Concretely: (1) Data: cc-mcp.json operator-data JSON beside cc-presets.json/cc-providers, OUTSIDE the Settings round-trip. Record per transport — sse {id, label, transport='sse', url, apiKeyEnv?}; stdio {id, label, transport='stdio', command, args[], env: {NAME->envVarName}}; shttp {id, label, transport='shttp', url, apiKeyEnv?, timeout}. Note api_key/env VALUES become env-var NAMES (apiKeyEnv / env-name map), expanded by loginShellWrap at spawn — preserves the no-secrets limit. (2) Pure CRUD in cc-core.lua: mcpList/mcpPush/mcpRemove mirroring presetList/presetPush/presetRemove (~3658), cap + replace-in-place + zero hs.*. (3) Pure validators (add to the L1 pre-flight validator family, sibling to presetList): validateUrl (http/https only), validateName (required + ^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$ + uniqueness across list), validateCommand (required + no spaces), validateEnvLine (KEY=VALUE), validateTimeout (1..3600). Unit-tested in tests/. (4) Builder: pure mcpConfigFile(records[]) -> the JSON Claude Code's --mcp-config expects; FX writes it, spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) threads --mcp-config / --strict-mcp-config into spawnSpec (3061). Side effects only via the fx table. (5) UI: an MCP section/picker in the New-session modal + Settings, list with edit/delete-with-confirm, attachable to an L1 agent profile via mcpServers[]. (6) Update todos.md L1 to add 'shttp' as a third transport and the stricter validation contract. Do NOT port: React/hooks, server PATCH, per-user settings persistence, stored api_key VALUES, health/discovery/status.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails on every axis; the ADAPT/reinforces-L1 ruling is correct. Verified against the actual codebase: DOUBLE-MISMATCH does NOT apply. (a) No executor/sandbox: the feature is a CONFIG registry + flag/file emission, not running an MCP server. Native Claude Code (local `claude` CLI, confirmed present; todos.md:225 documents `--mcp-config`/`--strict-mcp-config`) owns MCP execution; Shepherd only writes the config file and threads the flag through spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) into spawnSpec (3061). The judge correctly DROPs health pings/discovery/runtime-status, and L1 already DROPped them (todos.md:265-274). (b) The one real landmine — the stored api_key — is genuinely neutralized by an EXISTING, code-confirmed Shepherd convention: store the env-var NAME, expand at spawn. cc-core.lua:2620-2622 states secrets are never stored; profiles carry `authTokenEnv` (a NAME) which `loginShellWrap` (2754) expands as `$NAME` in the login shell at launch (put("ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN","$"..authTokenEnv) at 2728-2729). Reshape api_key->apiKeyEnv and the no-secrets limit holds. No multi-user/auth/server needed for a local Settings CRUD screen. PATTERN reshapes onto VERIFIED primitives. Pure CRUD: presetList/presetPush/presetRemove (cc-core.lua:3658-3705) are exactly the validate+cap+replace-in-place, zero-hs.* pattern mcpList/mcpPush/mcpRemove would mirror. Registry-by-id: providerById (2632). The mcp-config-file builder is pure; FX writes it; only side effects go through the fx table. All architecturally sound for this codebase. ALREADY QUEUED, hence ADAPT not DONT_ADD and not parallel-ADD. L1 explicitly lists 'MCP server registry (cc-mcp.json): named records {id,label,transport(stdio|sse|http),command,args,url,allowedTools,authTokenEnv}... Pure validator + mcp-config-file builder. MCP picker in the modal; attachable to a profile' (todos.md:258-260), and the pre-flight pure-validator family + config-by-ref resolver (285-293). There is currently zero MCP code in the lua (grep count 0 in cc-core.lua + claude-dashboard.lua), so this is net-new work folding into a queued bullet, not redundant with shipped code. ADD->ADAPT is the right downgrade: it extends an existing queued feature rather than standing up a separate system. The candidate even SHARPENS L1 in two concrete ways worth folding in: (1) add 'shttp'/streamable-HTTP as a third transport distinct from sse and stdio (L1 lists stdio|sse|http today); (2) adopt the stricter pre-save validation contract (name required + ^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$ + uniqueness, command required + no-spaces, env KEY=VALUE, timeout 1..3600, url http/https-only) into L1's pure validator family. Build only registry+validators+flag-emission; do NOT port React/server-PATCH/per-user-settings, stored api_key VALUES, or health/discovery/status.

#### System message / agent-tools inspector  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** A per-session "what is this agent configured with" inspector is genuinely useful on a watch/steer/spawn console. When an operator is staring at a fleet of tiles and one is misbehaving, "what model / permission mode / system-prompt persona / which skills, MCP servers, and gate tools is THIS session running?" is the first diagnostic question. Shepherd already half-answers it (the detail panel shows live model/base_url/permission_mode/effort/editor and is live-switchable) — a consolidated read-only inspector is the natural next surface, and it dovetails with the user's #1 stated priority (saved agents/skills/MCP defs in a directory): once you have L1 agent profiles, you want a "show what this session was spawned with / what this profile resolves to" view. The two-tab shape (config metadata vs. an expandable per-capability list) is a clean UX to copy.
- **Against:** The literal OpenHands feature draws its content from the in-process executor: the FULL system prompt, the agent class, and each tool's JSON schema/description are things OpenHands knows because IT builds the agent loop and assembles the prompt + tool registry before calling the LLM. Shepherd runs no loop and never assembles a prompt — for sessions started by the human in a terminal it has no way to know the system prompt, the resolved tool set, or the per-tool schemas; the status files (model/base_url/mode/effort/editor) carry none of that, and there is no local source for "agent class" or a per-tool schema dump. Building a faithful clone would require either an executor to introspect (the (a) half of the double mismatch) or scraping internals Claude Code owns. So the feature as-built is partly DONT_ADD-shaped — the tool-schema tab in particular has no honest local data source for a generic running session.
- **Ruling:** The NEED (per-session config transparency / "what is this agent configured with") is real and fits a watch/steer/spawn console, but the SOURCE must be reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives — classic ADAPT. OpenHands can show the system prompt + tool schemas because it owns the executor that assembled them; Shepherd does not run the loop, so it cannot introspect a running session's prompt or tool registry, and the status files carry none of it. What Shepherd CAN honestly show is the config it itself emitted at spawn (or that an L1 profile resolves to): the `--append-system-prompt` persona block (role/goal/backstory from L1's seedPrompt), the resolved skills[]/mcpServers[]/plugins[]/knowledge[], the policy bundle (L2) + the per-session resolved gate.tools, the provider/model/permission-mode/effort, plus the configured-MCP inventory derived from ~/.claude.json (already on L1's plan). The live-status fields (model/base_url/mode/effort) are the only authoritative per-session runtime facts and are already captured. So the inspector becomes a read-only "session/profile configuration" panel sourced from the spawn flags + resolved profile + gate, NOT an executor-introspected prompt/tool dump. The 'Tools' tab maps to "resolved gate.tools + configured MCP servers" rather than per-tool LLM schemas (which Claude Code owns and Shepherd can't enumerate). This is a presentation layer over data L1/L2 already produce, hence modest effort and reinforcing L1.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a read-only "Session config" inspector (a detail-panel expander or small modal) with two regions mirroring the OpenHands tabs but sourced from Shepherd's own primitives: (1) "Config" — live status facts already captured (model/base_url, permission_mode, effort, editor) PLUS, when the session was spawned from an L1 agent profile, the resolved profile: the `spawnFlags` Shepherd emitted (`--append-system-prompt` persona from seedPrompt {role,goal,backstory}, `--agent`, `--mcp-config`, `--plugin-dir`, `--add-dir` knowledge[]), provider, and Claude Code CLI version if cheaply obtainable (`claude --version`, cached). (2) "Capabilities" — the per-session resolved `gate.tools` (from `resolveGateTools`) + attached L2 policy bundle + the configured-MCP inventory derived from ~/.claude.json + project .mcp.json (L1's MCP inventory badge), each row expandable to its stored record (the cc-mcp.json {transport,command,args} / skill SKILL.md frontmatter), NOT an LLM tool schema. Pure derivation in cc-core (reuse providerById/resolveGateTools/the L1 resolver + a profile-by-session lookup); one FX read for the version. Off by default consistent with house style. Explicitly DROP the executor-introspected full-system-prompt and per-tool JSON-schema dump — no local source.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute ADAPT and could not. The literal OpenHands feature has an executor-only half that Shepherd genuinely can't build — confirmed: the only per-session source is ~/.claude/cc-status/<id>.json, and cc-status.sh writes just editor/permission_mode/effort/model/base_url (lines 210-214), never the system prompt or per-tool JSON schemas (Claude Code owns prompt assembly + the tool registry; Shepherd runs no loop). The judge already DROPPED exactly that part. So the refutation would need EITHER (a) the whole feature reducing to that impossible executor dump, OR (b) pure redundancy/server plumbing. Neither holds. The underlying need — a per-session "what is this agent configured with" view — is real for a watch/steer/spawn console and maps onto data Shepherd's own primitives produce: L1 (todos.md:242-301) threads seedPrompt{role,goal,backstory} into --append-system-prompt plus --mcp-config/--agent/--plugin-dir/--add-dir and resolves skills[]/mcpServers[]/plugins[]/knowledge[] + the MCP inventory from ~/.claude.json + .mcp.json; L2 supplies the attached policy bundle + per-session gate.tools via resolveGateTools (cc-core.lua:2537); model/base_url/permission_mode/effort/editor are already captured; and claude --version is already invoked (claude-dashboard.lua:425). No executor/sandbox/server/auth/secrets are involved — it's pure cc-core derivation + one FX read. It is not redundant: no inspector/session-config/system-prompt view exists today, and L1's cc-agents.json/agentList registry isn't shipped yet, so this is a read-only presentation layer that REINFORCES L1 (and L2), which is precisely the ADD->ADAPT condition. Recommended Shepherd shape matches the judge's: a read-only, off-by-default "Session config" expander/modal with a Config region (live status facts + resolved spawn flags/profile + cached CLI version) and a Capabilities region (resolved gate.tools + attached L2 bundle + configured-MCP inventory, each row expandable to its stored cc-mcp.json/SKILL.md record), explicitly NOT the executor-introspected full prompt or per-tool LLM schema. ADAPT stands.

#### Settings UI shell as tabbed routes (LLM/Agent/Condenser/MCP/Skills/Secrets/Integrations/App/API keys)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** Shepherd's Settings panel is one long single modal: provider rows, gate/policy fields, ledger, escalation, queue/routing, spawn, risk, drain/respawn/auto-continue, focus — all stacked in `showSettings` (claude-dashboard.lua:3729). As the L1/L2/L3/L4 backlog lands (Agent Profiles registry, named policy bundles, parameterized templates, MCP/skills inventories), that flat modal becomes unscannable. OpenHands' "settings = a left-nav shell with tabbed child routes" is exactly the IA that keeps a growing config surface navigable, and it maps almost 1:1 onto things Shepherd already has or has queued: LLM-settings = the existing Providers tab; Agent-settings = L1 Agent Profiles; MCP-settings (typed add/edit/delete table) = L1 `cc-mcp.json` registry; Skills (per-skill toggle of disabled_skills) = L1 skills card over `~/.claude/skills` + a `disabled_skills` set; App-settings (sound, confirmation/permission mode) = the existing notifications.sound / gate-armed / permission-mode prefs. The tabbed-overlay UI primitive already exists in-app (the audit overlay's Rows/Timeline/Alerts/Shift tabs, claude-dashboard.lua:3305), so this is pure presentation reshaping with no new architecture. The per-skill enable/disable toggle (`SkillsSettingsScreen` → `disabled_skills`) is a genuinely new, useful idea that fits the local model perfectly: a pure `disabledSkills[]` set filtered by the L1 skills enumerator and emitted as spawn flags, directly serving the user's #1 stated interest.
- **Against:** This is a UI-shell/IA pattern, not a feature — adopting "tabbed routes" wholesale risks importing an information architecture built for a multi-tenant SaaS, and several of its named tabs are structurally forbidden in Shepherd. A Secrets store and an API-keys tab are HARD scope violations: Shepherd NEVER stores keys (it resolves $ENV var NAMES through the spawned login shell). Org/billing is enterprise multi-tenant — no users, no server, impossible. Git/VCS integrations as OpenHands ships them (OAuth tokens to GitHub/GitLab held server-side) need stored secrets + a server. Condenser is an in-process agent-loop concept (context condensation during the run) — Shepherd runs no loop, so the tab is meaningless. So roughly half the proposed tabs are DONT_ADD on the double mismatch. There's also a real risk of premature scaffolding: Shepherd's settings modal works today and isn't yet overflowing; building the left-nav shell before L1/L2/L3 actually land is YAGNI, and the routes.ts/layout-with-child-routes mechanism is a React-Router idiom that has no analog in a single embedded webview — there are no "routes" here, just show/hide. Building a navigation framework for config that mostly doesn't exist yet inverts the order; the right move is to add tabs incrementally as each registry ships, not adopt the shell up front.
- **Ruling:** The underlying NEED — keep a growing config surface navigable instead of one infinite-scroll modal — is real and will sharpen as L1 (Agent Profiles + MCP registry + skills card), L2 (policy bundles), and L3 (templates) land. But the value is the IA, not the SaaS plumbing, and it cannot be copied as-is: Secrets, API keys, and org/billing are direct HARD-scope violations (no stored secrets, no users, no server); Git/VCS integrations need server-held OAuth tokens; Condenser is an agent-loop concept with no executor to attach to. That kills ~half the tabs outright. The legitimate remainder (LLM/Provider, Agent, MCP, Skills, App) all map onto existing or already-queued Shepherd primitives, so the correct verdict is ADAPT, reshaping the pattern onto Shepherd's webview and reinforcing L1 — NOT a parallel system. The one net-new, well-fitting idea is the per-skill enable/disable toggle (`disabled_skills`), which slots cleanly into the L1 skills card. Because Shepherd already has both a working Settings modal AND a tabbed-overlay primitive (audit overlay), and because the config surface isn't overflowing yet, this should be sequenced as a low-priority grouping/sectioning refactor of the existing modal that L1 piggybacks on (Agents/MCP/Skills sections appear as that registry ships) rather than a speculative routing shell built up front. Confidence high: I confirmed the flat modal at claude-dashboard.lua:3729, the existing tab primitive at :3305, the Providers tab, and the L1 plan for cc-agents.json/cc-mcp.json/skills in todos.md.
- **How (Shepherd):** Reshape the existing single-modal Settings (`showSettings`, claude-dashboard.lua:3729) into grouped/tabbed sections reusing the audit-overlay tab primitive (claude-dashboard.lua:3305-3308, :4553 `auditTab`). KEEP tabs: LLM/Providers (existing Providers tab), Agent (L1 Agent Profiles card over cc-agents.json), MCP (L1 cc-mcp.json typed add/edit/delete table), Skills (L1 enumerator over ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md frontmatter), Policy/Gate (existing gate.tools + L2 bundles), App (existing notifications.sound + gate-armed + spawn permission-mode), Ledger/Audit (existing). NET-NEW that fits: per-skill enable/disable as a pure `disabledSkills[]` set on the Skills card, filtered by the L1 skills enumerator and emitted via spawn flags (mirrors OpenHands `disabled_skills`). DROP entirely (double mismatch): Secrets store + API keys (no stored secrets — provider records hold env-var NAMEs only), org/billing (enterprise multi-tenant), Condenser (no agent loop), and OAuth-style Git/VCS integration tabs (no server-held tokens). Sequence as an incremental grouping refactor that L1/L2/L3 hang their cards on as they ship — not a routing shell built before the registries exist.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails — the ADAPT ruling survives skeptical scrutiny because the judge already amputated every part that would trip the common errors, and verified the anchors. Codebase facts confirmed: the Settings modal IS a single flat infinite-scroll stack of ~17 `s-sec` sections (claude-dashboard.lua:3119/:3729 — General, Queue, Escalation, Risk, Drain, Respawn, Providers, Policies, etc., no tabs), and a real reusable tab primitive already exists in the audit overlay (a-tab buttons :3305-3308 + `auditTab()` :4551 toggling .active + swapping body). So the IA value is real and needs no new infrastructure.\n\nChecklist test: (a) executor/sandbox — NO; the shell runs nothing, it groups existing webview controls. The only tab implying an agent loop is Condenser (context-compaction), which the mapping correctly DROPS (no 'condenser' string anywhere in the dashboard). (b) multi-user/secrets/server — this is the strongest hit, but the mapping already DROPS exactly the violating tabs: Secrets store, API keys, org/billing (enterprise multi-tenant), and OAuth Git/VCS integrations (server-held tokens). todos.md:235-236 codifies 'Secrets never stored' (records hold env-var NAMEs via `authTokenEnv`/`requiredEnv`, never values) and todos.md:270-274 explicitly DROPs per-agent billing, multi-tenant teams/keys, and 'stored auth values'. The kept tabs (LLM/Providers — existing `renderProviders` :3795; Agent — L1 cc-agents.json; MCP — L1 cc-mcp.json; Skills — L1 enumerator over ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md; App; Policy/Gate; Audit) are all local/no-secret/no-server and map onto existing or queued (L1/L2) primitives. (c) redundant/queued — yes, the surviving value reinforces L1's growing config surface plus the existing Providers tab; that is precisely why the verdict is ADAPT (a low-priority grouping refactor L1 hangs cards on) and NOT ADD (a speculative routing shell). (d) plumbing-mistaken-for-feature — half the tabs are exactly that SaaS plumbing, and those are dropped; the remainder is a legitimate end-user console IA concern.\n\nThe one net-new idea (per-skill enable/disable as a pure `disabledSkills[]` set emitted via spawn flags, mirroring OpenHands `disabled_skills`) slots cleanly into the queued L1 skills card with no executor. ADD->ADAPT is the correct calibration: it is a reshaping/reinforcement of an existing+queued feature, not a parallel system. No grounds to downgrade to DONT_ADD.

### Reinforces L4 — routing & orchestration  (5)

#### Pending-message queue: queue messages before a session is ready, auto-flush + rekey on READY  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** Shepherd users already want to line up work for a session that does not yet exist: L1's "spawn from a saved agent" seeds the session queue with a seedPrompt, and batch fan-out seeds per-row queues at spawn. The candidate generalizes that to "queue multiple ordered messages now, deliver them in order the instant a real session for this target becomes ready." That is a clean, on-model capability for a spawn console: arm a folder's queue before launching, then have the dispatcher flush it the moment the freshly-spawned session reaches done/ready — covering the explicitly-deferred "feed a brand-new session" gap (todos.md:202). The ordered-delivery and delete-after-delivery semantics are sane queue hygiene Shepherd's queue could adopt.
- **Against:** The core mechanism is largely redundant with what Shepherd already shipped, and its hardest part is solved more cleanly. Shepherd's queue is keyed by projectKey (launch folder), NOT session id, precisely so a queue can exist before any session id and survive respawn/clear — "the successor inherits the pending tasks automatically" (cc-core.lua:1944-1958). So the candidate's signature work — minting a task-{uuid} placeholder id and then rekeying it to the real conversation id on READY — is machinery Shepherd structurally does not need; there is no id to rekey. Auto-flush already exists via shouldFeed + the level-triggered routing dispatcher. The 10-msg cap / HTTP 429 is multi-user server-product plumbing (Double Mismatch side b) with no local analog. What is left after removing the redundant and server-only parts is small.
- **Ruling:** Apply the Double Mismatch: nothing here needs the executor or sandbox, but the cap/429 is server plumbing and the rekey is server-id bookkeeping. Strip both and the residual NEED — "stage ordered work for a target before it is ready, auto-deliver on ready" — is real and already half-built in Shepherd. The decisive fact is architectural: Shepherd's project-keyed queue (cc-core.lua queueKey, 1944-1958) was designed for exactly this problem, making the candidate's rekey step moot and its pre-id keying already-handled. Seeding a queue at spawn is L1; auto-delivery to a free member is the shipped Project Routing v1; the only genuinely new slice — auto-feeding a project that has NO live session yet (rather than an existing free member) — is an explicitly deferred routing follow-up (todos.md:202, "auto-spawn on starvation"). That places it as an extension of the routing/orchestration roadmap line, so ADAPT reinforcing L4, not a parallel pending-messages subsystem.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend L4 (declarative routing/orchestration) + the shipped Project Routing v1: let an armed project's projectKey-keyed queue (cc-core.lua queueKey/queueRouted) hold ordered tasks even when the project has zero live members, and have routeTask/routePick deliver them in created-at order to the first session that reaches `done` for that folder (the no-session-yet case the current routePick can't reach — its members list is empty). Ordered delivery already follows from queue order; queueFeedCommit's delivery-gated pop already gives "delete only after successful delivery." Pair with L1 spawn-seeding so "spawn from agent" can pre-stage a multi-message queue and the just-spawned session drains it on first ready. NO placeholder-id/rekey layer (projectKey makes it unnecessary), NO depth cap beyond the existing local queue UX, NO 429.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT (reinforces L4 + L1) ruling holds. I verified every load-bearing claim against the code. (1) Double Mismatch: nothing here needs the executor/sandbox/server. Staging text and pasting it into a session when it reaches `done` is pure STEERING, which Shepherd already does — no agent loop, no API call, no sandbox. (2) The OpenHands-specific mechanics are all server/DB plumbing that strips away: the placeholder `task-{uuid}` key and the rekey-on-READY step exist only because OpenHands has no stable identifier before a conversation DB row exists; Shepherd's queue is keyed by the STABLE projectKey/launch-folder (cc-core.lua:1952-1958, `queueKey`), which exists at spawn time and survives respawn/clear, so there is NOTHING to rekey — the pre-id keying and rekey are moot. The 10-msg cap + HTTP 429 are server plumbing (no HTTP layer; the local queue already has UX caps). Notably, the candidate's 'delete after delivery REGARDLESS of success' is the OPPOSITE of Shepherd's deliberate `queueFeedCommit` (line 1965), which pops ONLY on confirmed delivery precisely so a skipped paste can't silently destroy a queued task — so that bit is rejected, not copied. (3) Not fully redundant (so not DONT_ADD): the residual need — auto-deliver a project's queue to a target that is NOT YET LIVE — is genuinely unreached today. `routePick` (line 2041) iterates `members` sharing a queueKey; with zero live sessions `members` is empty and it returns nil, so the persisted queue just waits. (4) It is an EXTENSION of the queued roadmap, not a parallel pending-messages subsystem: the no-session-yet drain is the explicitly-DEFERRED 'auto-spawn on starvation' follow-up (todos.md:202, whose `queueStarved`/`starvedSince` clock already exists at cc-core.lua:2075), and the 'seed a queue at spawn, drain on first ready' path is L1 spawn-seeding (todos.md:255, 'seeds the session queue with seedPrompt'). Ordered delivery falls out of queue order for free. That places it squarely as ADAPT reinforcing L4 (declarative routing/orchestration, which already absorbs the 4c-E follow-ups) plus L1 — with NO placeholder-id/rekey layer, NO 429, NO depth cap beyond the existing local queue, and NO delete-on-failure. The judge's mapping is correct.

#### Sub-conversations: parent/child lineage with config inheritance on spawn  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** The core management pattern is real and Shepherd is missing exactly one piece of it. The candidate decomposes into three sub-patterns, two of which Shepherd already part-implements: (1) CONFIG INHERITANCE ON SPAWN — copy editor/cwd/permMode/provider+model from a source record into a new spawn — is literally already shipped as `respawnSpec` (cc-core.lua:2688), which copies editor, project(cwd), permission_mode, and provider/model from a dying session's record; the L1 Agent Profiles registry generalizes "spawn from a saved config" further. (2) CASCADE/COLLAPSE/FILTER maps cleanly onto the existing session groups + tile collapse + `applyGroups` (cc-core.lua:350). The genuinely-NEW and valuable piece is (3) a real spawn-time PARENT→CHILD EDGE (`parent_conversation_id`): when one session spawns/delegates to another, recording that lineage. This is precisely what the queued L4 "delegation / role-addressed handoff" item wants — "Delegate = render an L3 task + enqueue onto the named agent's queue, delivered by the router." A parent_session pointer stamped on the spawn/route ledger event (the existing `by:"router"`/delegation event already carries source/target) turns L4's handoffs into a navigable lineage, and the routing topology view (L4) becomes the natural place to render parent/child edges. It is all pure-read over data Shepherd already has (session records + ledger), fits the no-server/no-secrets model, and the user's stated interest in handing work off to saved agents directly favors making those hand-offs traceable.
- **Against:** Most of the candidate's described VALUE lives in the parts Shepherd structurally cannot or already has. The config-inheritance half is dominated by SANDBOX and GIT-REPO/BRANCH/PROVIDER inheritance — a child reusing the PARENT'S Docker/remote sandbox and its server-side git provider record. Shepherd manages no sandbox and stores no provider credentials, so that inheritance is meaningless here; the non-sandbox slice (editor/cwd/model/permMode) is ALREADY done by `respawnSpec` and generalized by L1, so re-adding it is redundant. The lineage/cascade-delete/`include_sub_conversations` machinery is OpenHands multi-tenant CONVERSATION-STORE bookkeeping (a server owning persistent conversation rows it can cascade-delete and paginate); Shepherd's "conversations" are live OS sessions Claude Code owns and that die on their own — Shepherd doesn't durably store conversations to cascade-delete, and "cascade-delete the parent's children" risks killing live work the human is steering, which cuts against the human-in-the-loop reason-to-exist. And Shepherd's existing "session lineage" already occupies the word for a different (respawn/clear churn) concept, so a parallel parent/child "lineage" risks conceptual collision. The net new surface that survives the double mismatch is small: a parent-id field on an already-planned L4 delegation event.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch line-by-line. (a) EXECUTOR/SANDBOX: "inherit the parent's sandbox" + "git repo/branch/provider" is sandbox-and-server inheritance — DONT_ADD that slice outright. (b) MULTI-USER SERVER: cascade-delete of stored conversation rows + `include_sub_conversations` pagination is conversation-store CRUD a server owns; Shepherd has no durable conversation store and shouldn't auto-kill live sessions. What SURVIVES is the management pattern: a spawn-time parent→child EDGE plus config-inheritance-on-spawn.
