Config inheritance is already shipped (`respawnSpec` copies editor/cwd/permMode/provider+model from a source record — the exact `_inherit_configuration_from_parent` move, minus sandbox/git) and generalized by L1 "spawn from a saved agent," so that half is redundant, not new. The single genuinely-new, in-scope idea is recording WHICH session spawned/delegated to WHICH — and that is already the explicit intent of L4's delegation/handoff item ("enqueue onto the named agent's queue, delivered by the router," ledger `by:"router"`/delegation) and its routing topology view. So verdict ADAPT, reinforces L4: add a `parent_session`/`spawnedBy` field to the delegation/route ledger event and surface the edge in L4's topology view; reuse session groups for collapse/filter. Confidence high because the mapping is unusually clean and the surviving surface is small. Effort S: it is a field on an already-planned event plus a topology annotation, not a new subsystem.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L4 (declarative routing & orchestration), do NOT build as a standalone "sub-conversations" subsystem. Concretely: (1) When a session spawns/delegates another (the L4 "Delegate = render L3 task + enqueue onto named agent's queue, delivered by router" path), stamp the new spawn/route ledger event with `parent_session`/`spawnedBy` (the `by:"router"`/delegation event near cc-core.lua ~2040-2098 already carries source/target — add the explicit parent pointer). (2) Surface parent→child as edges in the L4 routing topology view (the pure cc-core aggregation sibling to `fleetStats`/`lineageByProject`), so a hand-off chain is navigable. (3) Reuse the existing session groups + `applyGroups` (cc-core.lua:350) + tile-collapse for the "collapse children under parent / filter sub-conversations from default listing" behavior — no new `include_sub_conversations` store flag. (4) Config-inheritance-on-spawn is already shipped via `respawnSpec` (cc-core.lua:2688) and generalized by L1 "spawn from a saved agent" — do NOT re-implement; if a child should clone a live parent's config, route it through `respawnSpec`/L1's config-by-ref resolver. EXPLICITLY DROP: sandbox inheritance, git-repo/branch/provider-record inheritance, and cascade-DELETE of children (never auto-kill live sessions the human is steering; closing a parent must not stop its children). Keep the word "lineage" for the existing respawn/clear-churn read; call the new edge "parent/delegation" to avoid collision.
- **Skeptic:** Tried to refute; the ADAPT holds. The judge's line-by-line double-mismatch mapping is verified against the actual code/todos. (1) The dangerous slices are correctly dropped: "inherit the parent's sandbox" + git repo/branch/provider-record inheritance is executor/sandbox inheritance (mismatch a, DONT_ADD), and cascade-delete of stored conversation rows + the include_sub_conversations pagination flag is conversation-store CRUD a multi-tenant server owns (mismatch b) — Shepherd has no durable conversation store and must never auto-kill live sessions a human is steering. (2) The config-inheritance-on-spawn half is genuinely redundant (mismatch c): respawnSpec at cc-core.lua:2688 already copies editor/cwd(project)/permissionMode and providerId (resolved from model+base_url via providerByModel) from a source record — the exact _inherit_configuration_from_parent move minus sandbox/git — and L1 "spawn from a saved agent" + its config-by-ref resolver generalize it; re-implementing is forbidden. (3) The ONLY genuinely-new in-scope idea is recording which session spawned/delegated to which. I grepped cc-core.lua and claude-dashboard.lua and confirmed NO existing spawnedBy/parent_session edge exists (the only "delegat*" hits are code comments). That single edge maps cleanly onto queued L4's "Delegation / role-addressed handoff" item (todos.md:417-421, "enqueue onto the named L1 agent's queue, delivered by the router (ledger by:\"router\"/delegation)") — the by=="router" ledger event already exists (cc-core.lua:1169) — so it is a field on an already-planned event, plus an annotation on L4's planned routing-topology view (todos.md:422-426, the pure cc-core aggregation sibling to fleetStats ~954 / lineageByProject 1061). Collapse/filter reuses shipped session groups + applyGroups (cc-core.lua:350); no new store flag. Because the surviving surface is a small extension/reinforcement of a QUEUED feature (L4), not a new standalone subsystem, the correct verdict is ADAPT reinforcing L4 (ADD->ADAPT condition met), effort S. Cannot downgrade to DONT_ADD — a real, in-scope, non-redundant edge survives.

#### Callback / automation result ledger (per-fire SUCCESS/ERROR record)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** Shepherd runs four local automations every refresh tick (auto-respawn, auto-continue, router queue-feed, drain-close), all OFF by default, and they fire by injecting keystrokes / spawning windows — actions that genuinely can silently fail (no window match, not respawnable, paste dropped). Today the success/error story is uneven: the router feed already ledgers an honest outcome split (queueFeedCommit returns task_feed on delivery vs task_feed_skipped on a no-window-match miss, claude-dashboard.lua ~5649-5656), but auto-respawn's wouldFire-but-not-canRespawn path only print()s to console and never ledgers (claude-dashboard.lua ~5601-5603), and auto-continue ledgers the fire (auto_continue, ~5615) with no confirmed delivery outcome. The candidate's core idea — a UNIFORM per-fire result row {status SUCCESS|ERROR, ids, detail/error string, ts}, so an operator can audit which automations fired, when, and whether they failed — closes exactly that gap and rides Shepherd's existing strengths: the append-only JSONL audit ledger (ledgerFor) already stamps projectKey/sessionId/ts and already filters/sorts/paginates in the 📜 Audit overlay, so the result history is mostly free. It plugs directly into queued L4's task_done outcome-counter event. Pure double-mismatch clean: no executor (automations already run locally), no sandbox, no server, no users, no secrets.
- **Against:** The OpenHands shape is server/SQL plumbing — an indexed StoredEventCallbackResult table, an EventCallbackResultPage paginator, and a try/except wrapper around an in-process callback processor. Shepherd has no firing engine to wrap: the level-triggered dispatcher + the ledger ARE the engine, so the literal "wrap execute_callback in try/except, store the result" design does not transfer. The need is also already PARTIALLY met (the router feed split) and the rest is a small follow-on to work already queued in L4 ("emit a task_done ledger event with toolRequests/approvals/escalations/continues counters"), so a separate "callback result ledger" subsystem risks being a parallel system to L4 rather than a feature. The refresh loop is also already defended with pcall in many places, so "exceptions become ERROR records instead of crashing the loop" is low marginal value. Risk of over-building: SUCCESS rows for automations that fire correctly thousands of times add ledger noise unless deliberately scoped to the failure/skip edges that actually matter.
- **Ruling:** Judging the pattern not the plumbing: the value is "an auditable record of every automation fire and whether it succeeded or failed, with a detail string," which is squarely a watch/steer-console need and maps cleanly onto Shepherd's existing audit ledger. It is not DONT_ADD — it requires none of the double-mismatch structures (executor/sandbox/server/users/secrets); the automations already run in-process locally and already write JSONL. It is not plain ADD — the SQL-table/pagination/try-except-callback-processor framing assumes an in-process callback engine Shepherd lacks, so it must be reshaped onto ledger event types + the existing render-time Audit-overlay pagination. The honest scope is: (1) standardize an automation-outcome convention across all four automations — extend ledgerFor calls so each fire records a result/outcome field (success vs the skip/not-respawnable/error edge) with a short detail string, reusing queueFeedCommit's already-correct task_feed / task_feed_skipped pattern as the template; (2) ledger the currently-unrecorded auto-respawn wouldFire-but-not-canRespawn case (it has rs.reason in hand) and an auto-continue delivery outcome; (3) surface a filter in the 📜 Audit overlay for failed automation fires. This is a direct extension of the shipped ledger and a concrete down-payment on L4's task_done outcome counters, hence ADAPT reinforcing L4. It does not touch the user's saved-agents/skills/MCP priority, so no extra weight there.
- **How (Shepherd):** OpenHands event-callback-result ≈ Shepherd automation-fire ledger event. Concretely: add an `outcome`/`detail` convention to the existing ledgerFor automation events in claude-dashboard.lua — auto_respawn (~5595), auto_continue (~5615), the router feed (~5656, already has commit.event task_feed/task_feed_skipped via core.queueFeedCommit ~1965), and drain_close (~5463). Add the missing ledger write for auto-respawn's wouldFire-but-not-canRespawn branch (~5601, carries rs.reason as the ERROR detail) and an auto-continue delivery outcome via the dispatchSerialized delivered callback. Pure helper lands beside queueFeedCommit in cc-core.lua (classify fired vs skipped vs errored → {outcome, detail}). Render: a "failed automations" filter chip in the existing 📜 Audit overlay (claude-dashboard.lua, the ledger row/filter JS ~4591-4649 already special-cases auto_respawn/auto_continue). Folds into queued L4 task_done so per-task and per-automation outcomes share one event family. No new SQL/pagination subsystem — reuse JSONL + the overlay's existing sort/cap (ledgerCapVictims, capped slice ~549).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt FAILS — the ADAPT (reinforces L4) holds. Checked all four downgrade triggers against the actual code: (a) Executor/sandbox? NO. The OpenHands framing ("callback-processor try/except") implies an in-process agent loop, but Shepherd's four automations ALREADY run in-process in the ~1s refresh loop and ALREADY call ledgerFor: auto_respawn (claude-dashboard.lua:5595), auto_continue (:5615), router/auto-feed (:5520 via core.queueFeedCommit), drain_close (:5463). The feature is an outcome/detail FIELD on those existing writes — no new engine, sandbox, or action execution. Trigger does not apply. (b) Server/multi-user/secrets? NO. It's an append to the shipped opt-in JSONL ledger (cc-ledger/). No server, users, or keys. Trigger does not apply. (c) Redundant / already queued? This is the strongest angle and it justifies the ADD->ADAPT downgrade the judge already made — it does NOT reach DONT_ADD. Shepherd's ledger already records these automation fires with timestamps + ids + details, the 📜 Audit overlay already filters/sorts/caps (ledgerCapVictims, capped slice), and cc-core already models the success-vs-skipped convention (queueFeedCommit at cc-core.lua:1965-1967 returns task_feed vs task_feed_skipped). L4 in todos.md (lines 427-431) explicitly queues a task_done outcome-counter event. So the bulk is shipped/queued — hence not a fresh ADD. But it is NOT fully redundant: a genuine ERROR outcome is unrecorded today — the auto-respawn wouldFire-but-not-canRespawn branch (claude-dashboard.lua:5601-5602) only does `print(... rs.reason)` with NO ledgerFor write. Standardizing a success/error outcome field + ledgering that failure branch + a failed-automations filter chip is a real, small extension of the shipped ledger and a down-payment on L4's outcome counters. That is exactly ADAPT. (d) Plumbing mistaken for a console feature? The SQL-table/pagination/try-except framing IS OpenHands server plumbing, correctly stripped. The residual end-user value (audit which automations fired, when, and whether they failed) is a legitimate watch/steer-console need and maps onto JSONL + the existing overlay. Conclusion: the double mismatch does not apply; the feature is neither pure ADD (ledger + overlay + L4 task_done exist) nor DONT_ADD (real unrecorded ERROR branch at :5601 + useful failed-automation filter). ADAPT reinforcing L4 stands. Relevant files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/claude-dashboard.lua (fire points 5463, 5520, 5595, 5601, 5615; overlay filter JS 4588-4650), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua (queueFeedCommit 1965-1968, NARRATE event kinds 642-667, lineage/standup counters 1048-1215), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md (L4 task_done outcome counters 427-431).

#### Per-session git changes / diff view  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** This is one of the rare OpenHands features that does NOT hit the double mismatch. OpenHands only needs the app-server proxy + runtime session key (_proxy_git_runtime_call) because the agent's working tree lives in a Docker/remote sandbox it must reach across; that whole layer is incidental plumbing, not the value. Shepherd has no sandbox: every session's working tree is a real LOCAL folder it already tracks as item.cwd, and Shepherd ALREADY shells out to local git in steady state — FX.gitRoot (claude-dashboard.lua:954, git -C <cwd> rev-parse, with repo/non-repo caching), git remote get-url origin (:1012), and git-root collision detection (:5360). So 'git -C <cwd> status --porcelain' and 'git -C <cwd> diff' are a direct, cheap read with zero new infrastructure (no executor, no sandbox, no server, no secrets). The need is also explicitly documented as a gap: cc-core.lua:1128 says the shift report is 'ops only' and refuses a 'what shipped' line BECAUSE 'Shepherd has no git/CI/diff ground truth.' This feature IS that ground truth — it lets a watch/steer console answer 'what did this session actually change?', the natural complement to status tiles and the shift report. The status-icon model (added/deleted/modified/renamed/untracked), the 100-file cap, and the 'not a git repo'/'waiting' graceful states all map cleanly onto an existing per-session overlay/tab.
- **Against:** The literal OpenHands implementation is a web-IDE artifact: a Monaco DiffEditor per file with a three-way old/diff/new VIEW_MODES toggle, rendered in a React frontend (file-diff-viewer.tsx, use-unified-git-diff.ts). Monaco is a full code-editor component — far too heavy for Shepherd's embedded Hammerspoon webview, and load-bearing in OpenHands precisely because that app IS the editor, which Shepherd structurally is not (the actual code editor is the user's VS Code/Cursor window, one jump-to-window away). Shepherd also deliberately declined a 'what shipped' surface (cc-core.lua:1128, todos.md:161) on the principle that a prompt is an instruction not an outcome; a naive diff tab risks re-introducing exactly the outcome-claiming the design rejected. Live diffs against a 1s refresh could also be noisy/expensive on large repos if not gated, and the value is partly redundant: the user can already jump to the real editor's source-control view in one click. So copying it as-is is wrong on both UX weight and design philosophy.
- **Ruling:** The underlying NEED is real and uniquely well-suited to Shepherd because the working tree is local — this is not blocked by the double mismatch (the proxy/runtime/sandbox is the only OpenHands-specific part, and it dissolves locally). But it must be reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives rather than copied: not a Monaco side-by-side IDE component, but a per-session 'Changes' overlay tab (mirroring the existing audit/shift/lineage overlays) showing 'git status --porcelain -z' files with the status icons and a plain unified-diff text view, computed lazily on tab-open (NOT every 1s tick) and reusing the cached FX.gitRoot resolver. It directly extends the shipped Fleet shift report from 'what the fleet DID (ops)' toward 'what each session CHANGED', converting a documented self-imposed limitation into a real capability. Because it's an observability extension of shipped routing/shift-report work rather than a new system, I tag reinforces=L4 (the nearest queued observability/orchestration line) while noting it's most precisely an extension of the shipped shift report. Verdict ADAPT, high confidence.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a per-session 'Changes' overlay tab (same overlay framework as the audit/shift/lineage tabs). On tab-open for the selected tile: resolve root via cached FX.gitRoot(item.cwd); if nil -> 'not a git repo' state; if cwd missing -> 'waiting' state. New FX.gitChanges(cwd) runs 'git -C <q> status --porcelain=v1 -z' (cap 100, newest/sorted) and FX.gitDiff(cwd, path, ref?) runs 'git -C <q> diff [ref] -- <path>'. Parse porcelain in pure cc-core (core.parseGitStatus -> {path,status,renamedFrom}) so it is unit-testable with the same status taxonomy (A/D/M/R/??); render status icons + a collapsible per-file unified-diff <pre> in the webview (NOT Monaco). Compute lazily on open + a manual refresh button, never on the 1s loop. Off by default / opt-in like every other automation surface. Optionally feed a per-session 'files touched' count into the shift report and lineage detail line, finally giving the deliberately-omitted 'what changed' signal (cc-core.lua:1128) without claiming outcomes. Reuses item.cwd, FX.gitRoot cache, the overlay/tab + drawer-row pattern, and the existing local-git-on-PATH convention.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails on the verdict; succeeds only on the reinforcement tag. The double mismatch genuinely DISSOLVES locally: a session's working tree is on the local filesystem at item.cwd, so `git -C <cwd> status --porcelain`/`git diff` needs NO sandbox, NO in-process executor, NO agent loop, NO server/auth/secrets. Shepherd already shells out to git this exact way (FX.gitRoot at claude-dashboard.lua:954 with the gitRootByCwd cache; FX.runImprove at :1012 runs `git -C <cwd> remote get-url`). The only OpenHands-specific piece — the proxy/Docker runtime and its 'waiting for runtime' state — is precisely what evaporates locally (the judge correctly remaps it to a 'cwd missing' state). NOT redundant: the shipped shift report explicitly omits this (todos.md:161 'Ops only — no what shipped line') and cc-core.lua:1128 documents the deliberate gap ('Shepherd has no git/CI/diff ground truth'); this feature fills that self-imposed limitation. The judge also correctly stripped the heavy part: there is ZERO Monaco/DiffEditor/CodeMirror/iframe in the codebase (it's a plain hs.webview with no bundler), and the audit overlay already has a real tab framework (.a-tab Timeline/Alerts/Shift, auditTab() switching, <pre> bodies) plus the local-git-on-PATH convention — so a 'Changes' overlay tab rendering parseGitStatus + a unified-diff <pre>, computed lazily on tab-open (never on the 1s tick) and off by default, sits cleanly on existing primitives. WHERE THE JUDGE OVER-REACHED (the only refutable part): the reinforces=L4 tag is wrong. L4 is 'Declarative routing & orchestration' and explicitly DROPS in-process execution (todos.md:395-434) — a git-diff observability tab is not a routing feature. The honest mapping (which the judge half-admitted) is that it extends the SHIPPED shift report / session lineage observability, not the queued L4 routing line; no queued Lx covers 'what each session changed.' So the verdict stays ADAPT (it's an extension of an existing/shipped feature, reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives), but the reinforcement target should be the shipped shift-report/lineage observability, not L4. One caveat to honor in build: exclude remote/bridge tiles (their cwd is a REMOTE path; running local git there is meaningless — same exclusion the collision detector already applies at :5352).

#### Event-callback registry + dispatcher (named, typed automation rules bound to conversation + event kind, run in sequence)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** 
- **Against:** 
- **Ruling:** The need is real and already partially built, but the OpenHands implementation hits both halves of the double mismatch (it is server+SQL+webhook AND assumes the in-process executor for most processors) and, more decisively, directly contradicts the architectural decision L4 already records ("no firing engine; the level-triggered dispatcher + the ledger ARE the engine"). That makes it a textbook ADAPT-reinforces-L4 rather than ADD (cannot copy the bus) or DONT_ADD (the abstraction genuinely transfers). The reshape: do NOT build an event bus or a callback-row table that fires on incoming events. Instead, fold the GOOD parts into the existing level-triggered loop — (1) generalize the scattered `enabled` flags into a small set of named, opt-in automation rules keyed by (projectKey/sessionKey, trigger-kind) evaluated each tick by pure predicates beside `shouldFeed`/`shouldAutoContinue`/`isHung`; (2) adopt the self-mutating lifecycle status (ACTIVE/DISABLED/COMPLETED/ERROR, run-once, retry-until-success, auto-retire) as the persisted rule shape — that is the most valuable import and the cleanest loop-guard; (3) keep "run in deliberate sequence, not parallel" — it matches Shepherd's existing single-dispatcher-per-tick / staggered-keystroke discipline; (4) cap the processor catalog to what is locally expressible: ledger-append (logging), relabel (set-title), nudge, the routing feed. Skip SQL/webhook/Python-import entirely. This is squarely L4's territory (declarative orchestration on the shipped routing dispatcher), and it is the abstraction L4 needs to make its route labels and handoffs feel like first-class named rules rather than ad-hoc flags.
- **How (Shepherd):** Plug into L4 (declarative routing & orchestration) as the "named automation rule" data model, layered on the existing level-triggered dispatcher — NOT a new event bus. Concretely: a small `cc-rules.json`-style registry of opt-in rules `{name, status(ACTIVE|DISABLED|COMPLETED|ERROR), trigger:{scope:projectKey|sessionKey, kind:done|error|hung|approval|starved}, processor:{kind:feed|continue|nudge|relabel|log, ...}, once?:bool, retryUntil?:bool}` evaluated each refresh tick by pure cc-core predicates that sit alongside `shouldFeed` (~1975), `shouldAutoContinue` (~2200), `isHung`/`applyProgress` (~2375). Status self-mutation runs through the existing `fx` table (persist the rule file) the same way `routePending`/`alerted` markers are managed today; lifecycle transitions emit a `by:"rule"` ledger event reusing `narrateEvent`/`filterLedger`. The processor catalog maps onto already-present effects: log→`appendLedger`, relabel→`setLabel`, nudge→`handleAction "nudge"`, feed→`routeTask`. Reuses L1/L3's name→resolver and the validator family (~3590–3722) for the rule registry. Sequential execution = today's single-dispatcher-per-tick + staggered-keystroke order.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT-reinforces-L4 ruling holds. I verified the judge's three load-bearing claims directly against the source. 1) The double mismatch is real but the judge already strips it correctly. The OpenHands feature literally is a "return-value event bus" / firing engine ("on each incoming event the engine selects ACTIVE callbacks and runs them in sequence"), and its processors live inside the agent loop. todos.md:401-402 explicitly DROPs exactly this: "DROP all decorators / in-process method execution / return-value event bus / in-memory flow state — Shepherd has no firing engine; the level-triggered dispatcher + the ledger ARE the engine." So if the ruling had been ADD (copy the bus), I'd downgrade. But the ruling is ADAPT, and the reshape explicitly says do NOT build an event bus or a callback-row table that fires on incoming events — fold only the lifecycle-status data model onto the existing tick. That survives the mismatch. 2) The mapped processors require NO executor/sandbox/server. I confirmed all four pre-exist as LOCAL effects: log->FX.appendLedger (claude-dashboard.lua:532), relabel->core.setLabel (cc-core.lua:307), nudge->core.handleAction "nudge" (dashboard ~2219), feed->core.routeTask (cc-core.lua:2062). None call an LLM, run an agent, or touch a network/SQL/secret. No (a)-executor and no (b)-server/secrets dependency remains after the reshape. 3) NOT redundant — the imported abstraction genuinely does not exist yet. There is no callback/processor/event-bus/registry machinery anywhere in cc-core (grep found only a metrics-bucket dispatch table), and there is no cc-rules.json or any "named automation rule" data model. Today the automation flags are genuinely SCATTERED across separate ad-hoc config branches — queue.routing.enabled, hung, drain, respawn.enabled, autoContinue.enabled (verified in cc-config.example.json) — each with bespoke per-folder budget bookkeeping (e.g. stepAutoContinue at cc-core.lua:2220, the routePending markers at 2007). The judge's import (a unified, opt-in, named-rule shape with self-mutating lifecycle status ACTIVE/DISABLED/COMPLETED/ERROR + run-once/retry-until-success/auto-retire) is a real abstraction OVER those flags, evaluated each tick beside the existing pure predicates shouldFeed (1975), shouldAutoContinue (2200), isHung (2375). That is precisely L4's declared territory (declarative routing/orchestration on the shipped routing dispatcher) and the thing L4 needs to make its route labels/handoffs feel like first-class named rules rather than scattered flags. Could not justify DONT_ADD (abstraction transfers and is not already built/queued as a concrete shape) nor ADD (cannot copy the bus; it reinforces queued L4). ADAPT-reinforces-L4 is the correct verdict. Key files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md (L4 section lines 395-434; the "no firing engine" sentence at 401-402); /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua (predicates 1975/2200/2375, dispatcher 2007-2098, setLabel 307); /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/claude-dashboard.lua (FX.appendLedger 532, handleAction); /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-config.example.json (scattered enabled flags).

### Reinforces L3 — templates  (5)

#### Webhook-driven issue/PR resolver: triggers -> rendered seed prompt -> agent job  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L3
- **For:** The valuable, transferable core of this feature is "an inbound signal selects a parameterized template, renders a seed prompt, and launches a worker to act on it." Strip the server/sandbox and that is precisely the local Shepherd loop: a trigger -> rendered prompt -> spawned Claude Code session. Two parts map cleanly onto already-queued work. (1) The Jinja issue_prompt/conversation_instructions templates, and the per-provider prompt-convention templates (branch/PR naming, "ask before pushing"), are exactly L3 parameterized+versioned prompt templates with a local definition source — they become {name, description, expected_output} records in cc-templates.json / the ~/.claude/agents dir, rendered via the planned templateVars/renderTemplate before feeding spawnSpec's [prompt]. The "resolver convention per repo" is just a saved L1 agent profile + L3 template, not a new system. (2) The classify-event-then-route step (issue-label vs @openhands mention vs PR-comment -> pick the handling flow) is exactly L4's @router: a route label on a queued task resolving to a target member behind the queue.routing.enabled double opt-in. So the NEED — "a structured external prompt convention turns a unit of work into a ready-to-paste seed for a session" — is real and already has homes (L1/L3/L4). The local 'claude' CLI's --append-system-prompt/--agent makes emitting the rendered seed feasible without any server.
- **Against:** Almost everything load-bearing in this feature is the OpenHands double mismatch in pure form, and lands on the DONT_ADD list. The trigger TRANSPORT is a network server: a GitHub App / webhook receiver that Shepherd categorically cannot host (NO network server). Verifying the signature/access requires a stored webhook secret and per-provider OAuth — Shepherd stores NO secrets and has NO multi-user auth. The "launch an agent (ConversationTrigger.RESOLVER) that branches/commits/runs-tests/pushes/opens-a-PR" is the in-process EXECUTOR running inside a managed sandbox — Shepherd runs no agent loop and manages no sandbox; the agent here is an already-running Claude Code session it only watches/steers. The per-provider Manager+View+callback fan-out across GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket/Azure/Forgejo/Jira/Linear/Slack is multi-tenant SaaS integration plumbing with credentialed clients — none of which Shepherd can or should host. has_exact_mention's word-boundary regex, the GitHub-App reactions, and the _add_reaction acknowledgement are all server-side artifacts. If you remove the server, the secrets, the multi-provider OAuth, and the sandboxed executor, what remains is just "render a template and spawn a session" — which Shepherd already plans (L3) and partly has. So adding the feature as-described is impossible, and the residue is not new.
- **Ruling:** Decompose into transport + classification + render + execution. Transport (webhook server, signature/secret verification, per-provider OAuth Managers/Views/callbacks) and execution (sandboxed agent that commits/pushes/opens PRs via ConversationTrigger.RESOLVER) are the double mismatch verbatim: no server, no secrets, no users, no executor, no sandbox -> not buildable. That kills ADD outright. But the remaining two seams are genuinely useful and already queued, so this is not DONT_ADD either. The render seam — a parameterized seed prompt with per-provider conventions (branch/PR naming, ask-before-pushing) — is a textbook L3 case: those j2 templates become local template/agent records rendered by the planned templateVars/renderTemplate into spawnSpec's [prompt], with the per-repo convention captured as an L1 agent profile. The classification seam — event-type -> handling flow -> target — is L4's @router (route label -> member resolution behind the existing double opt-in). Net: ADAPT, reinforces L3 (primary: the seed-prompt-template-with-conventions is the heart of the feature's reusable value), with a clear L4 touchpoint for the routing half. Weighting the user's stated priority favorably reinforces this: "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory" is exactly the resolver-convention-as-saved-profile shape, minus the server. Effort to capture the residue is essentially already covered by L1+L3+L4 scope; no new primitive needed.
- **How (Shepherd):** Capture only the render + classify residue; build nothing of the transport/executor. L3 (primary): model each provider's resolver convention as an L3 template record in cc-templates.json (or a ~/.claude/agents file) — {name, description, expected_output, vars} with built-in {{var}} interpolation for issue/comment body, repo, branch-name convention, and an "ask before pushing" line baked into the prompt text; render via the planned templateVars/renderTemplate -> spawnSpec [prompt] / spawnFlags --append-system-prompt, human-submits via the no-auto-send templateInsert path. The per-repo convention bundles into an L1 agent profile (folder + provider + permMode + that L3 seed template + a read-only/ask-before-push L2 policy bundle). L4 (secondary): the event-type -> flow selection becomes a route label on the queued task (queueSplitLines @review:-style prefix) resolved by the planned label->target resolver beside routePick, behind queue.routing.enabled. Explicitly DROP: the webhook server, signature/secret verification, per-provider OAuth Managers/Views/callbacks, has_exact_mention regex + GitHub-App reactions, jira_dc service-account env, and the sandboxed branch/commit/push/open-PR executor.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute and could not break the ADAPT. The feature decomposes into transport + classify + render + execution. Two of the four seams are the double mismatch verbatim and the judge already kills them: the webhook server, signature/secret verification, and per-provider OAuth Managers/Views/callbacks are server+secrets+auth (Shepherd has none), and the ConversationTrigger.RESOLVER agent that branches/commits/runs-tests/pushes/opens-a-PR is the in-process executor+sandbox (Shepherd runs no agent loop and only watches status files + steers via keystrokes/hooks). That correctly forecloses ADD. So my refutation had to land on either (1) the residue is too thin to count, or (2) it is fully redundant with queued L3 -> DONT_ADD. Both fail. (1) fails because the surviving residue is not generic glue: the OpenHands resolver's distinctive transferable asset is the per-provider seed-prompt CONVENTION — parameterized over issue/comment body + repo, with branch/PR-naming and an explicit 'ask before pushing' line baked into the prompt text. That is the literal heart of queued L3 (todos.md ~354-393): templateVars/renderTemplate -> spawnSpec [prompt] / --append-system-prompt, the {description, expected_output} structured record, built-in {{var}} interpolation, and attachments[]. It also matches the user's stated #1 priority ('pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory') as a saved L1 agent profile (folder + provider + permMode + that L3 seed template + a read-only/ask-before-push L2 bundle). So real, console-meaningful value survives. (2) fails because 'the residue is already covered by queued L3, no new primitive needed' is exactly the definition of ADAPT-reinforces in this rubric, not DONT_ADD — DONT_ADD is reserved for features whose worth depends on the executor/sandbox/server/auth or that are pure plumbing with no console value, and a saved per-repo resolver convention you spawn a local session from is neither. The classification seam is correctly demoted to secondary: once the webhook is dropped there is no event to classify, so it collapses to L4's route-label on a queued task (@review:-style prefix in queueSplitLines -> a label->target resolver beside routePick, behind queue.routing.enabled). That weakens the L4 half but does not touch the L3 core. Net: ADAPT, reinforces L3 (primary), L4 secondary, with the entire transport/executor majority explicitly DROPPED. The ruling holds.

#### New-conversation / spawn home + canned-prompt macros + git control bar  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L3
- **For:** The valuable core here is the canned-prompt library: a Macros submenu (increase test coverage, fix README, auto-merge PRs, clean dependencies) and a GitControlBar (Repo/Branch/Pull/Push/PR) that inject ready-made prompts with one click, plus TaskSuggestions filtered to the connected repo. In OpenHands these are pure UI that call setMessageToSend — they do NOT run git or the agent; they just hand the agent a prompt. That is exactly Shepherd's steer model, and Shepherd already proves the pattern works: the shipped Improve button resolves the session's git origin remote (FX.runImprove, claude-dashboard.lua:1004-1024 via core.repoFromRemote) and injects a canned "suggest, don't apply" prompt into the running session. So a curated set of one-click starter/git macros, repo-aware, is a low-risk widening of an existing primitive and lands squarely on the user's stated #1 interest in saved, reusable work definitions. Repo-filtering of suggestions is feasible too — Shepherd already caches the per-cwd git root (FX.gitRoot, claude-dashboard.lua:954) so it can key built-in macros to "is this a repo / what repo".
- **Against:** The feature as specified is mostly a HomeScreen rebuild that Shepherd already has, just differently shaped. "Start from scratch" / NewConversation / CreateConversationButton = the in-panel New-session modal (folder browser, recent dirs, new-project, editor/provider/mode pickers, presets) Shepherd shipped. RecentConversations = the live tiles + recent-dirs + session lineage. So porting the Home layout is redundant. The RepoConnector ("launch against a repo+branch") is the one genuinely new idea, but its OpenHands value is cloning/checking-out a remote into a managed workspace — that needs the executor/sandbox/server Shepherd structurally lacks; Shepherd spawns against an already-checked-out LOCAL folder, not a remote URL. And the GitControlBar's Pull/Push/PR only do real work in OpenHands because an agent loop + VCS-token store back them; Shepherd has no secrets store and no executor, so its version is unavoidably just "type a prompt and hope the session does it" — which is already what nudge/templates do. There is a real risk of shipping a redundant Home page plus a git bar that over-promises.
- **Ruling:** No double-mismatch blocker: every sub-feature here is the OpenHands MANAGEMENT/UI layer (setMessageToSend, suggestion lists, a button row), not the executor, sandbox, server, or secrets store. But the value splits three ways. (1) The Home/NewConversation/RecentConversations layout is REDUNDANT — Shepherd already has the New-session modal, presets, tiles, recent-dirs, and lineage; rebuilding it adds nothing. (2) The RepoConnector's clone-a-remote semantics are DONT_ADD — that is sandbox/workspace provisioning; Shepherd only spawns into local folders. (3) The Macros submenu + GitControlBar + repo-filtered TaskSuggestions are the real, transferable nugget, and they are a thin shell over primitives Shepherd already has: saved task TEMPLATES (cc-templates.json, "Tpl ▾" inserts into the input, never auto-sends) and the Improve-button injection-against-a-repo path. Those canned prompts ARE parameterized prompt templates, so this reinforces L3 rather than introducing a parallel "macros" system. The git buttons are just templates whose text says "open a PR for the current branch" / "push" — injected, human-submitted, no git executed by Shepherd. Net: ADAPT, fold the macro/git-prompt set into L3 as built-in, repo-filtered starter templates surfaced as one-click chips; explicitly drop the Home rebuild and the remote-clone RepoConnector.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend L3 (parameterized prompt templates, cc-templates.json) with a small set of BUILT-IN starter templates seeded on first run: "increase test coverage", "fix the README", "open a PR for the current branch", "review and clean unused dependencies", "pull then summarize what changed". Surface them as one-click chips in the detail panel (and optionally in the New-session modal's queue-seed area), reusing the existing template-insert path (claude-dashboard.lua:1886 core.templatePush / queue-seed) so they inject into the chat input and the human submits — never auto-send, honoring off-by-default. Add a pure repo-filter predicate keyed off the already-cached FX.gitRoot(cwd) (claude-dashboard.lua:954) + core.repoFromRemote (already used by Improve) so git-flavored macros only show for sessions whose cwd is a repo; non-repo sessions see only generic macros. The GitControlBar becomes a "Git" chip group of these templates, not new git execution. Use {{branch}}/{{repo}} interpolation from L3's renderTemplate (resolved locally via git -C cwd, same as Improve) so "open a PR" prefills the current branch. Explicitly DROP: the HomeScreen/NewConversation/RecentConversations layout (redundant with the shipped New-session modal + tiles + lineage) and the RepoConnector's clone/checkout-a-remote behavior (executor/sandbox territory Shepherd cannot host).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT (reinforces L3) holds. Verified against the codebase: (1) No double-mismatch on the transferable nugget. The Macros submenu + GitControlBar + repo-filtered TaskSuggestions are OpenHands' MANAGEMENT/UI layer (setMessageToSend-style injection, a suggestion list, a button row) — not the executor, sandbox, server, or secrets store. The git buttons inject prompt TEXT the human submits ("open a PR for the current branch"/"push"); Shepherd runs no git. Confirmed Shepherd has ZERO git clone/checkout/pull/push/gh-pr execution anywhere — the only "clone" tokens are comments about namespacing already-local checkouts (cc-core.lua:2851, claude-dashboard.lua:5353/5404). So the (a)-executor and (b)-server/secrets mismatches do not bite the surviving nugget. (2) It is a true reinforcement of L3, not a parallel system. Verified the exact primitives the judge cited: templatePush/templateList/templateGet store {name,text} in cc-templates.json (cc-core.lua:3578-3638); the no-auto-send insert path templateInsert (claude-dashboard.lua:3476-3485) sets the #nudge input value and focuses it, human submits — never auto-sends, honoring off-by-default; FX.gitRoot (claude-dashboard.lua:954) gives a pure cwd→repo predicate for the repo-filter; core.repoFromRemote + FX.runImprove (claude-dashboard.lua:1004-1058) are the existing inject-against-a-repo path. Canned prompts ARE parameterized prompt templates, so they belong in L3 (todos.md:354-393, which already plans {{var}} interpolation, render-before-spawn via templateInsert, and structured records) rather than a new macros store. (3) I cannot honestly drive it to DONT_ADD: a built-in, repo-filtered starter set is a genuine increment over L3-as-queued (which today holds verbatim text with no seeded built-ins and no repo-filtered chip surfacing) — that is the textbook ADD→ADAPT case (extension/reinforcement of a queued feature), not redundancy. (4) The judge already correctly carved off the DONT_ADD pieces and I confirmed them: the Home/NewConversation/RecentConversations layout is redundant with the shipped New-session modal + tiles + recent-dirs + lineage, and the RepoConnector's clone-a-remote is workspace provisioning = sandbox territory Shepherd cannot host (spawnInner only spawns into an existing local folder path, cc-core.lua:2970). Net: the surviving macro/git-prompt/repo-filter nugget folds into L3 as one-click built-in chips reusing the existing template-insert path; ADAPT stands.

#### Parameterized task templates with typed inputs, regex validation, and {{var}} interpolation (render-before-spawn)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L3
- **For:** This is the cleanest management-layer transfer in the whole OpenHands checkout — it needs NO executor, sandbox, server, users, or secrets. A "task skill with {{var}} placeholders rendered before being sent as the kickoff prompt" is exactly a parameterized spawn/queue seed prompt: Shepherd already stores verbatim task text (cc-templates.json via templatePush, cc-core.lua:3604) and pastes it with NO auto-send (templateInsert, claude-dashboard.lua:3476), so the human-in-the-loop submit boundary is preserved. The slash-command surface (/fix_test, /update_pr_description) maps onto Shepherd's template chips + the New-Session/queue modal. The directional guardrails (fix code not tests) are just prompt content, free to copy. It directly serves the user's stated #1 priority — saved agent/skill defs in a directory — because the per-task-type .j2 prompts rendered with repo/issue/branch terms are precisely the "definition source" L3 calls for. Crucially it adds ONE thing L3 underspecifies: per-input regex validation (validation.pattern '^https://github.com/.+/.+/pull/[0-9]+$'), which is trivially feasible as a Lua pattern (cc-core already validates URL-ish keys and allow/deny content via string:match at cc-core.lua:59, 2904) with zero PCRE dependency — a concrete, low-cost enrichment of the planned input-schema collection.
- **Against:** The build-it-as-is path is contaminated by infrastructure Shepherd structurally lacks. Jinja2 rendering of .j2 templates implies a YAML/Jinja parser Shepherd has no business adding (L3 explicitly says "no YAML parser, no Jinja2 — keep it pure cc-core"). The richest concrete recipes lean on data Shepherd never sees: address_pr_comments / update_pr_description need PR-thread state and a VCS API round-trip; the suggested-task .j2 selection by task_type (merge_conflict/failing_checks/unresolved_comments) presumes an issue→PR resolver and provider-API context that lives entirely in OpenHands' server/integration tier, not on a watch/steer console. Regex validation in PCRE form does not map 1:1 to Lua patterns (no alternation, backrefs, {n,m}), so "regex validation" can only be approximated, not faithfully copied. And the core value — typed inputs + interpolation + render-before-spawn — is already fully queued as L3 down to renderTemplate(text, vars) refusing to render on a missing required var; greenlighting this as a fresh feature risks a parallel system instead of enriching the planned one.
- **Ruling:** Near-verbatim match to the already-queued L3, so the verdict is ADAPT/reinforces=L3, not a new ADD. L3 already specifies {{var}} interpolation, render-before-spawn (refuse on missing required), an input-schema/field-collection modal, a local definition-source directory scanned with rg/fd, and the no-auto-send templateInsert paste path. The double mismatch decides what to keep vs drop: KEEP the typed-inputs list, the {{var}} body interpolation, the per-input description/type, and the directional-guardrail prompt content (pure data, fits spawn/queue seed). ADD the one genuinely-incremental piece L3 omits — per-input validation patterns — implemented as Lua patterns (cc-core already does this kind of match-validation), accepting that PCRE features degrade to Lua-pattern approximations. DROP the Jinja2/.j2 rendering engine (L3 forbids it), the task_type→template selection that presumes the issue→PR resolver, and the recipes that require VCS API/PR-thread state (address_pr_comments, update_pr_description's live PR fetch) — those depend on the server/integration tier Shepherd does not host. The recipes survive only as static seed-prompt templates the human reviews before submit.
- **How (Shepherd):** Enrich the existing cc-templates.json record (currently {name, text}; cc-core.lua:3577-3638) per L3: add an optional inputs[] schema {name, description, type, pattern}. Add pure cc-core functions templateVars(text) (ordered {{name}}/{{name?}} extraction) and renderTemplate(text, vars) -> (rendered, missingRequired) that refuses to render on a missing required var or a value failing its Lua-pattern check. New-Session modal + queue prompt render one validated field per declared input, disabling Spawn/Feed until required+valid, then interpolate into the spawnInner [prompt] arg / --append-system-prompt; paste via the existing no-auto-send templateInsert (claude-dashboard.lua:3476) so the human submits. Ship the OpenHands recipes (/fix_test, /update_test, /address_pr_comments, /update_pr_description) as starter template defs in L3's local definition-source directory, with the GitHub PR_URL pattern translated to a Lua pattern (^https://github%.com/.+/.+/pull/%d+$). Drop the Jinja2 engine and task_type→.j2 selection (no resolver, no VCS API in scope).
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute the ADAPT(reinforces=L3) ruling and could not. The evidence confirms it on every axis. WHAT THE FEATURE ACTUALLY IS (verified in the OpenHands checkout): the four recipes are bare .md files with YAML frontmatter (skills/fix_test.md, update_test.md, update_pr_description.md, address_pr_comments.md). Each declares an inputs:[] list ({name, description, type, optional validation.pattern}) and a {{ VAR }} body. update_pr_description.md carries exactly the pattern "^https://github.com/.+/.+/pull/[0-9]+$". Render = interpolate the inputs into the body, then hand the rendered text to an agent as the kickoff prompt. (a) Executor/sandbox? NO for the portable core. The interpolation/render step is pure string substitution; it needs no agent loop. The "use the GitHub API"/"run the test command" instructions are work the AGENT performs — in OpenHands that's its sandboxed CodeActAgent, in Shepherd it is the already-running Claude Code GUI session. So the template-render-and-paste mechanism transfers cleanly and does NOT secretly need an in-process executor. (a) only bites if Shepherd itself tried to fetch live PR state — which it must not, and which the judge already drops. (b) Server/secrets? YES, but only for the half the judge already DROPs. The per-task-type Jinja2 prompts (merge_conflict/failing_checks/unresolved_comments/open_issue) live in openhands/app_server/integrations/templates/suggested_task/*.j2 and are selected in service_types.py:90-117 (get_prompt_for_task) off a SuggestedTask{git_provider, repo, issue_number} object plus get_provider_terms()'s tokenEnvVar (GITHUB_TOKEN etc.). That object is populated by the server-side VCS resolver scanning a repo's PRs/issues over the network — the multi-provider, credentialed integration tier Shepherd does not host. The judge correctly drops the Jinja2 engine, the task_type→.j2 selection, the issue→PR resolver, and address_pr_comments/update_pr_description's live PR fetch. So (b) refutes only the already-dropped half. (c) Redundant with queued L3? YES — and this is why the verdict is ADAPT, not a new ADD. todos.md L3 (lines 354-393) already specifies: {{var}}/{{name?}} interpolation, renderTemplate→(rendered, missingRequired) that REFUSES on a missing required var (360-362), render-before-spawn with the New-Session/queue modal collecting one field per declared input and disabling Spawn until required are filled (363-365, 390-393), a configurable LOCAL definition-source directory scanned with rg/fd (367-371), the no-auto-send templateInsert paste path (verified at claude-dashboard.lua:3476-3484 — sets the nudge textarea, focuses, human submits), and it EXPLICITLY forbids Jinja2 (372-373). cc-templates.json is today {name,text} (cc-core.lua:3577-3638, templatePush overwrites in place at :3613), matching the judge's mapping exactly. A parallel ADD would duplicate L3, so the verdict cannot go up. GENUINELY-INCREMENTAL PIECE confirms reinforces=L3 (not DONT_ADD): per-input regex validation. L3's input-schema collection declares required/optional vars but omits per-input validation patterns. This one bit is a real extension, implementable as Lua patterns — cc-core already does match-validation (resolveGateTools s:match(\"%S\") at :2538, regex-escape at :818, normGitUrl gsubs at :471-475), and the GitHub PR_URL pattern degrades cleanly to ^https://github%.com/.+/.+/pull/%d+$. The typed-inputs list, {{var}} body interpolation, and directional-guardrail prompt content (fix_test = \"DO NOT modify the tests yourself\"; update_test = inverse) are pure data that ride the existing spawn/queue seed path. CONCLUSION: cannot downgrade to DONT_ADD (the core is portable, local, single-user, no-secrets, and improves the spawn/queue console) and cannot raise to a parallel ADD (near-verbatim to queued L3). ADAPT, reinforces L3, holds. Relevant files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md (L3 at 354-393, L1 batch fan-out at 294), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua:3577-3638 (templates) and :2537-2547/:818 (Lua-pattern validation precedent), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/claude-dashboard.lua:3476-3484 (no-auto-send templateInsert), /Users/adam/Programming/check out/OpenHands/skills/*.md (portable recipes), /Users/adam/Programming/check out/OpenHands/openhands/app_server/integrations/service_types.py:90-117 + templates/suggested_task/*.j2 (the dropped server/resolver tier).

#### Suggested-tasks discovery feed  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L3
- **For:** The "discover external work items → render a ready-made per-type prompt → launch a fix in a session" loop is NOT foreign to Shepherd — it already ships exactly one instance of it: the Improve button (cc-core.lua:481 improvePrompt + claude-dashboard.lua:1004 FX.runImprove) pulls a repo's un-applied leaderboard cards over HTTP, builds a review-first prompt, and injects it into a session. The candidate is the same shape with a richer, more useful source (assigned issues, your open PRs, failing CI, merge conflicts, unresolved review comments). The data is genuinely read-only and obtainable with the user's OWN auth via the local `gh` CLI / `git` — no secrets are stored (same posture as improveCredsRead reading LB creds from the shell env, and the providers tab resolving $VARs through the spawned shell). It cleanly feeds three already-queued primitives: each TaskType's prompt = an L3 parameterized template ({{repo}}/{{issue}}/{{pr}}), "launch a fix" = spawn-from-agent (L1) + enqueue onto the armed project's queue (L4 routing / queuePush). For a fleet console this is a high-value triage surface: a single pane that says "here is the actionable maintenance backlog across your repos, click to hand one to a session" is squarely in the watch/steer/spawn mission and matches the user's stated priority of handing work to saved agents.
- **Against:** OpenHands' implementation is server-shaped: a ProviderHandler that fans out authenticated GraphQL/REST queries across multiple stored-credential providers, a paginated FastAPI endpoint (SuggestedTaskPage), and per-provider features.py — all the multi-tenant/secrets-store plumbing Shepherd structurally rejects. Built as-is it would pull Shepherd toward being an API client with provider tokens, which the hard scope forbids. There is also a meaningful altitude question: live network polling of GitHub for CI/PR state on a ~1s-ish console is a different cadence and failure mode than reading local cc-status files, and it duplicates work a developer often already does in the GitHub UI / `gh`. The "actionable work" most relevant to a Claude Code fleet (failing CI on a branch a session is editing, merge conflicts) is arguably better surfaced by the session itself than by a separate polling feed. So the as-is feature is the wrong altitude, and even adapted it risks being a heavier integration than its incremental value over the existing Improve button + manual `gh` triage.
- **Ruling:** Not ADD: the OpenHands artifact (multi-provider fan-out, paginated router, stored per-user secrets) is server/multi-tenant plumbing that violates the hard scope, so it can't be copied close to as-is. Not DONT_ADD: the value does NOT depend on the agent-execution loop, the sandbox, multi-user auth, or stored secrets — the work-item data is read-only and fetchable with the user's own `gh`/`git` auth resolved through the spawned shell, exactly like the existing Improve button and provider env injection already do. The double mismatch therefore does not bite; this is the management/discovery layer, not the executor or the server. Verdict is ADAPT: reshape it as a local, opt-in discovery feed that reuses Shepherd's own primitives. It reinforces L3 most directly (the per-TaskType Jinja2 prompt is the load-bearing transferable piece → versioned parameterized templates with {{repo}}/{{issue}} interpolation), and chains into L1 (launch = spawn-from-saved-agent) and L4 (enqueue/route the rendered task). Confidence is high that the NEED fits and the secrets/server objections are avoidable; the build is gated only by a UX/altitude decision (which TaskTypes are worth polling, and at what cadence) — so keep it OFF by default like every other automation. Drop MERGE_CONFLICTS/FAILING_CHECKS scope to what `gh`/`git` can answer cheaply and let the rest be manual.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a local, opt-in "Work feed" overlay (sibling to the ☰ drawer items / audit overlay). A pure cc-core aggregator + an FX layer that shells `gh` (detect→use→degrade per the tooling stance; absent gh = feed disabled, never broken) reads per-repo actionable items: `gh issue list --assignee @me`, `gh pr list --author @me`, `gh pr checks` (FAILING_CHECKS), mergeable state (MERGE_CONFLICTS), `gh pr view --json reviewThreads` (UNRESOLVED_COMMENTS). Pure `core.classifyWorkItems`/`workFeed` tag each with a TaskType + repo/ref, mirroring service_types.py. Repos come from the same git roots Shepherd already discovers (FX.gitRoot / spawn folder index) — no provider-token store; gh uses the user's own login. Per-TaskType prompt = an L3 template (extend cc-templates.json with {{repo}}/{{issue}}/{{pr_number}} interpolation + a render-before-spawn step), replacing OpenHands' suggested_task/*.j2. One-click "Fix this" renders the L3 template and either injects it into a selected session (the Improve/pasteIntoWindow path, dispatchSerialized) or spawns from a saved L1 agent and enqueues onto the armed project's queue via queuePush (L4 routing). Poll cadence is NOT the 1s tick — a separate slow timer (≥60s, like the plan-window usage poll) or on-demand "Refresh". Off by default; ledger a `work_feed_dispatch` event.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; ADAPT holds. I checked all four common-error axes against the actual codebase. (a) NO executor/sandbox: the feed only READS work-item data and renders a prompt; the "Fix this" action is the EXISTING paste-into-window (FX.pasteIntoWindow, the Improve path at claude-dashboard.lua:1054) or spawn-from-agent + queuePush — no agent loop, no Docker/remote runtime. The executor objection does not bite. (b) NO secrets/server/multi-user: I verified `gh` is installed (/opt/homebrew/bin/gh v2.79.0) and authed as the user's own account WSAdam with the token in the macOS keyring — Shepherd stores nothing, identical in spirit to the authTokenEnv/providerEnv name-not-value stance (cc-core ~2714). "No network server" means Shepherd doesn't RUN a server; it already makes OUTBOUND calls (hs.http to /api/oauth/usage, ntfy, the leaderboard). Shelling read-only `gh`/`git` is more conservative and fits the detect->use->degrade tooling stance (absent gh = feed disabled). Shepherd ALREADY shells git via hs.execute (git remote get-url origin; git rev-parse --show-toplevel at claude-dashboard.lua:961/1012). The network/secrets objection does not bite. (c) NOT redundant: grepped todos.md + both mining reports — there is NO prior work-feed/suggested-tasks/discovery item; this is genuinely new surface, so DONT_ADD-as-redundant is wrong. (d) The OpenHands artifact (multi-provider fan-out, paginated router, per-user secrets store) IS server/multi-tenant plumbing and correctly cannot be copied as-is — which is exactly why the verdict is ADAPT not ADD. The end-user value (surface actionable work; one click to launch a fix) is a legitimate feature for a console whose reason to exist is watch/steer/spawn. Two precision notes on the judge's reasoning that do NOT change the verdict: (1) the analogy "exactly like the existing Improve button" is imprecise — the Improve button uses hs.http.asyncPost to a configured leaderboard server with a token from ~/.zshrc, NOT `gh`; the load-bearing claim (read-only via the user's own CLI auth + Shepherd's git-discovery primitives) still holds. (2) The transferable KERNEL is the per-TaskType {{repo}}/{{issue}} parameterized prompt = squarely L3, with launch=L1 spawn-from-agent and enqueue=L4 routing; the polling/aggregation/classify shell is net-new glue (correctly NOT ADD, since it can't be lifted as-is) that must be off by default on a slow timer (like the 180s OFFICIAL_TTL usage poll at claude-dashboard.lua:404), ledgered. So "ADAPT, reinforces L3, chains L1/L4" is the right shape for a local, opt-in, single-user feed.

#### Issue/PR comment & review-thread reader (resolver context fetch)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L3
- **For:** The NEED is real and already half-built in Shepherd. Addressing PR review feedback / resolving an issue is one of the most common things a human hands to a local Claude Code session, and the bottleneck is exactly the manual copy-paste of the issue body + comment thread (especially inline review threads, which are tedious to reconstruct by hand). Shepherd ALREADY validates this pattern: the Improve button pulls external context (claimed leaderboard cards), runs M.improvePrompt (cc-core.lua:481) to build a review-first instruction, and pastes it into a session — fetch-external-context-then-build-a-paste-prompt is a shipped Shepherd idiom. Critically, the usual blockers don't apply: this needs no executor and no sandbox (it is pure read + string-build), and no secrets store — the local `gh` CLI is already authenticated on the user's machine, so `gh pr view --json title,body,comments,reviews` / `gh api .../comments` resolves locally through the spawned shell exactly the way Shepherd already resolves $ENV. The review-thread walk (comment -> root, collecting file/line) is just GraphQL that `gh api graphql` can run. Dropped into L3 as a "resolve issue" / "address PR review" template whose render step shells out to gh to fill {{issue_body}}/{{thread}}, it turns a 5-minute manual paste into one click on a session tile.
- **Against:** Most of OpenHands' engineering value here is plumbing Shepherd would throw away: four provider implementations (GitHub/GitLab/Azure/Bitbucket), a Comment normalization layer, and a GraphQL review-thread walker — all of which exist to feed a SERVER-SIDE agent loop and a per-user secrets store, neither of which Shepherd has. The reply_to_pr_comment half is squarely executor territory (Shepherd never acts back on the PR; the human/session does). It also widens Shepherd's surface into VCS-provider territory it has deliberately stayed out of — there is currently zero `gh`/GitHub code in cc-core.lua or claude-dashboard.lua, so this introduces a brand-new external dependency and per-provider divergence (only GitHub via gh is realistic locally; GitLab/Azure/Bitbucket would be unauthenticated dead ends). And it is arguably redundant: a user can already paste a PR URL or `gh pr view` output into a session by hand, or use the existing /improve and bulk-paste paths. The marginal convenience may not justify owning gh-output parsing and the fragility of GraphQL thread reconstruction.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: neither blocker fires. No executor/sandbox is required (read + template only), and no secrets store is required because the local authenticated `gh` CLI is Shepherd's exact analogue to OpenHands' per-user creds — it resolves through the spawned shell, never stored. So this is not DONT_ADD. But it is not ADD either: the feature's worth in Shepherd is NOT the four-provider normalization or the server-fed Jinja templates — it is the single outcome of giving a running session the PR/issue conversation as paste context. That outcome already has a home: the Improve-button precedent (fetch external context -> build review-first prompt -> paste) plus L3 (parameterized, versioned templates with {{var}} interpolation and render-before-spawn from a local definition source). Reshaping onto L3 keeps the genuine need while discarding the server/sandbox/multi-provider plumbing. Hence ADAPT, reinforces L3. Scope it to GitHub-via-gh first (the only locally-authenticated path), human-submits, no reply-back. Confidence high: the fit to an existing shipped pattern (Improve) and a queued item (L3) is unusually clean.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add an L3 template variant "PR review feedback" / "resolve issue" whose render step shells out through fx (NOT pure cc-core) to `gh`: `gh pr view <url|n> --json title,body,comments,reviews,reviewThreads` and/or `gh api graphql` to walk inline review threads, then a pure cc-core normalizer (mirroring M.parseLedger / improvePrompt) collapses the JSON into a chronological, truncated thread string bound to template vars {{issue_body}}/{{thread}}/{{file}}:{{line}}. The rendered text feeds the existing render-before-spawn -> spawnSpec [prompt] / --append-system-prompt path (cc-core.lua:3061) or the paste-into-session path the Improve button uses (M.improvePrompt, cc-core.lua:481). gh availability + auth is a pre-flight check like L1's validation; truncation cap mirrors the ledger export limit pattern. Read-only and human-submit by default (no reply_to_pr_comment, in keeping with HITL scope). GitHub-only to start; other providers explicitly out of scope.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted on all four common errors; none flips the verdict, so the ADAPT(high)→reinforces-L3 ruling holds. (a) Executor/sandbox: does NOT fire. The feature is read-only (`gh pr view --json ...` / `gh api graphql` to walk review threads) + a pure normalizer + paste-as-context. No agent loop, no Docker, no action execution. Correctly NOT a blocker. (b) Secrets/server/multi-user: does NOT fire, though this was the strongest angle. I verified Shepherd shells out only to `git` (claude-dashboard.lua:1012, `git remote get-url origin`) and NEVER to `gh` (grep for gh across all .lua/.sh: zero hits). The shipped Improve button fetches via `hs.http.asyncPost` to a private leaderboard with a stored `GRADE_PREVIEW_TOKEN` (claude-dashboard.lua:991-1062) — that IS a token, but it's read from the shell env (`zsh -ic`, line 997), never stored by Shepherd. `gh` is the same shape: a LOCAL CLI carrying its own OAuth token (~/.config/gh), resolved through the spawned shell, never persisted in cc-config. That matches context.md's exact carve-out ("resolves $ENV vars via the spawned shell, never stores keys"). So the no-secrets-store limit is not violated. (c) Redundant/already-queued: this is the crux and is why it's ADAPT not ADD. The feature's only transferable worth is the single outcome "give a running session the PR/issue conversation as paste context." That outcome already has a home: the shipped Improve precedent (fetch external context → review-first prompt → paste via FX.pasteIntoWindow / core.improvePrompt, cc-core.lua:481) PLUS queued L3 (render-before-spawn templates, {{var}} interpolation, templateInsert no-auto-send human-submit path, cc-core.lua:354-393 / claude-dashboard.lua:3480). The OpenHands-specific machinery — four-provider normalization, server-fed Jinja templates, resolver→PR reply-back — is exactly L3's DROP list ("credentialed git/SaaS network clients", "no Jinja2"). Reshaping onto L3 keeps the need, discards the plumbing. ADD would wrongly re-import the redundant normalization/templates. (d) Server/library detail mistaken for a console feature: partially true of the OpenHands source (it's a resolver backend), but the judge already scopes OUT the reply-back/automation and keeps only the end-user paste-context outcome. So this doesn't justify DONT_ADD either. Best refutation I can mount: L3's definition-source bullet says "Strictly local-disk — no network client, no token storage," and a `gh` fetch reaches the network. But `gh` is a locally-authenticated CLI shelled out to (like the existing `git` call), not a credentialed client Shepherd builds/stores — the DROP list targets Bitbucket/GitLab/Langfuse token clients, not the user's own `gh`. The friction sharpens the scoping (GitHub-via-gh only, pre-flight gh-auth check like L1 validation, truncation cap like the ledger export, read-only + human-submit, no reply_to_pr_comment) but does not flip the verdict. Conclusion: the double mismatch genuinely fails to fire, and the fit to the shipped Improve flow + queued L3 is clean. Holds as ADAPT, reinforces L3.

### Reinforces L2 — policy bundles  (2)

#### Human-in-the-loop confirmation buttons (approve/deny, HIGH-risk banner, keyboard shortcuts, double-submit guard, lock badge)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L2
- **For:** Human-in-the-loop approve/deny is literally Shepherd's reason to exist, so this feature lands dead-center in scope — no executor or sandbox needed, it's pure watch-and-steer. Decomposed, the candidate has five parts and the two that Shepherd lacks are genuinely valuable and cheap: (1) in-panel keyboard shortcuts to confirm/reject the selected tile's pending request (today there's only a global Cmd+Alt+A "approve the front approval" hotkey — nothing to deny-by-key or to act on the *selected* tile), which speeds the operator's core loop; and (2) a per-pending-action HIGH-risk banner. Shepherd's risk signal today is a *per-session* score derived from ledger history (cc-core.lua sessionRisk) — it has NO concept of "THIS specific pending action is high-risk," whereas OpenHands attaches security_risk to the action itself. Surfacing a red banner above Approve/Deny when the pending request is flagged high-risk is exactly the kind of decision-point transparency that reduces rubber-stamping, and it fits cleanly: cc-status.sh already composes the pending summary, and L2 (named policy bundles with a per-rule flag/observe tier) is the natural place to mark a request HIGH.
- **Against:** Four of the five sub-features are already shipped, so a straight ADD would be redundant. Inline Confirm/Reject under the pending action = Shepherd's #b-approve / #b-deny buttons in the detail panel, shown with the "Wants: <summary>" pending block (claude-dashboard.lua:3064-3065, 4346). The double-submit guard is already present and stronger than OpenHands' "tracked action IDs": Shepherd publishes a per-request nonce in the pending block that the panel echoes back as "allow <nonce>", so the gate consumes ONLY the matching request and a stale/double click cannot answer the next gated call (cc-core.lua:1109-1118, cc-approve.sh:216-226). The "lock badge for confirmation mode" maps to Shepherd's existing armed-gate / "hands-free approve" / Headless state. The remaining additive idea — per-action risk — leans on OpenHands' model where the EXECUTOR computes security_risk while running the action; Shepherd never runs the agent, so it can only heuristically flag a request (pattern/tool match), not get a real model-derived risk grade. That makes the banner a softer, lower-fidelity signal than the source, and risks implying a guarantee Shepherd can't back.
- **Ruling:** No double-mismatch trip: this is pure watch/steer, no executor, sandbox, server, users, or secrets. But it is mostly redundant with shipped functionality — the inline approve/deny control, the pending-summary surface, the double-submit guard (nonce, stronger than action-ID tracking), and the confirmation-mode/lock indicator (armed gate / Headless toggle) all exist. So ADD is wrong; the underlying NEED (clear, fast, transparent decision point) is already met for its core. Two thin deltas remain real: (a) per-pending-action HIGH-risk banner, and (b) in-panel keyboard shortcuts for approve/deny on the selected tile. Both are reshapes onto existing primitives rather than a new system, which is exactly ADAPT. The risk banner reinforces L2 (named policy/guardrail bundles already plan a per-rule flag/observe tier that rides the ledger + risk score as an indicator only) — that tier is precisely where a "this request is HIGH" flag belongs, then the panel hangs a red banner on the already-present pending block. Confidence is high because the overlap is verified directly in the source. Effort is S: add a `risk` field to the pending block (cc-status.sh / cc-approve.sh), render a banner in renderDetail's pending section, and bind two keys in the existing document keydown handler scoped to a selected tile with a pending gate.
- **How (Shepherd):** Keep the shipped Approve/Deny buttons + nonce double-submit guard + armed-gate "hands-free approve" indicator as-is (they already cover Confirm/Reject, the double-submit guard, and the lock badge). Add only the two deltas: (1) HIGH-risk banner — extend the pending block written by cc-status.sh / cc-approve.sh with an optional `risk` (low/med/high) field; populate `high` from an L2 per-rule flag tier (or a pattern/tool heuristic); in renderDetail's pending branch (claude-dashboard.lua ~4346) render a red banner above #b-approve/#b-deny when risk=="high". (2) Keyboard shortcuts — in the existing document keydown handler (claude-dashboard.lua:4836), when a tile is selected and gate=="waiting": Cmd+Enter -> act('approve'), Shift+Cmd+Backspace -> act('deny'), reusing the nonce'd path so the guard already applies. Document under L2's transparency work; surface in the hotkey legend (core.hotkeyLegend).
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute the ADAPT and could not. No double-mismatch: approve/deny only writes a decision file that cc-approve.sh polls (lines 216-277) — no executor, no sandbox, no server, no users, no secrets. So it is pure watch/steer, the heart of what Shepherd is for. DONT_ADD is unsupportable because two real deltas survive verification, but ADD is equally wrong because the core is already shipped (and stronger): - Confirm/Reject: #b-approve / #b-deny already exist (claude-dashboard.lua:3064-3065, act('approve')/act('deny')). - Double-submit guard: the per-request NONCE in cc-approve.sh (NONCE="$$.$NOW", lines 222-277) binds a decision to the exact displayed request via a PID-owned CLAIM mv + nonce match, explicitly so a double-clicked Approve cannot answer a request that was never shown. This strictly subsumes OpenHands' client-side action-ID tracking — the judge is right it is stronger. - Lock / confirmation-mode badge: the armed-gate "(hands-free approve)" indicator (claude-dashboard.lua:4347) plus the Headless toggle already cover the lock badge. The two surviving deltas are genuine but thin reshapes onto existing primitives, which is exactly ADAPT: (a) Per-pending-action HIGH-risk banner. Shepherd's M.sessionRisk (cc-core.lua:1371) is an empirical PER-SESSION indicator derived from ledger history (deny rate, auto-deny hits, timeouts) — it does NOT classify "this specific pending request is HIGH." So a per-action HIGH flag is a new signal. It maps cleanly onto the already-QUEUED L2 flag/observe tier, which todos.md:320 says explicitly 'rides the existing ledger + risk score as an indicator only' — the correct home for a per-rule 'this request is HIGH' flag, then the panel hangs a red banner above #b-approve/#b-deny in renderDetail's pending branch (~4346). 'Reinforces L2' is verified correct. (b) In-panel keyboard shortcuts for approve/deny on the SELECTED tile. Verified there is no panel keydown handler for this: the only document keydown (claude-dashboard.lua:4836) handles just Escape, and the existing global Cmd+Alt+A (line 5758) acts on the FRONT-MOST approval via a window keystroke, not the selected tile through the nonce'd decision path. So scoped Cmd+Enter -> act('approve') / Shift+Cmd+Backspace -> act('deny') on the selected waiting tile (reusing the nonce path so the guard already applies) is genuinely new. Small, but real. Both deltas are small (effort S), reuse shipped primitives (pending block, keydown handler, nonce decision path, L2 observe tier), and add no new system. That is ADAPT, not ADD and not DONT_ADD. The ruling holds.

#### Per-user/per-action rate limiting (Redis-backed, 429 + retry-after)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L2
- **For:** The abstract primitive — "bound how often an action may fire per time window, and refuse/defer when exceeded" — is real and useful in a fleet console. A runaway session that hammers Bash/Edit, or auto-continue/auto-respawn loops firing too frequently, are exactly the kinds of bursts an operator would want capped. Shepherd already has the materials: L2 has queued a per-session usage limit (toolLimits = {Bash=5}) enforced as a soft autoDeny by='usage-limit' counted from the ledger, and riskScore already tracks toolRequests and lastReqTs (cc-core.lua:1393-1394). OpenHands' one transferable idea over the queued count-based cap is the SLIDING-WINDOW shape (RATE_LIMIT_*_USER_SECONDS = N events per rolling window) versus an absolute lifetime count. That window variant could be folded into the L2 bundle as a soft, off-by-default, per-session rate cap (autoDeny by='rate-limit' + a "X/N per Ws" tile badge), or as a min-interval debounce on Shepherd's own automations (auto-continue/auto-respawn). Pure cc-core math over ledger timestamps; no new infrastructure.
- **Against:** As shipped, the feature is the (b) half of the OpenHands double mismatch in its purest form: it is multi-user web-server infrastructure. It is keyed by user_id (Shepherd has no users — it is single-user, single-operator), it guards HTTP routes and emits HTTP 429 + retry-after (Shepherd serves no HTTP and makes no API calls, so there is no request to reject and no status code to return), it protects SaaS actions like org invitations (no analog in a local console), and it depends on Redis as an external shared store (Shepherd is filesystem-only, no network server, no daemon). None of create_redis_rate_limiter / setup_rate_limit_handler / RateLimitException / check_rate_limit_by_user_id has a home: the user key, the HTTP handler, and the Redis backend are all things Shepherd structurally cannot and should not host. Porting it as-is would be inventing a server to rate-limit. Moreover Shepherd does not run the agent loop, so it cannot intercept the agent's API call rate at all — Claude Code owns true enforcement; anything Shepherd does is necessarily soft/advisory.
- **Ruling:** The literal feature is DONT_ADD-shaped: Redis + user keys + HTTP 429 + retry-after is the multi-tenant server that Shepherd is explicitly NOT (no server, no users, no API calls, no external store). But the verdict is ADAPT, not DONT_ADD, because a genuine, reshapable need survives the strip-down: a per-time-window cap on how often an action fires. That need is already 90% covered by the L2 per-session usage limit (toolLimits, soft autoDeny by='usage-limit', counted from the ledger). The only net-new idea worth keeping is the SLIDING-WINDOW formulation (N per rolling Ws, the *_USER_SECONDS pattern) as opposed to an absolute count — a small variant field on the same L2 bundle, plus optionally a min-interval debounce on Shepherd's own off-by-default automations (auto-continue/auto-respawn) so they cannot thrash. So it reinforces L2 rather than spawning a parallel rate-limit system. Enforcement is soft (next-request autoDeny / automation defer), never a 429, and stays off by default to respect the scope limits. Confidence is high: the server/Redis/user/HTTP plumbing is unambiguously out of scope, and the surviving window-cap idea maps cleanly onto an existing queued primitive.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend L2's per-tool usage limit with a sliding-window variant: alongside the queued toolLimits = {Bash=5} (absolute count), add a windowed cap, e.g. rateLimits = {Bash={n=10, windowSec=60}}, on the same named/attachable policy bundle. Enforce purely in cc-core: tally the session's tool_request events from the ledger (reuse the toolRequests / lastReqTs machinery in riskScore around cc-core.lua:1393-1394), and when the count within the trailing window meets/exceeds n, return autoDeny with by='rate-limit' + a deny-reason; the side-effect goes through fx.writeDecision and cc-approve.sh's emit_deny, exactly like the existing usage-limit path. Tile shows an "X/N per Ws" badge; per-session reset. Secondarily, apply the same min-interval idea as a debounce on Shepherd's own automations (auto-continue, auto-respawn) so a bounded loop cannot fire faster than a configured floor. All off by default, attached only via a bundle — never default_on (per the explicit scope note in todos.md:329). DROP entirely: Redis, the user_id key axis, the HTTP 429 + retry-after handler, RateLimitException, and any notion of org-invitation/SaaS endpoint protection — Shepherd has no server, users, or API calls, and enforcement is soft because Claude Code owns the real loop.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; the ADAPT (reinforces L2) ruling holds. I verified every load-bearing fact in the source. The LITERAL feature is correctly stripped: Redis, the user_id key axis, HTTP 429 + retry-after, and RateLimitException are the multi-tenant server Shepherd explicitly is NOT (context.md scope: no network server, no users, no secrets, no API calls) — the judge already DROPS all of it. The strongest refutation angle is (c) redundancy: L2 ALREADY queues a per-tool usage cap. Confirmed in todos.md:343-346 — bundle field `toolLimits = {Bash=5}`, "pure cc-core counts a session's invocations from the ledger (reuse the tool_request tally in riskScore ~1393); at/over ceiling -> autoDeny by='usage-limit' + reason; X/N tile badge; per-session reset. Soft (next-request) limit ... Off by default; attach via bundle, never default_on." The ledger tally it reuses is real (cc-core.lua:1393-1399: tool_request increments toolRequests and stamps lastReqTs).
