The soft-enforcement plumbing is real (cc-approve.sh:40-43 emit_deny writes permissionDecisionReason; line 155 the auto-deny path). The off-by-default constraint is explicit (todos.md:329 'Keep ALL opt-in — do NOT port default_on'). The auto-continue/auto-respawn debounce targets are real (cc-core.lua:786, 2134, 2209). So nothing here needs an executor/sandbox (error (a)) or a server/auth/secrets (error (b)). The only survivor over the already-queued absolute toolLimits is the SLIDING-WINDOW shape (N per trailing Ws) — a genuinely distinct enforcement (bounds rate, not lifetime total) plus a min-interval debounce floor on Shepherd's own automations. That is a real, in-scope refinement, so it cannot be pushed to DONT_ADD — but it is a small variant field on the SAME L2 bundle, SAME ledger tally, SAME autoDeny/emit_deny path, SAME tile badge, so it reinforces an existing queued primitive rather than warranting ADD. That is precisely ADAPT.

---

## ❌ DON'T-ADD — 38 items (executor / sandbox / multi-user-server / secrets)

### Observability & Status (7)
- **Live status model: dual agent-execution + host/sandbox status merged onto persisted info, normalized state enum** — Decomposed, the candidate is two enums merged: a host/sandbox lifecycle enum (STARTING/RUNNING/PAUSED/ERROR/MISSING, normalized from Docker states) and an agent-execution enum (fetched live from a running sandbox's API). Shepherd has neither a sandbox, an agent-execution loop, nor a server, so the literal feature — its actual code value, the batched sandbox queries, the /api/conversations overlay, the _docker_status_to_sandbox_status mapping, the conversation_url — cannot exist here. That is the clean double-mismatch DONT_ADD. Critically, the only genuinely transferable idea (a normalized session-status enum + live-status-overlaid-on-persisted-identity, with a possibly-deleted marker) is NOT a gap: Shepherd already ships it. parseStatusList persists/tags/sorts; the refresh loop overlays the derived error status and recomputes staleness; mergeStatusLists unions sources; shouldPrune/staleDuplicateKeys handle the MISSING/possibly-deleted concept with a deliberately more conservative policy than a flat flag (it refuses to treat resting sessions as dead). So the feature is simultaneously (a) impossible in its load-bearing parts and (b) redundant in its transferable parts — both independent grounds for DONT_ADD. The one sliver worth noting (a first-class MISSING tile badge) is too thin and too entangled with the existing stale/prune invariants to justify reshaping; touching it risks the carefully-reasoned "staleness is not death" invariant for marginal UX gain. It matches no queued L1-L4 item (those are agent profiles / policy bundles / templates / routing), so there is nothing to reinforce.
- **Per-conversation append-only event store (one JSON per event, replayable log)** — The underlying capability — a durable, replayable, per-conversation event log with retention and export — is real, but Shepherd already ships it as the audit/event ledger (append-only JSONL, filter/redact/purge/export, narrative, timeline, risk, lineage). The candidate is therefore redundant with existing functionality. Its only distinguishing features are all blocked by the OpenHands double mismatch: per-user prefixing (no multi-user), S3/GCS pluggable backends (no network/server, local-only by hard scope), webhook ingestion from a sandboxed agent-server (no listener, no sandbox, no executor), and event types that capture the agent's own actions/observations (no in-process agent loop to observe). The one-file-per-event storage shape is additionally an anti-pattern on a local single-user box versus the ledger's daily-JSONL O_APPEND design. There is no clean reshape that adds anything to the ledger except mechanics Shepherd cannot host, so this is DONT_ADD on both grounds (redundant + structurally blocked), not ADAPT. If anything in the candidate were to be cherry-picked it would be incremental event-type richness on the existing ledger, which is already covered by the queued L-items stamping by:"router"/decision events — not a parallel store.
- **Event search / filter / count API (by kind, timestamp range, sort, paginate; fast file-count path)** — This is the rare candidate that clears the double mismatch (pure management/observability — no executor, sandbox, server, users, or secrets) yet still fails on the redundancy clause. Shepherd's audit/event ledger is the structural twin of OpenHands' event store, and the slicing primitives already implemented (filterLedger by types + sinceTs/untilTs + newest-first sort, capLedgerSlice with a truncated flag and full-export bypass, fleetStats/fleetStandup for kind-counted badges) cover every load-bearing part of the candidate. The only deltas are cursor pagination (not a real need for a single-user in-memory window capped at 2000) and the no-filter file-count fast path (a server-scale optimization that doesn't map to Shepherd's daily-file layout and is already obviated by cached aggregate counters). Adding a separate search/count API would re-implement what filterLedger/capLedgerSlice/fleetStats already do and create a drift risk. Verdict DONT_ADD on grounds of redundancy with shipped functionality, not on the double mismatch.
- **Attention-state notification (status pill + tab-flash + sound, fire-on-transition-when-unfocused)** — This is the redundancy branch of DONT_ADD, not the double-mismatch branch — and that distinction matters for the rule. The feature needs no executor, sandbox, server, or secrets; the PATTERN transfers cleanly. But Shepherd has already independently arrived at the same design and shipped it: pulsing status dot (per-status color + pulse), fire-once-per-episode transition alerting, off-by-default sound, out-of-band push, and a ledger-backed notification history. Importing the OpenHands feature as a feature would duplicate all of that. The browser-tab-title-flash specifically is a browser-tab affordance with no analog in a native macOS webview, and its intent is already served by ntfy push + hs.alert. The single piece with real marginal value is the unfocused-gate (suppress the ding when the panel is frontmost), and Shepherd already owns the exact primitive needed (the webview focusChange signal). That is a small follow-up tweak to the existing escalation channel, not grounds for an ADD/ADAPT verdict on a new feature. Calling this ADAPT would imply a reshaping effort that doesn't exist — the reshaping is already done and shipped.
- **Per-conversation cost / token-usage accounting + metrics visualization (budget bar, context-window gauge, token breakdown)** — The candidate decomposes into (1) a metrics-visualization layer Shepherd already ships (token breakdown, per-model split, context-window gauge, inline budget/usage bars, fleet+plan-window rollups, all local and zero-cost), and (2) one new capability — accumulated USD cost vs a max-budget ceiling. Part (1) is redundant: re-adding it violates the do-not-re-add rule. Part (2) is the only delta worth weighing, and it fails on two independent grounds. First, the double mismatch: the candidate's cost value originates from the in-process executor that makes the priced API calls and is delivered through the agent-server webhook (process_stats_event into a SQL row) — Shepherd has no executor, makes no API calls, and has no pricing source, so it could at best recompute an estimate from a pricing table. Second, that estimate is not just unavailable but actively misleading for Shepherd's reality: the user is on a flat-rate Anthropic subscription (the real meter is the OAuth plan-window %, which Shepherd already surfaces) and gateway/local model pricing is genuinely unknown — which is precisely why README:329 documents tokens-not-dollars as an intentional scope choice. The reasoning-token bucket is a trivial one-line extension to parseUsageLine, not a feature justifying ADD/ADAPT. Net: the valuable parts exist; the new part is structurally unsupportable and explicitly out of scope.
- **Health / readiness / liveness probes (server orchestration)** — Double mismatch (b): the feature exists to serve load balancers / Kubernetes against a multi-user web server — Shepherd is a local single-user macOS webview with no network server, no traffic, no orchestrator, so the endpoints have no consumer and no subject. There is no executor/sandbox angle here; it is purely server-orchestration plumbing, which is DONT_ADD by definition. The abstract "is it healthy?" need is real but is already covered, and at higher fidelity than the static-string OpenHands stub: STALE_SECONDS/parseStatusList (liveness via status-file freshness), watchdogShouldReset + transcriptError (readiness/frozen detection), the Error state, per-session risk score, and fleet insights/shift report (fleet rollup). So it is simultaneously unhostable in its native form AND redundant in its transferable form — both independent grounds for DONT_ADD. It does not advance the user's saved-agents/skills/MCP priority at all.
- **Consent-gated product analytics + typed lifecycle event catalog + anonymous install id (PostHog)** — Splitting the feature: (1) typed, consent-gated, off-by-default lifecycle event catalog — already SHIPPED as Shepherd's audit ledger (cc-core.lua ledgerFor/appendLedger, NARRATE catalog at line 642, ledger.enabled default false, captureTypes filter, retention GC, redact/export/purge, insights/shift-report). There is nothing to add here; it is redundant. (2) PostHog egress + persisted cross-install distinct_id — this is the only novel delta, and it is precisely what Shepherd structurally cannot host: it requires an outbound network call (no network server) and a stored vendor API key (no secrets), and a distinct_id for vendor-side attribution is meaningless for a single-user local tool with no backend to send to. Unlike a typical ADAPT, there is no residual unmet need: the legitimate need ('a centralized place to emit named lifecycle events under consent') is fully met by the ledger, and the remaining piece is the forbidden phone-home. This is therefore a clean DONT_ADD on BOTH counts — redundant with shipped functionality AND blocked by the server/secrets prong of the double mismatch. The one transferable nugget (write a documented EVENTS.md-style schema doc for the existing ledger event types) is a doc chore on an existing system, not a feature to copy, so it does not lift this to ADAPT.

### Settings & Profiles (6)
- **Manual title + metadata update (partial PATCH with validation discipline)** — The candidate decomposes into a rename feature and a partial-PATCH/deep-merge discipline, and Shepherd already has working analogs of both. core.setLabel/setGroup deliver immutable, consistency-checked, projectKey-stable tile renaming — including the same "reset-to-default clears the entry" rule. core.overlayConfig is precisely a save-one-page-don't-clobber-another partial overlay with a keep-list (the model_fields_set / deep_merge intent), and it consciously avoids generic deep-merge for the same array-replace reason. The "sensitive fields via guarded routes only" goal is satisfied structurally (no secrets storage is a hard scope limit) and concretely (risk.weights hand-edit-only). Shell-injection rejection exists as cc_sanitize. Everything genuinely NEW in the candidate — editable per-conversation repo/branch/git_provider metadata, a public flag, and propagating title to an agent-server — depends on the multi-tenant conversation/server model that Shepherd is explicitly not. The lone marginal gap (overlayConfig does not validate incoming values at the save boundary) is a one-line hardening, not a feature, and does not justify importing this. Verdict DONT_ADD on the double mismatch plus redundancy.
- **Concurrency-safe config writes (per-user lock around load-modify-store)** — This is a textbook DOUBLE MISMATCH (b)-side artifact: the feature's value is entirely a consequence of being a multi-worker async web server, which Shepherd structurally is not. The underlying NEED — don't lose an update during read-modify-write — splits two ways and both are already resolved. (1) For IN-PROCESS mutations, Lua's single cooperative thread makes every load->mutate->store handler atomic; there is no second execution context to race, so a mutex guards nothing. (2) For the only genuine race, which is CROSS-PROCESS (dashboard vs. shell hooks/cc-approve), an in-process lock is the wrong tool entirely (it can't coordinate separate OS processes), and Shepherd already uses the correct primitive — atomic temp+rename and O_APPEND — with explicit code comments showing the author understood and defended against exactly this race. So the feature is simultaneously meaningless (no executor/server/threads to serialize) and redundant (the real race is already handled by a better mechanism). Per the rubric, 'redundant with what Shepherd already has' plus 'needs the server' is a clean DONT_ADD. The L1 Agent Profiles work inherits the same single-thread + atomic-write guarantees for free, so it raises no new concurrency requirement.
- **Model & provider discovery / verified-model catalogue (curated allow-list, paginated search)** — DOUBLE MISMATCH applied: the discovery ENGINE (live Bedrock/Ollama/litellm enumeration) is the (a)/server mismatch, and the enterprise VerifiedModel admin CRUD is the (b) multi-tenant mismatch — both DONT_ADD on their own. But the management/settings PATTERN underneath survives: a curated, local, off-the-wire allow-list of known model ids (+ canonical provider prefix + a recommended default) that feeds the model picker. That is a config artifact, not a service — exactly Shepherd's providers registry shape, and a natural field-set on L1 agent profiles (which already store provider/model and have a config-by-reference resolver). So ADAPT, reinforcing L1. Strip network discovery, Bedrock/Ollama probing, pagination (single-user scale makes it pointless), and the enterprise DB/auth/roles. Keep only: a small static catalogue/allow-list + a substring filter in the picker + a recommended-default hint + bare-name to provider-prefix normalization for display/validation. This is a thin enrichment of an already-queued item, not a standalone build, which is why it is ADAPT-into-L1 rather than ADD. The against side (Claude Code already owns the real list; catalogue drift) keeps scope minimal — a convenience overlay on top of configured providers, never an authority.
- **Operator feature-flags & server config (hide LLM settings, billing, store/auth class selection)** — This is the cleanest DONT_ADD in the set: the feature is 100% the multi-user web-server product layer, with zero residue that survives the double mismatch. Walking the four named toggles plus the two store classes plus the SaaS override, each one maps to a forbidden capability: HIDE_LLM_SETTINGS → operator-vs-enduser distinction (no such split — single user); ENABLE_BILLING/APP_MODE → SaaS commerce/deploy tiers (no server, no billing); user_auth_class → multi-user auth (no users); secret_store_class → stored secrets (the explicit hard "NO secrets storage" limit — Shepherd resolves $ENV via the spawned shell and stores only var NAMES); settings_store_class → server-side per-user persistence (Shepherd has one local cc-config.json). The SaaSServerConfig-overrides-OSS-via-dynamic-import pattern is the canonical "serve N tenants from one binary" mechanism — antithetical to a local single-user panel. The only thing that even looks transferable (FEATURE_FLAGS → conditionally hidden UI) is, for a single user who is also the operator, either pointless (hide your own settings) or already covered by the existing Settings UI. The pluggable-store-class idea is convergent with patterns Shepherd already arrived at independently (fx table, operator-data-outside-Settings, config-by-ref resolvers in L1/L2) — so it offers nothing to copy. No L1–L4 backlog item is reinforced: L1/L2 resolvers dereference agent/skill/policy NAMES to spawn flags, a fundamentally different concern from operator-tier UI gating. Verdict: DONT_ADD, high confidence.
- **Database SSL-mode configuration helpers** — The feature's entire value is configuring TLS for a relational database connection — and Shepherd structurally has no database, no server, no DB connection, and no secrets store. It is the (b) multi-user-web-server side of the double mismatch (persistence backend), with zero overlap onto Shepherd's watch/steer/spawn model. The superficial resemblance to Shepherd's config-validation helpers does not survive inspection: providerEnv/spawnFlags emit Claude-Code launch flags and env-var name references, not SQL driver connect-args, and there is no consumer for a normalized DB_SSL_MODE anywhere in the codebase. No primitive (spawn/presets/queue/routing/gate/ledger/providers/usage/tiles) maps to a DB connection layer, and none of the queued items (L1-L4) touch persistence backends. There is nothing to ADAPT because there is no local need: Shepherd will never open a Postgres connection. Clean, high-confidence DONT_ADD.
- **Per-user/per-org application settings cascade (feature flags, app settings, consent/TOS/onboarding)** — Map each pillar onto Shepherd's primitives and each lands outside scope. Org-defaults->member-diff cascade = multi-tenant (no users — there is no "member" to diff against an "org default"). Per-user consent/TOS/onboarding = remote-user lifecycle on a web server (no users, no server, and a local owner needs no consent gate). Env feature flags gating routes = server plumbing (no server, no routes). The only residue that survives the mismatch is the abstract idea "feature flags decide which capabilities are visible," and Shepherd ALREADY implements that as its flat, off-by-default *.enabled toggles read by core.config and surfaced in the Settings tab. Adding a hierarchical/SaaS-style cascade on top would be structure without a second tier to populate — redundant with the existing single-tier toggles. The legitimately-transferable seam (a base config overlaid by a more specific one) is not this feature; it is already covered by overlayConfig today (cc-core.lua:2491-2510) and will be extended naturally by L1 (agent-profile fields overriding fleet defaults) and L2 (named bundles attached over the base gate). So this candidate adds nothing those don't, and its distinctive parts (orgs, users, consent, server routes) are exactly what Shepherd structurally cannot host. DONT_ADD rather than ADAPT-reinforces-L1, because L1's cascade is a per-profile/per-spawn override (single-user, local) whereas this candidate's whole value is the multi-tenant + remote-consent layer that L1 does not and should not absorb.

### Secrets & Auth (6)
- **User-scoped event isolation via storage path prefix** — The candidate's value is 100% multi-tenant access control: making one user's events unreadable by another user via an unformable path. That depends on auth (user_id from user_context), multiple coexisting users, and shared multi-tenant storage — all three of which Shepherd structurally lacks and its scope explicitly forbids (single-user, no server, no auth). The pattern (isolation-by-unformable-path) is elegant but has no target in Shepherd: there is exactly one human, so there is nothing to isolate them from. The nearest Shepherd construct, SSH host namespacing, is directory organization, not a security boundary, and Shepherd already has it — so even the conceptual echo is redundant rather than additive. This is not a management/skills/UI pattern reshapeable onto a session/tile/ledger primitive; its worth is entirely the user-auth boundary. Clean DONT_ADD on the (b) half of the double mismatch.
- **Session-key lifecycle: rotate-on-resume / invalidate-on-pause / RUNNING-only validity** — This is a textbook double mismatch. The feature presupposes a server-side STORED credential (OpenHands persists session_api_key_hash and rotates the real key against runtime-api) AND a server-owned SANDBOX with RUNNING/PAUSED/STOPPED states it authorizes against (validate_session_key 401s unless RUNNING). Shepherd has neither half. I verified the secret model directly: cc-core.lua providerEnv (lines 2710-2735) and envPrefix (2738+) emit credentials as `$VAR` shell references that the SPAWNED SHELL expands at launch — the secret value never enters Shepherd's process, files, or memory, and the comments explicitly call out that plain execution expands `$MY_KEY` to "". There is no stored key to rotate, no hash to update, no credential to invalidate on pause. Equally, Shepherd makes no API calls and authenticates nothing, so the RUNNING-only-validity check has zero analog. A Shepherd "pause" is a human switching windows or draining a GUI session that Claude Code owns end-to-end; Shepherd cannot mint a credential on resume because it never held one. The only Shepherd primitive that is key-like — the nonce-bound gate (and its SSH-routed variant) — already enforces a tighter validity window than this feature describes (single-use, atomic PID-owned claim, mtime-fenced, consumed per request), so there is nothing to import; doing so would re-solve a closed problem. The user's stated priority (saved agents/skills/MCP, declarative defs) does not touch credential lifecycle, so it pulls no weight here.
- **Git provider token storage with live token validation + auto-detection** — The candidate's substance is token PERSISTENCE + LIVE-PROBE validation/auto-detection + OAuth REFRESH-and-replay, all keyed per user. That is the textbook OpenHands double mismatch hitting at once: (b) a multi-user server with a per-user secrets store, and (a) executor/proxy behavior (probing provider endpoints, refreshing and replaying calls). Shepherd has no server, no users, no stored secrets, no HTTP client, and runs no agent loop — so none of the four pillars has a home. I checked whether any reshaped core survives: the only Shepherd-compatible residue is "a profile can name a git-token env var, NAME only, expanded by the login shell," but that capability is already covered by the L1 design (authTokenEnv on MCP records, requiredEnv + core.missingEnv NAME-presence checks, cc-core.lua:2728). So even after maximal adaptation there is nothing NEW to add — the genuinely novel parts (validate-by-probing, type/host mismatch 401, transparent refresh) are precisely the parts Shepherd cannot do. This isn't a reshape candidate; it's already been explicitly declined in todos.md (DROP credentialed git/SaaS clients; DROP stored auth values). High confidence: the repo's own design docs name this exact feature class as out of scope. Effort framing is moot since the verdict is DONT_ADD, but if forced the validate/probe/refresh substance is the whole feature and is unbuildable here.
- **Single-user / no-tenancy default auth (user_id always None / 'root')** — The feature's entire reason to exist is to be the degenerate case of OpenHands' multi-user auth abstraction. Its "value" — confirming "no users" is a first-class config — is something Shepherd already asserts as a HARD SCOPE LIMIT (no multi-user, no secrets storage), not as a pluggable auth mode. There is nothing to build: Shepherd has no UserAuth interface to satisfy, no identity-keyed settings/secrets store, no server request context to extract a user from. The only thing transferable would be a vacuous "currentUser = local operator" constant, which buys no new watch/steer/spawn behavior and would merely seed the appearance of an auth layer the project explicitly rejects. This depends squarely on multi-user auth + stored secrets — both named DONT_ADD triggers in the double mismatch. It does not reinforce L1-L4: those are about agent profiles, policy bundles, templates, and routing, none of which need an identity object. Verdict: DONT_ADD, high confidence.
- **Multi-user identity & auth stack (UserAuth provider, JWT/JWE, key rotation, OAuth/SSO, device flow, API keys)** — Every sub-feature maps to "authenticate remote users" or "store/rotate their secrets" — the two things Shepherd's architecture forbids by design, not omission. There is no Shepherd primitive this reshapes onto: a "conversation" is a local GUI session it watches, not an authenticated remote session; the headless approval gate approves an ACTION on a local session, not a login. The closest existing surface (providers / L1 authTokenEnv) was built specifically to AVOID holding secrets — it holds NAMES, shell-expanded at spawn — so this candidate would regress that invariant, not extend it. No ADAPT path exists because the underlying NEED (authenticate users, hold keys) simply does not exist for a single-user local tool driven by the human at the keyboard. It is not even an L2 extension, since the gate governs tool actions, not identity. DONT_ADD, high confidence.
- **Stripe billing / credits / subscriptions + BYOR managed-LLM key issuance** — This hits BOTH halves of the double mismatch at once: (a) it is downstream of OpenHands being an LLM-proxying executor that pays for calls — Shepherd runs no agent loop and proxies nothing, so there is no spend to bill; (b) it is multi-user/multi-org SaaS billing tied to auth — Shepherd is single-user with no server, no users, no orgs. Worse, BYOR violates the hard NO-secrets invariant directly: it persists encrypted live credentials and refreshes them, which is precisely the anti-pattern Shepherd codifies against (it stores only the env-var name, e.g. authTokenEnv, and lets the login shell expand $ENV). It does not reinforce any queued item — none of L1-L4 concern money or managed keys, and L1 Agent Profiles deliberately stores requiredEnv[]/authTokenEnv NAMES, so BYOR would actively corrupt L1's secrets posture rather than extend it. The only honest adjacent need (per-session/provider spend visibility) is already substantially shipped via token/context usage tracking, the plan-window usage poll, and per-folder respawn budgets, so there is nothing left to ADAPT.

### Sandbox & Runtime (5)
- **Delete conversation with cascade + shared-resource reference-count cleanup** — This is a textbook hit on BOTH halves of the OpenHands double mismatch. (a) The reference-counted "shared resource" is the executor's SANDBOX, and the conditional skip-agent-server-delete exists to avoid destabilizing sibling AGENT LOOPS — both are the in-process executor/sandbox layer Shepherd structurally lacks. (b) The cascade operates on a multi-tenant conversations DB. Shepherd's closest primitive (action "close" → closeWindow + removeStatus) already covers "end a session and drop its tile," and it is deliberately trivial precisely because Shepherd doesn't own the process or any backing resource: the editor/OS owns lifetime, the status file is the only artifact, and there is no inter-session shared resource to count. Lineage exists but is a read-only ledger rollup (lineageByProject), explicitly NOT a persisted parent/child chain (cc-core comment: "deliberately NO new persisted lineage chain"), so there's no tree to cascade down either. Implementing cascade+refcount would require first inventing a sandbox-sharing model and a conversation tree — manufacturing the very plumbing Shepherd was scoped to avoid — to gain zero new operator capability beyond the existing close. It also doesn't touch the user's stated priority (saved agents/skills/MCP defs). DONT_ADD with high confidence.
- **Sandbox/runtime spec presets — named immutable spawn templates (image/command/env/working_dir)** — The double mismatch knocks out image (sandbox) and command (executor) outright, and the literal initial_env dict violates the no-secrets limit. What survives is only the abstract pattern: a named immutable launch bundle you spawn from, with a factory of curated defaults. That pattern is not new to Shepherd — it ships as spawn presets and is already queued for its full superset as L1. So this is ADAPT/reinforces-L1, not ADD (the object can't be copied as-is) and not DONT_ADD (the underlying need is real and Shepherd-native). The ONE incremental idea worth folding into L1 is get_default_sandbox_specs()'s factory-of-defaults: ship 2-3 curated starter agent profiles (and the existing presets) seeded out of the box, so the registry isn't empty on first run — which directly advances the user's stated priority of pre-configured agents/skills/MCPs in a directory. initial_env must reshape onto Shepherd's name-an-env-var provider model + L1's requiredEnv[], never a stored value dict. working_dir is already the folder field. image/command are dropped.
- **Pluggable launch-target backend selection by environment (docker / process / remote)** — Two reasons converge on DONT_ADD. (1) Redundancy: the transferable, server/sandbox-free kernel — pick a launch target by environment, target dictates command/env — is already fully implemented in `spawnSpec`'s editor dispatch (kitty/vscode/cursor/terminal) + the `spawn.editor` startup default + per-session `editor` recall + ssh as the remote modifier. There is no missing capability to build. (2) Double mismatch: OpenHands' three concrete backends (docker image, in-process agent_server, remote sandbox REST) are about provisioning and running the agent EXECUTOR inside a SANDBOX — the (a) executor mismatch — and the remote one is a multi-tenant sandbox server, brushing (b). Shepherd runs no loop and no sandbox, so those backends have no referent. The only sliver beyond what ships is "a saved profile carries its launch target," and that already belongs to L1's profile shape (the profile is a superset of the spawn preset, which already includes `editor`), so it reinforces L1 rather than justifying a new injector system. No new build is warranted.
- **Uniform sandbox/session lifecycle verbs (start/resume/pause/delete) with existence booleans** — A Sandbox & Runtime feature whose worth depends on the sandbox half AND the server half of the double mismatch. (1) The verbs ARE container ops (run/pause/unpause/stop+remove) and the deliverable is a REST router with HTTP 404 semantics — Shepherd has neither a container nor a server. (2) Two of the four verbs (pause/resume) have no coherent meaning for a Claude Code GUI session: there is no process to suspend and revive; Shepherd watches a window and reads hook-written status, it does not control the process. (3) The status enum is not Shepherd's to own — statuses are derived from Claude Code hooks (cc-status.sh), not set by a lifecycle command. (4) The one transferable idea — a uniform lifecycle vocabulary with a consistent existence/status return contract — is largely ALREADY PRESENT: cc-core.lua M.act() already dispatches explicit verbs (spawn via spawnSpec, close = closeWindow+removeStatus, stop, continue, plus graceful drain via shouldDrainClose and bounded auto-respawn via respawnSpec), and the existence/404 analogue already exists as sessionFree/hasSession + the "a vanished tile drops out" refresh logic keyed by projectKey. So there is no net-new need: the part that fits is built, and the part that isn't built (container start/pause/resume/delete + REST) is structurally impossible. A minor naming/return-shape cleanup of act()'s verbs is conceivable but is internal refactoring, not a feature to import. DONT_ADD with high confidence.
- **Capacity guard: auto-evict/pause oldest sessions over a max-concurrency limit** — The feature is auto-pause-oldest-sandbox-to-stay-under-a-resource-cap. Its worth depends entirely on owning sandboxes and pausing them (the sandbox primitive) to ration server compute across a multi-tenant cap — both halves Shepherd structurally lacks. Shepherd has no pause/resume of a suspended runtime (cc-core has stop/drain/respawn, all operator-armed and bounded; sessionFree/routePick reason about busy-vs-free but never suspend), and no compute budget to protect since the human owns the Mac and the real cost ceiling (plan window) is already tracked. The auto-evict-oldest behavior is also actively anti-pattern: silently closing the operator's oldest live session on every spawn contradicts the off-by-default, human-in-the-loop, never-destroy-a-running-session ethos. The only transferable residual — a soft "N sessions running" advisory — is already covered by fleet insights and requires none of this machinery, so there is nothing worth adapting. Not a match for L1/L2/L3/L4: L2 guardrails are per-session tool-policy bundles, not a global fleet-size killer.

### Conversations & Sessions (4)
- **Conversation/session search + count with filters, sort, time-range, and cursor pagination** — The feature's transferable kernel (filter sessions/events by substring + created/updated time-range + kind, plus a count for badges) is already shipped across filterTiles, filterLedger, searchArgv/parseSearchResults, capLedgerSlice, and the existing tally functions (unseenNotificationCount/fleetStats/fleetStandup). What remains net-new is precisely the server/DB/sandbox layer: opaque next_page_id cursor pagination capped at <=100/page, the stored-info-DB-merged-with-live-status service, sort-enum REST endpoints, a /count HTTP endpoint, and a sandbox_id filter. Those are the double mismatch — no server, no DB, no opaque cursor store, no sandbox, single-user in-memory fleet. The only sliver not already present (a calendar-style created/updated date-range picker and explicit tile sort orders) is a cosmetic extension of filters that already exist, not enough to justify importing the feature; if wanted it is a trivial widening of filterTiles/the header, not a new search/pagination subsystem. Net: redundant with shipped capability plus sandbox/server-bound remainder -> DONT_ADD.
- **Batch get conversations/events by id (tile hydration, tolerate holes)** — The feature's value is bound to the OpenHands server/multi-tenant context: id-list batching, UUID validation, and a hard cap only earn their keep when a remote untrusted client requests an arbitrary subset of rows over HTTP and concurrency hides per-id DB/network latency. Shepherd has no server, no remote client, no untrusted input, and no per-record latency. The legitimate underlying NEED — build the visible tiles from many records, tolerate missing ones without failing the whole view — is already fully satisfied by refreshList()/parseStatusList, which scans all local status files in one pass and drops bad/empty entries gracefully (the exact "graceful holes" semantics proposed). The ledger and SSH-mirror paths share that model. So this is both (1) gated by the double mismatch and (2) redundant with shipped behavior. The "tolerate holes" robustness idea is good but is not a new feature here — it's the existing behavior. Verdict DONT_ADD on redundancy + double-mismatch grounds; no Lx backlog item is about fetch ergonomics, so reinforces is empty.
- **Multi-conversation switcher / fleet overview panel (cards with status badge, repo/branch, model, timestamps, per-card actions, infinite scroll)** — FIT QUESTION: the panel's value (a card grid of sessions you watch/steer/navigate) is precisely Shepherd's reason to exist — and it is already built (tiles + status colors + status-priority sort + right-click overflow menu + detail panel + tile search + session groups + relabel + jump). The candidate is therefore redundant with what Shepherd already has, which the rubric lists explicitly as DONT_ADD. The one part that would be NEW is the part Shepherd cannot/should-not host: infinite-scroll pagination over a paginated server endpoint (no server, single user, tiny local set) and the sandbox_status/ARCHIVED badge (no sandbox, sessions end rather than archive) — the multi-user-server + sandbox half of the double mismatch. The genuinely missing scraps — a git-branch label on the tile and a list-view density mode — are minor polish that don't justify importing the feature and aren't worth reframing to ADAPT; they can be picked up as small standalone tweaks if desired. No queued backlog item (L1-L4) is about the fleet view itself, so there is nothing to reinforce. Net: this is already shipped, and its distinctive machinery is structurally out of scope. DONT_ADD, high confidence.
- **Read-only / archived / shared conversation replay (event stream timeline without a live runtime)** — Apply the double mismatch by splitting the candidate. The SHARED/PUBLIC half (no-auth /shared route, batch-by-id + search-without-enumeration) is the multi-user web-server prong — Shepherd has no server, no users, no public surface — so that half is DONT_ADD outright. But the ARCHIVED half maps onto something Shepherd already does for live tiles: the per-session timeline (M.sessionTimeline) IS a read-only paginated event-stream replay, and the ledger + transcript are durable on disk after the session dies. The only structural change is to stop the prune loop from making dead history unreachable: when a tile would be pruned, retain a lightweight "archived" tombstone whose only affordances are the existing read-only Timeline drill-down + transcript snippet (no input box, no nudge/approve, panes marked 'archived — read only'). That's a faithful reshape onto Shepherd's primitives, not a copy of the OpenHands plumbing — hence ADAPT, not ADD or DONT_ADD. It matches none of the queued L1-L4 (those are agent profiles / policy bundles / templates / routing — orthogonal to history replay); it instead EXTENDS the already-shipped per-session timeline + lineage, so 'reinforces' is left empty. The double-mismatch boundary is respected: nothing here runs an agent, manages a sandbox, authenticates a user, or stores a secret — it only keeps WATCHING (now: watching the past) data Shepherd already collects locally.

### Git & Integrations (4)
- **Multi-provider Git service abstraction (one interface across GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket/BBDC/Forgejo/Azure DevOps)** — Clean DONT_ADD on the secrets axis of the double mismatch. The entire payload — ProviderHandler picking a service per stored token, get_authenticated_git_url string-building oauth2:/x-token-auth:/Basic credentials, ALLOW_INSECURE_GIT_ACCESS, looping over a user's provider_tokens — presupposes (1) a stored multi-provider secrets vault keyed by user, and (2) an agent that clones/pushes inside a managed sandbox at conversation start. Shepherd has neither and is hard-scoped against both: context.md:13 ("no secrets — it reads session status off the local filesystem") and cc-core.lua:2620 ("Secrets are NEVER stored... cc-config.json holds no key and Shepherd's own process never sees one"). Shepherd's provider profiles are deliberately the inverse of a token store: they name an env var the spawned shell expands, precisely to avoid holding credentials. The clone/push URL building is also redundant — the spawned Claude Code session uses the user's own git/gh credential config in its shell; Shepherd emits spawn flags and watches status, it does not set up checkouts. The user's stated priority (saved agents/skills/MCP in a directory) gets no lift from a remote-VCS auth abstraction, and it doesn't fit the L1-L4 backlog (agent profiles, policy bundles, templates, routing — none touch VCS auth). The only transferable atom is a host→URL-shape normalizer, but that's a cosmetic enhancement to the existing GitHub-only Improve/identity logic with no user-facing demand, far below the cost of porting the abstraction, so it doesn't rescue the verdict. The closest existing surface (repoFromRemote) already host-agnostically strips to owner/repo without needing any of this.
- **Repository / branch / installation discovery (search + paginated listing)** — The value of this feature is the authenticated remote provider-API discovery layer (repo/branch/installation search across GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket/Azure with cursor pagination). That depends on stored provider secrets and a network client/router — both explicitly out of Shepherd's scope (no secrets storage, no network server, never makes API calls). It is the multi-user-auth + secrets half of the double mismatch. The legitimate underlying need — choosing a spawn target — is already fully served by Shepherd's LOCAL folder discovery (fd-backed folderScanArgv, pure fuzzyFilter, recent dirs) plus its lightweight local git awareness (FX.gitRoot, repoFromRemote). There is a tiny, non-API-dependent sliver worth noting (a local-branch picker via `git branch`/`git worktree list` against a chosen folder), but that is NOT this feature — it would carry none of the provider/installation/pagination machinery and isn't what the candidate proposes. As specified, this is DONT_ADD.
- **Proactive failing-CI conversation starter (off-by-default, deduped, double-gated opt-in)** — The candidate is the cleanest possible illustration of the OpenHands double mismatch compounded by a missing data substrate. Its value chain is: GitHub webhook (needs a server — Shepherd has none) -> find PR + dedupe bot comments + post comment (needs the credentialed, multi-tenant GitHub API + ProactiveConversationStore — Shepherd makes no API calls and stores no secrets/users) -> "@OpenHands please fix" auto-response (needs the in-process agent executor — Shepherd runs no agent loop; the agent here is a Claude Code GUI session Claude Code itself drives). Every link is a hard scope wall. Beyond the (a) executor + (b) server mismatch, there is a third independent blocker: Shepherd has no GitHub/CI integration at all (verified by grep across *.lua/*.sh/*.md — the only GitHub strings are the ripgrep/fd README links), so there is no PR/CI/head-SHA primitive to reshape the trigger onto. Critically, the underlying NEED — proactive, off-by-default, deduped, bounded response to a failure signal with a ready-to-use fix affordance — is ALREADY met by shipped features: the magenta Error tile + Continue button, Auto-Continue (per-folder budget, grace delay, anti-loop reset, ledgered), the escalation/hung watchdog, and notification history. The transferable design philosophy is something Shepherd already practices, not something to import. There is no ADAPT target: the data source (CI failure on a remote PR) is unreachable from a local, server-less, secret-less console, and where a local analog exists (session/turn failure) it is already covered. Hence DONT_ADD, not ADAPT — no Lx is reinforced because porting it would either duplicate Auto-Continue/error-tile or require building the very server/API/executor the scope forbids.
- **Slack integration — @mention bot to start/continue a conversation** — This is the clearest DONT_ADD shape in the OpenHands corpus: a single feature that trips every prong of the double mismatch simultaneously — server (six FastAPI webhook routes), multi-user (Slack→OpenHands user mapping), secrets (persisted OAuth + bot tokens), and executor/sandbox (start_job provisions the conversation). There is no PATTERN to peel off and reshape: unlike a skills library (transferable as a saved-agent registry) or an event stream (transferable as the ledger), the Slack integration's entire worth IS the remote-trigger-over-public-internet-with-stored-creds mechanism. Strip that out and nothing transferable remains. Moreover, the one legitimate, in-scope sliver of the need — "trigger/continue a session remotely and be notified" — is already covered by Claude Code's native Remote Control (which Shepherd wires up but does NOT itself implement as a server) plus the 🔔 notification history. Building any inbound trigger surface in Shepherd would mean opening a network listener and persisting a webhook secret, both explicit hard-scope violations. The user's stated priority (saved agents/skills/MCP in a directory) is not advanced by this feature in any way. No reinforces target: this does not extend L1–L4, since those are all local declarative/routing primitives, not remote ingress.

### Enterprise (2)
- **Org/role permission gating + multi-tenant MCP config storage (manage_mcp; layered org/member overrides)** — This feature is three things wearing one name. (a) The manage_mcp permission guard and utils/org/permissions.ts / authorization.py MANAGE_MCP are RBAC over multiple users — Shepherd is structurally single-user (context.md HARD SCOPE LIMIT: NO multi-user), so there is nobody to gate; the guard collapses to a no-op. (b) The per-user_settings/org/org_member mcp_config columns with org-vs-member layered resolution is a multi-tenant data model living in a FastAPI server's DB — Shepherd has NO network server and NO DB; it reads/writes flat local JSON. (c) The isSaasMode / app_mode==SAAS branch where the local Tavily key field is hidden and the search server is proxied is the secrets-storage + server-proxy mismatch: Shepherd NEVER stores keys and NEVER proxies API calls. All three load-bearing aspects hit OpenHands double-mismatch (b) head-on. The only transferable kernel — "manage MCP servers, with a fleet default that per-target settings can override" — is NOT new: it was already mined from LiteLLM/crewAI into the L1 Agent Profiles registry (cc-mcp.json named records + picker + attach + config-by-reference resolver) and the L2 named-bundle resolver (session override > attached bundle > fleet default). And the exact multi-tenant shell of this candidate is already on L1's explicit DROP list: "all Prisma tables, multi-tenant access-groups/teams/keys + assignment/propagation... stored auth values." So this candidate is redundant-with-queued for its good part and structurally-impossible for the rest. Verdict DONT_ADD, not ADAPT, because there is no residual NEED left to reshape once L1/L2 are accounted for — adapting it would just re-derive L1's already-rejected enterprise layer. Confidence high: the scope limits and the existing DROP list make this unambiguous.
- **Organization / team / RBAC / invitations (multi-tenant)** — This is the canonical OpenHands double-mismatch, hitting all four disqualifiers at once: multi-user auth (org members/roles/RBAC), a network server (invitation links, email, signin), an inbound webhook receiver (git org claims routing resolver callbacks), and per-tenant stored secrets/billing. None survives Shepherd's hard scope (single-user, no server, no secrets, reads local status files). The only transferable kernel — a higher-level named grouping that swaps default provider + policy + visible sessions — is redundant: Shepherd already ships session groups + providers and has queued L1 (Agent Profiles), L2 (named policy bundles + attachments by project/group/provider), and L3 (templates). Those queued items even explicitly DROP "multi-tenant access-groups/teams/keys" and "RBAC/admin checks," so this candidate has already been adjudicated out. I deliberately do NOT set reinforces=L1/L2, because adding an org ENTITY (with members, invitations, claims, billing) is not an extension of those items — it is the precise thing they carved out. Marking it ADAPT->reinforces would mislead by implying net-new work plugs into L1/L2; the honest call is that the real need is already met and the rest is out of scope.

### Skills & Microagents (1)
- **Skill type taxonomy + trigger classification (knowledge/keyword, repo/always-on, task/slash-command)** — The classification's discovery value (labeling skills on a card) is real but thin, and it reduces to an optional enrichment of the L1 skills enumerator rather than a feature of its own. The substantive engine of this OpenHands feature — splitting triggers by '/' prefix into Task vs Keyword triggers, treating null as repo, and having the prompt manager inject knowledge bodies on keyword match / always-merge repo bodies — is loader-and-prompt-manager machinery that only matters because OpenHands assembles the agent's context and drives the loop. Shepherd does neither: it watches ~/.claude/cc-status/*.json and steers via keystrokes/hooks; Claude Code natively decides skill activation from the description, with no trigger field present in the local format Shepherd would read (confirmed empirically). So Shepherd cannot host the injection/always-on behavior, and cannot even source the trigger taxonomy from real skills. What little transfers (badge a skill as always-on/keyword/task on the L1 card) is not worth a new system and is already covered by L1's "skills card from frontmatter." Verdict DONT_ADD: redundant with the natively-owned activation mechanism plus the OpenHands executor mismatch; any salvageable scrap folds into L1 as a non-priority cosmetic.

### Events & Callbacks (1)
- **Inbound event/conversation webhook ingestion (executor/sandbox -> app-server push, session-key auth)** — The candidate is pure server/sandbox plumbing, not a transferable management pattern. Its three jobs map onto already-shipped Shepherd mechanisms with no gap: (1) "event batches persisted to an event store" = the opt-in append-only JSONL ledger written by hooks (cc-lib.sh cc_ledger_append) and read via parseLedger/filterLedger; (2) "status/metrics/model-switch/state updates from events" = the status files (parseStatusList) consumed by the ~1s refresh loop, plus live /model + /effort switching Shepherd already exposes; (3) "tags + automation triggers" = shipped session groups/tags and the level-triggered Project Routing v1 dispatcher + bounded automations. The transport itself (authenticated inbound HTTP webhook, X-Session-API-Key, JWT secret vendor) is exactly what Shepherd's hard scope forbids — no network server, no multi-user, no stored secrets — and is replaced by the filesystem-write/pull model (local) and rsync-pull-over-SSH-keys (remote). This is not ADAPT because there is no residual need to reshape: the need is already fully satisfied, and the only thing the feature would add is a forbidden ingress path. It does not extend L1-L4 (those are agent profiles, guardrail bundles, prompt templates, and routing — none of which this push pipeline advances). Verdict DONT_ADD with high confidence.

### Web UI (1)
- **Typed event-stream chat rendering (per-event-kind expandable components, auto-scroll, suggestions, attachments)** — Apply the double mismatch. The feature is a CHAT/EVENT-STREAM RENDERER. Its distinctive components — action/observation pairs, MCP tool-call events, hook-execution events, finish/error/reject events, typing indicator, optimistic messages — are all sourced from OpenHands' (a) in-process executor event bus. That is mismatch (a): no agent loop, no streamed actions/observations. Shepherd's only window into a session's content is a status file and a transcript tail it polls ~1s; it has no per-event objects to dispatch and can never reconstruct an observation-pair or a hook-execution event, because those never touch the filesystem it reads. The human-meaningful subset (live peek of latest assistant text, expandable detail sections via toggleExpand, AskUserQuestion clickable options in d-ask, image paste-to-attach in the nudge box, and a fully typed event ledger with per-kind emoji narration + per-session timeline drill-down) is ALREADY SHIPPED — confirmed in cc-core.lua (transcriptSnippet, narrateEvent/NARRATE map, sessionTimeline, gateDecisionSummary) and claude-dashboard.lua (d-pending/d-ask/d-activity, toggleExpand, pendingImage). So the part that transfers is already present, and the part that is new is structurally unhostable. There is a thin ADAPT seam (per-event-kind expandable cards + auto-scroll/jump in the 📜 Audit overlay), but it is a cosmetic upgrade to an existing surface, not this feature, and it does not match any queued L1-L4 item (those are agent profiles / policy bundles / templates / routing — not event rendering). Net: DONT_ADD. Re-skinning Shepherd's panel into a full scrolling conversation would duplicate the editor's own chat UI at lower fidelity for no steering benefit, which cuts against Shepherd's reason to exist (watch/steer/spawn, human-in-the-loop), not be a second transcript viewer.

### Resolver & Automation (1)
- **Background maintenance task processor (DB-backed queue, retries, stale-detection)** — Both prongs of the double mismatch fire. The feature's VALUE is running operational backend jobs (executor) as a persistent service over a multi-tenant DB (server). Shepherd is neither: no server, no DB, no background job execution beyond steering live Claude Code sessions, no stored secrets, no remote API calls — so the one-off sync scripts are categorically un-hostable. Crucially, the only abstractly-transferable pattern — a polling loop with bounded retries and a stale-WORKING->errored timeout sweep — is ALREADY shipped and unit-tested: the 1s refresh tick, core.isHung/trackProgress watchdog (escalation.hung), shouldAutoRespawn's 600s frozen threshold with a per-folder respawn budget (~ NUM_RETRIES), stepAutoContinue's loop-guarded per-folder retry budget, and routeTask/routePick with queueStarved. So it is redundant on the mechanism and out-of-scope on the substance. It does not match L1 (agent profiles), L2 (policy bundles), L3 (templates), or L4 (declarative routing) — none is a generic backend job runner — so no reinforces. Verdict DONT_ADD, high confidence.
